============================================================================================
CHAPTER 01: SURGICAL EMERGENCIES
============================================================================================

Chapter 01: Surgical Emergencies — Final (compact FRCEM study notes)

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Definition: Surgical emergencies = time‑critical conditions where rapid diagnosis + source control (surgery/debridement/airway) ± resuscitation determine outcome.
- Common entities: body‑packing/pack rupture (stimulant/opioid toxicity/obstruction), necrotising soft‑tissue infection (including Fournier’s), intra‑abdominal sepsis (perforation, ischaemia, appendicitis, cholecystitis), bowel obstruction/volvulus (sigmoid/caecal), Boerhaave’s (transmural oesophageal rupture), abscesses needing formal I&D, major lower GI bleed, gallstone ileus, pilonidal disease.
- Pathophysiology keys:
  - Toxin release from ruptured packages → rapid sympathetic storm (cocaine/amphetamines) or opioid depression.
  - Necrotising fasciitis → fulminant fascial spread with microvascular thrombosis → rapid tissue necrosis and sepsis.
  - Volvulus/strangulation → vascular compromise → ischemia → perforation/sepsis.
  - Boerhaave’s → full‑thickness oesophageal tear → mediastinitis, pleural contamination, sepsis.
- Management principle: ABCs first, then rapid surgical escalation for source control; imaging should not delay debridement or laparotomy when clinical suspicion is high.

ملخص بالعربية:
حالات الطوارئ الجراحية تحتاج تشخيصًا سريعًا وإزالة مصدر المرض (جراحة/تفريغ/تنفس). أمثلة شائعة: تهريب أو انفجار حزم مخدرة، التهاب لفافي نخرى، انفتال/انسداد أمعاء، تمزق المريء. الاستقرار الأولي ثم تحويل جراحى عاجل يغيّر النتيجة.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Red flags requiring immediate escalation:
  - Haemodynamic collapse, refractory shock, severe arrhythmia.
  - Rapidly spreading soft‑tissue pain ± crepitus, systemic toxicity → presume necrotising fasciitis.
  - Severe chest/epigastric pain after vomiting, subcutaneous emphysema, pleural effusion/pneumomediastinum → Boerhaave’s.
  - Sudden peritonitis, rigid abdomen, free air on imaging → perforation → urgent laparotomy.
  - Signs of packer rupture/toxicity: extreme agitation, diaphoresis, hypertension, tachycardia, hyperthermia (sympathomimetic) or respiratory depression, miosis (opioid).
  - Bowel obstruction with peritonitis, persistent vomiting, rising lactate/leukocytosis → strangulation.
  - Ongoing massive lower GI bleeding with instability → urgent localisation/IR/surgery.
- Key differentials (match sign → context):
  - Coffee‑bean sign (AXR) + elderly = sigmoid volvulus.
  - Inverted‑comma = caecal volvulus.
  - Psoas sign (pain on hip flexion against resistance) = retrocaecal appendicitis; obturator sign (pain on passive internal rotation of flexed hip) = pelvic appendicitis.
  - Rigler’s sign = pneumoperitoneum (free intraperitoneal air).

الإنعاش والعلامات الحمراء بالعربية:
أعراض تستدعى إحالة فورية: صدمة غير مستقرة، ألم منفلت وسريع مع علامة نخرية، ألم صدري بعد قيء مع هواء في المنصف (Boerhaave)، علامات ثقب أو انسداد مع ارتفاع اللاكتات. اربط العلامة السريرية بسياق المريض (coffee‑bean = انفتال القولون السيني، Rigler = هواء حر).

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate priorities: ABCs, monitor, IV access ×2, NBM if surgical abdomen suspected, analgesia, antiemetic, group & save if bleeding.
- Rapid investigations:
  - Erect chest X‑ray (free subdiaphragmatic air, pneumomediastinum).
  - Supine + erect abdominal X‑rays (obstruction patterns, coffee‑bean/inverted‑comma signs).
  - Bloods: FBC, U&Es, LFTs, amylase, lactate (ischaemia marker), coagulation, blood cultures if sepsis.
- CT use:
  - CT abdomen/pelvis with IV contrast = problem solver for undifferentiated acute abdomen (SBO transition point, volvulus whirl, pneumatosis, free air, collections).
  - CT thorax with oral water‑soluble contrast = best for suspected oesophageal leak/Boerhaave’s.
  - CT angiography = localise active lower GI bleeding; sensitivity detects rates ~0.3–0.5 ml/min (use if ongoing bleed and stable enough).
- Body‑packer testing (RCEM guidance):
  - Stable asymptomatic and consents → low‑dose non‑contrast CT abdomen/pelvis (high sensitivity) [RCEM 2020].
  - Stable symptomatic → contrast CT to characterise complications.
  - Unstable or clear toxicity/obstruction → urgent surgical removal/critical care [RCEM 2020].
- Adjuncts:
  - Water‑soluble contrast (Gastrografin) can be diagnostic and therapeutic in adhesive SBO.
  - Do not delay theatre for imaging when necrotising fasciitis or peritonitis is clinically suspected.

النهج التشخيصي بالعربية:
الأولوية ABC ثم صيام المريض ووضع خط وريدي. صور صدر واقفة وبطن مستلقي/واقفة أولاً؛ CT مع تباين وريدي هو الفاصل لمعظم الحالات الحادة. CT صدري مع تباين عن طريق الفم لتحديد تسرب المريء. لا تؤخر الجراحة إذا كان الاشتباه السريري قوياً.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- Initial resuscitation (single‑page algorithm):
  1. ABCs → secure airway if compromised; C‑line management: IV access ×2, monitor, ECG.
  2. Oxygen to maintain SpO2 ≥94% (or individualised).
  3. Give 500–1000 mL crystalloid bolus if hypotensive; reassess. Consider further boluses/vasopressors if persistent shock.
  4. Analgesia/antiemetic; NBM; urine output monitoring; senior surgical call early.
- Suspected necrotising fasciitis / Fournier’s:
  - Immediate broad‑spectrum IV antibiotics, aggressive fluid resuscitation, urgent extensive surgical debridement (do not delay for imaging), ICU level care, consider rapid CT if it will not delay theatre.
- Suspected Boerhaave’s:
  - NBM, high‑flow IV fluids, broad‑spectrum IV antibiotics covering oral flora and anaerobes (adjust per local protocol), analgesia, urgent CT thorax with oral water‑soluble contrast, early surgical/thoracic referral; drain large pleural collections.
- Small‑bowel obstruction (SBO):
  - If peritonitis/strangulation → urgent laparotomy.
  - If stable: NBM, IV fluids, NGT if vomiting, analgesia, CT abdomen/pelvis. Conservative trial up to 48–72 h if improving; use water‑soluble contrast study to predict resolution. Operate if no improvement or deterioration.
- Volvulus:
  - Sigmoid without peritonitis → endoscopic detorsion + decompression; elective resection later.
  - Caecal volvulus → early surgery (endoscopic detorsion often unsuccessful).
- Body‑packers/packers (summary algorithm per RCEM 2020):
  - Unstable or signs of toxicity/obstruction → urgent surgical/critical care input and removal [RCEM 2020].
  - Stable symptomatic → CT with IV contrast to assess complications [RCEM 2020].
  - Stable asymptomatic + consents → low‑dose non‑contrast CT and observation [RCEM 2020].
  - Stable asymptomatic + capacity but refuses imaging → document capacity/refusal and follow local forensic/legal pathway (RCEM 2020).
  - Consider whole‑bowel irrigation for retained packages per local protocol (avoid if obstruction suspected).
- Major lower GI bleed:
  - Resuscitate, transfuse as indicated, correct coagulopathy, consider urgent CT angiography if ongoing bleeding and stable; IR embolisation or surgery if localisation and patient suitable.

الخوارزميات الطارئة بالعربية:
أولاً: ABC ثم سوائل وريدية وملء مصدات وطلب جراحة مبكراً. التهاب اللفافة النخري يحتاج إزاحة جراحية فورية. تمزق المريء: صيام، مضادات حيوية واسعة، CT مع صبغة فموية، وإحالة صدرية. انسداد الأمعاء: جراحة عند التهاب بريتوني أو احتشاء؛ تجربة محافظة 48–72 ساعة إذا كان المستقر.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- Key drugs and considerations (verify local protocols/dosing):
  - Broad‑spectrum IV antibiotics for intra‑abdominal sepsis/Boerhaave’s/necrotising infection — choose hospital empiric regimens covering Gram‑negatives and anaerobes (e.g., piperacillin–tazobactam per local guidance) and adjust for renal function.
  - For suspected opioid toxicity from concealed packages: naloxone boluses and consider naloxone infusion if repeated doses needed (follow local toxicology guidance).
  - Vasopressors per ICU protocols for refractory septic/cardiogenic/shock states.
- Key procedures to mobilise:
  - Urgent surgical debridement for necrotising fasciitis/Fournier’s (formal theatre, not delayed bedside I&D).
  - Laparotomy/laparoscopy for perforation, ischaemia, failed conservative SBO, or pack removal when indicated.
  - Endoscopic detorsion for sigmoid volvulus (if no signs of ischaemia).
  - CT angiography ± IR embolisation for ongoing lower GI bleed.
  - Pleural drainage (chest tube) for large pleural collections secondary to oesophageal rupture.
  - Whole bowel irrigation (polyethylene glycol) for retained packages if indicated and no obstruction — follow local toxicology/forensic protocols.
  - Manual rectal pack removal only with senior surgical/forensic advice and appropriate analgesia/anaesthesia.
- Disposition and follow‑up:
  - ICU/HDU for unstable, post‑op, or toxic patients.
  - Surgical in‑patient admission for all confirmed necrotising infections, perforations, most SBOs unless fully resolved under observation.
  - Asymptomatic body‑packers: admit for observation per local/legal protocol; RCEM suggests observation and CT options [RCEM 2020]—document decisions and legal aspects if imaging refused.
  - Outpatient/clinic follow‑up after operative management as per surgical team; ensure wound care, antibiotics review, and social/forensic liaison where relevant.

الأدوية والإجراءات والمتابعة بالعربية:
استخدم مضادات حيوية وريدية واسعة الطيف وفق بروتوكول المستشفى. نفّذ إزالة جراحية عاجلة للالتهابات النخرية وتمزقات المريء والثغرات المعوية، وأنبوب صدري للانصباب الكبير. استخدم Naloxone في سمّيات الأفيونات وPEG للغسيل المعوي عند تعليمات التسمم. النزلاء المستقرون يتطلبون مراقبة جراحية؛ غير المستقرين إلى ICU.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Quick exam mnemonics:
  - Acute surgical abdomen workflow: ABC → NBM → IV fluids → analgesia/antiemetic → erect chest + AXR → CT if uncertain → surgical review.
  - SBO checklist: NBM, fluids, NGT if vomiting, CT ± Gastrografin, operate for peritonitis/strangulation or failed conservative trial (48–72 h).
- High‑yield signs to memorise:
  - Coffee‑bean sign = sigmoid volvulus (elderly, distension).
  - Inverted‑comma = caecal volvulus.
  - Whirl sign (CT) = mesenteric torsion/volvulus.
  - Rigler’s sign = both sides of bowel wall seen = pneumoperitoneum.
  - Psoas sign vs obturator sign: know which hip movement provokes pain and clinical context.
- Common pitfalls:
  - Don’t delay theatre for imaging in suspected necrotising fasciitis or peritonitis.
  - Plain AXR/CXR can miss small oesophageal leaks or small pneumoperitoneum → low threshold for CT if clinical suspicion.
  - Confuse packer vs stuffer: packers carry large, well‑wrapped loads and higher rupture risk; management/legal approach differs — follow RCEM/local guidance [RCEM 2020].
  - Do not perform blind rectal pack removal if ischaemia/perforation suspected — get surgical/forensic input.
  - Beware discharging a patient who refuses imaging without documenting capacity and following local custody/legal policy.
- Exam technique tips:
  - In MCQs, match age & classic sign (e.g., coffee‑bean + elderly = sigmoid volvulus).
  - Choose answers that prioritise resuscitation and surgical referral over investigations when instability or peritonitis present.

نصائح وعيوب شائعة بالعربية:
احفظ علامات الأشعة والمناورات السريرية (coffee‑bean، Rigler، psoas/obturator). لا تؤخر الجراحة عند الاشتباه القوي في إفراز نخرى أو ثقب؛ لا تكتفِ بالأشعة البسيطة إذا الشك السريري مرتفع. تمييز packer عن stuffer مهم قانونياً وطبياً (RCEM 2020).

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary
- الإجراءات العملية الفورية:
  - استقر: ABC، O2، خطين وريديين، صيام، سوائل؛ استدعِ الجراحة مبكراً.
  - علامات تضعك في المسار الجراحي: صدمة، التهاب بريتواني، ألم شديد متصاعد مع crepitus (نخر لفافي)، ألم صدر بعد قيء مع هواء في المنصف (Boerhaave).
  - تصوير مفيد سريعًا: صورة صدر واقفة + بطن واقفة/مستلقي؛ CT مع تباين وريدياً هو الفاصل لمعظم الحالات.
  - حزم المخدرات داخلية: مريض سمّي/غير مستقر → إزالة عاجلة؛ مريض سليم يوافق → CT منخفض الجرعة/مراقبة (RCEM 2020).
  - انسداد الأمعاء: صيام، سوائل، أنبوب أنفي، CT ± Gastrografin؛ جراحة عند علامات احتشاء/فشل.
- نقاط تحذيرية: لا تؤجل تفريغ نخرى؛ لا تكتفٍ بالـAXR عند الشك في تمزق المريء؛ وثّق رفض الفحوص واحضر استشارات قانونية/شرطية عند الحاجة.

ملخّص سريع بالعربية:
ابتدِ بالإسعاف ثم قيّم للعمليات الجراحية العاجلة. استخدم CT بسرعة عندما يكون التشخيص غير واضح أو المريض مستقر. اتبع إرشادات RCEM بشأن تهريب المخدرات (2020) ودوّن كل قرار عند رفض الفحوص.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Body‑packing/internal drug concealment: recognition, toxicity patterns, imaging choices, RCEM algorithm for packers/stuffers, legal/consent issues [RCEM 2020].
- Necrotising soft‑tissue infection / Fournier’s: recognition, immediate debridement, antibiotics, ICU needs.
- Boerhaave’s (oesophageal rupture): presentation, imaging (CT with oral contrast), antibiotics, drainage, surgical referral.
- Bowel obstruction / SBO: adhesive causes, initial conservative management, CT features, Gastrografin use, indications for surgery.
- Volvulus (sigmoid vs caecal): AXR signs (coffee‑bean, inverted comma), endoscopic detorsion vs surgery.
- Perforation / pneumoperitoneum: Rigler’s sign, urgency of laparotomy.
- Appendicitis/cholecystitis: focal signs (Rovsing, psoas, obturator, Murphy), imaging, early surgical referral.
- Abscess requiring I&D vs bedside drainage: indications for formal theatre vs simple drainage.
- Major lower GI bleed: resuscitation, CT angiography thresholds and IR/surgical options.
- Gallstone ileus, pilonidal disease: brief recognition and management pathways.
- Imaging signs and bedside clinical signs: coffee‑bean, inverted‑comma, whirl sign, Rigler, psoas/obturator, Rovsing.
- Practical resuscitation priorities and disposition (ED → surgical ward/OR/ICU).
(Note: question numbers not provided; map lists all concepts represented in source notes.)

Sources to verify
- RCEM: Management of Suspected Internal Drug Traffickers (December 2020) [RCEM 2020]. Also consult local hospital surgical and toxicology protocols, ERC guidance, NICE acute abdominal pain and sepsis guidance, and national critical care/IR guidance for local doses and pathways.

This material is educational revision only — not patient‑specific advice. Verify local protocols and senior surgical/toxicology input for clinical decisions.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 02: PAIN AND SEDATION
============================================================================================

Chapter 02 — Pain and Sedation

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core: ED pain care uses a stepped approach — simple analgesics → systemic opioids/inhaled analgesia → local/regional blocks → procedural sedation when immobility or deep analgesia required. Choose modality by pain severity, site, comorbidity, airway risk and need for immobility. [RCEM]
- Procedural sedation: aims to provide anxiolysis, analgesia and immobility while preserving cardiorespiratory stability. Drugs act on CNS respiratory drive and airway reflexes — risk of hypoventilation/apnoea, aspiration and haemodynamic effects.
- Regional/field blocks: deposit local anaesthetic near nerves or in fascial planes to produce reversible nerve conduction block; reduce systemic opioid needs but carry risks (intravascular injection, nerve injury, LAST, masking compartment syndrome).
Arabic: نظرة عامة — نهج طبقي لتسكين الألم؛ التخدير الإجرائي يؤثر على الجهاز التنفسي والانعكاسات الهوائية؛ الكتل العصبية تمنع التوصيل العصبي وتقلل الحاجة للأفيونات لكنها تحمل مخاطر خاصة.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Immediate sedation risks: hypoventilation/apnoea, hypoxia, airway obstruction, aspiration, laryngospasm, haemodynamic instability, emergence agitation (ketamine), vomiting. Monitor and act early.
- Block-specific complications: LAST, local infection/haematoma, nerve injury, failed block, masking evolving compartment syndrome. Check anticoagulation status (not always absolute contraindication for fascial‑plane blocks — follow local guidance).
- High‑risk patient factors: severe cardiorespiratory disease, pregnancy, hepatic/renal impairment, recent oral intake, extreme age, allergies, anticoagulation, poor cooperation.
- Red flags requiring senior review/avoidance of ED sedation: ASA IV or unstable physiology for non‑life‑saving procedures; uncontrolled airway risk; suspected full stomach with urgent need for deep sedation without airway protection.
Arabic: التعرف وعلامات الخطر — راقب نقص التهوية، نقص الأكسجة، ولـLAST؛ اعتبر الأمراض المصاحبة والتخثر؛ ASA IV عادة يمنع التخدير الإجرائي الانتخابي بالقسم.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Pre‑procedure checks (mandatory): focused airway assessment, fasting history, current meds and allergies, baseline vitals, ASA classification, consent and plan for rescue/airway management. Document neurovascular baseline for limb procedures.
- Monitoring essentials: continuous pulse oximetry and capnography (if available), ECG and BP at least every 5 minutes during sedation. Post‑block neurovascular checks and observation for systemic toxicity.
- Regional block planning: confirm anatomy/landmarks, consider ultrasound guidance to improve accuracy and reduce vascular puncture, check coagulation status and allergies.
- Interpretation pitfalls: do not assume return to SpO2 92% RA is safe for discharge — many ED protocols require return to baseline oxygen saturation and alertness. Verify local discharge criteria.
Arabic: النهج التشخيصي — فحوصات قبيل الإجراء: طريق هوائي، صوم، أدوية، ASA، موافقة؛ مراقبة مستمرة بالأكسيمتر والـcapnography؛ استخدم الموجات فوق الصوتية عند الإمكان.

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. Procedural sedation — concise algorithm
1) Prepare: consent, NPO check, IV access, monitoring (SpO2, capnography, ECG, BP), resuscitation kit, reversal agents (naloxone, flumazenil) available, defined team roles (operator, dedicated monitor, assistant). "Crisis plan" audible. [AAGBI; RCEM]
2) Induction (commonly tested adult doses): ketamine 1.0 mg/kg IV bolus over 30–60 s; titrate small aliquots for effect; reduce dose in frail/elderly. (Use local protocol for alternatives/opiates/benzos.)
3) Monitor continuously; reassess sedation depth, ventilation and circulation.
4) Manage airway/respiratory compromise: immediate airway manoeuvres → supplemental O2 → jaw thrust/adjuncts → bag‑mask ventilation → escalate to advanced airway if refractory. Treat causes (opioid/benzo reversal vs supportive care for ketamine).
5) Emergence agitation (ketamine): calm environment, reassurance; consider midazolam 0.03 mg/kg IV for severe agitation. [RCEM]
6) Recovery/discharge: patient alert and oriented at baseline, baseline vitals and SpO2 on room air (many ED protocols require return to baseline; SpO2 92% RA often considered unsafe for discharge), tolerating oral fluids, minimal nausea, and elapsed observation period (e.g., 2 hours after reversal agents per local protocol). Document discharge criteria met. [RCEM procedural sedation BPC 2022]
Arabic: خوارزمية التخدير الإجرائي — التحضير (أدوار، معدات، أدوية عكسية)، كيتامين 1 mg/kg اختبار شائع، مراقبة مستمرة، تدبير العجز التنفسي حسب الأولويات، ميدازولام 0.03 mg/kg للتهيج؛ لا تخرج المريض إلا بعد عودة القيم الأساسية.

B. Regional/field block — concise algorithm (example: fascia iliaca)
1) Indication/contraindication check: allergy, local infection (contra), coagulation status, patient cooperation. Anticoagulation may be relative — consult policy. [RCEM]
2) Final safety step: "Stop Before You Block" timeout — confirm patient, side, block, consent, equipment, resuscitation readiness.
3) Technique (landmark FIB): identify inguinal ligament; divide into thirds; point 1 cm below junction of lateral/middle third; needle passes fascia lata (first pop) then fascia iliaca (second pop); deposit LA below fascia iliaca to bathe femoral/ilioinguinal plexus. Use ultrasound when possible.
4) Dosing/monitoring: observe for LAST; monitor every 5 minutes for first 15 minutes and at 30 minutes; document neurovascular status. Typical FIB requires larger volume (often 30–40 ml) compared with femoral nerve block. Verify local maximum LA doses.
5) LAST immediate actions: stop injection, call for help, airway/O2, control seizures with benzodiazepines, treat arrhythmia per ACLS avoiding agents exacerbating toxicity, 20% lipid emulsion: bolus 1.5 ml/kg IV then infusion 0.25 ml/kg/min (follow ASRA/local protocol). [ASRA]
Arabic: خوارزمية الكتلة العصبية — تحقق من الموانع، نفّذ "Stop Before You Block"، استخدم العلامات أو الموجات فوق الصوتية، راقب لاكتشاف LAST؛ مستحلب دهني 20% حسب إرشادات ASRA في حالة سمية جهازية.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
Drugs — exam‑important doses/limits (verify local formularies)
- Ketamine (procedural sedation adult): 1.0 mg/kg IV bolus commonly tested; titrate and reduce for frailty. [RCEM]
- Midazolam (for ketamine emergence agitation): 0.03 mg/kg IV commonly tested. [RCEM]
- Methoxyflurane (Penthrox): not licensed in many jurisdictions for <18 yrs; common limits cited in BNF: max 6 ml/24 h (2 × 3 ml vials) and 15 ml/week (5 vials). Patient exhales into carbon chamber to reduce staff exposure. [BNF]
- Local anaesthetics: lidocaine ≈ 4.5 mg/kg (without adrenaline) commonly quoted; with adrenaline ≈ 7 mg/kg. Bupivacaine/levobupivacaine commonly cited ≈ 2–2.5 mg/kg (sources vary) — verify local protocols. Avoid exceeding institutional maxima.  
- LAST treatment: 20% lipid emulsion bolus 1.5 ml/kg then infusion 0.25 ml/kg/min (ASRA guidance). [ASRA]
Procedures — practical tips
- Wrist block: block median, ulnar, superficial radial nerves; landmarks — median between palmaris longus and FCR; ulnar between FCU and ulnar artery; superficial radial dorsolateral. Use ~3–5 ml per nerve (exam values approximate).
- Ankle block: five nerves — deep peroneal, superficial peroneal, posterior tibial, sural, saphenous. Remember common peroneal divides proximal to ankle.
- Fascia iliaca vs femoral block: FIB is further from neurovascular bundle (lower intravascular risk), needs larger volume and may give variable coverage; FNB uses lower volume but is closer to vessels.
Disposition and follow‑up
- Observation: immediate post‑procedure monitoring until return to baseline; specific timing per local policy (e.g., every 5 min initially for sedation; first 15 min then 30 min post‑block checks).
- Discharge criteria: alertness at baseline, hemodynamic stability, SpO2 at/near baseline on room air (do not rely on an absolute 92% RA as safe), able to tolerate oral fluids, controlled pain, minimal nausea/vomiting, escort or safe transport if required. Give written advice about block numbness and signs of infection/haematoma/LAST and when/how to seek help.
- Follow‑up: analgesia plan, wound care review, review of anticoagulation management if modified, outpatient ortho/anaesthesia review for ongoing block or complications as needed.
Arabic: أدوية وإجراءات والمتابعة — جرعات شائعة للاختبار: كيتامين 1 mg/kg؛ ميدازولام 0.03 mg/kg؛ Penthr ox حدود BNF; ليدوكايين ≈4.5 mg/kg؛ بوبيفاكين ≈2–2.5 mg/kg (تحقق محلياً). راقب المريض حتى عودة القيم الأساسية وقدّم تعليمات واضحة عن المضاعفات والمتابعة.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Ketamine dosing: 1.0 mg/kg IV is the usual exam answer for adult procedural sedation; midazolam 0.03 mg/kg for emergence agitation. [RCEM]
- Don’t accept “SpO2 92% RA is safe for discharge” as a blanket rule — many ED protocols require return to baseline; 92% RA is often flagged as unsafe in exam stems.
- Methoxyflurane (Penthrox): memorise 6 ml/24 h and 15 ml/week limits as exam fact (BNF) and remember age restriction (<18 yrs in many jurisdictions). [BNF]
- LAST management: lipid emulsion bolus 1.5 ml/kg → infusion 0.25 ml/kg/min is the tested sequence (cite ASRA). Know basic ACLS modifications (avoid vasopressin, high doses of local‑anaesthetic‑sensitising agents). [ASRA]
- Anticoagulation and nerve blocks: exam traps often present anticoagulation as absolute contraindication — for many fascial plane blocks this is not absolute; answer should reflect block‑specific and policy‑dependent considerations. [RCEM]
- Local anaesthetic dose variability: questions may offer bupivacaine limits of 2 mg/kg vs 2.5 mg/kg — flag that sources differ and select the option consistent with local/formulary guidance if in clinical context; if an exam requires a single best answer, choose the commonly cited 2 mg/kg only if that matches offered options.
- Entonox pitfalls: contraindicated in suspected pneumothorax, bowel obstruction, recent middle ear surgery, or when gas expansion is hazardous — common exam distractors.
- Opioid conversion: codeine ≈ 10:1 oral morphine (exam shorthand) — useful for calculations but clinically unreliable due to CYP2D6 variability (and paediatric codeine restrictions). [MHRA]
Arabic: حيل الامتحان — كيتامين 1 mg/kg وميادازولام 0.03 mg/kg شائعان؛ لا تقبل 92% كحد آمن للخروج دون سياق؛ Penthr ox 6 ml/24h و15 ml/week؛ LAST: دهون 20% حسب ASRA؛ موانع Entonox شائعة في الامتحانات؛ تحويل الكوديين 10:1 قاعدة اختبارية وليست مطلقة.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (concise)
- استخدم نهجاً طبقيّاً لتسكين الألم: مسكنات بسيطة → Entonox → كتلة عصبية → تخدير إجرائي.  
- تحقّق قبل الإجراء: طريق هوائي، صوم، أدوية، حساسية، ASA، معدات إنعاش، أدوار الفريق.  
- كيتامين 1 mg/kg IV (إجابة امتحان شائعة)؛ ميدازولام 0.03 mg/kg لعلاج اضطراب الخروج.  
- Penthr ox (methoxyflurane): حد 6 ml/24h و15 ml/week؛ حذارٍ في <18 سنة حسب الترخيص المحلي. [BNF]  
- حدود ليدوكايين ≈4.5 mg/kg؛ حدود البوبيفاكاين ≈2–2.5 mg/kg — تحقّق من بروتوكولك المحلي.  
- LAST → أوقف الحقن، تنفس/أكسجين، بنزوديازيبين للتشنجات، مستحلب دهني 20%: دفعة 1.5 ml/kg ثم تسريب 0.25 ml/kg/min. [ASRA]  
- لا تخرج المريض حتى يعود لمستواه الإدراكي والفيزيولوجي الأساسي وتحقق معايير المراقبة المحلية.  
Arabic short take‑home: اتبع فحوصات ما قبل التخدير، استخدم "Stop Before You Block"، راقب عن كثب، وتحقق من البروتوكول المحلي للجرعات والموانع.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered in supplied stems)
- RCEM procedural sedation guidance: pre‑sedation checks, monitoring frequency, ketamine dosing, management of hypoventilation/airway, emergence agitation, discharge criteria (procedural sedation concepts). [RCEM procedural sedation BPC 2018–2022]
- AAGBI / Anaesthesia guidance: team roles, airway/rescue preparation, capnography use (sedation safety concepts). [AAGBI]
- BNF / MHRA stems: methoxyflurane (Penthrox) licensing, age limits, daily/weekly dose limits; codeine regulatory cautions. [BNF; MHRA]
- Regional block technique and safety: fascia iliaca block landmarks/technique, wrist block/anatomical landmarks, ankle block five‑nerve list, comparative notes FIB vs FNB, anticoagulation considerations. (Field block concepts)
- Local anaesthetic pharmacology and dosing caveats: lidocaine, bupivacaine/levobupivacaine maximums and volume considerations (LA dose/last concepts).
- LAST recognition and management: immediate actions, benzodiazepine seizure control, lipid emulsion regimen (ASRA guidance).
- Entonox / inhaled analgesia: contraindications (pneumothorax, bowel obstruction, raised ICP), paediatric considerations (age/policy variations).
- Opioid conversion and prescribing pitfalls: codeine→morphine approximate conversion, CYP2D6 caveat, paediatric codeine restrictions.

Sources to verify: RCEM (procedural sedation and pain guidance), AAGBI, ASRA (LAST guidance), BNF (methoxyflurane), MHRA (codeine in children), local anaesthesia formularies and institutional sedation policies, ERC/NICE where relevant.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 03: PAEDIATRICS
============================================================================================

Chapter 03: Paediatrics — Final concise FRCEM revision chapter

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Principles
  - Children = age‑dependent physiology: small children decompensate fast (higher metabolic rate, larger head, smaller airway, higher fluid requirement). Use weight‑based dosing and rapid estimation: WETFLAG / weight ≈ (2 × age) + 8 kg for quick calculations. [RCUK Mar 2022]
  - ABC first: airway/oxygenation → circulation (perfusion, HR thresholds) → disability (GCS/seizures) → exposure/investigations.
  - Prioritise time‑critical diagnoses: neonatal/early‑onset sepsis, airway foreign body, septic arthritis, intussusception, status epilepticus, severe asthma, DKA with cerebral oedema, cardiac arrest.
- Pathophysiology highlights
  - Shock in infants may be masked (compensated shock): tachycardia, delayed capillary refill, normal BP until late.
  - Respiratory distress: distinguish upper (stridor, drooling, epiglottitis/bacterial tracheitis) from lower (wheeze, bronchiolitis, asthma) airway disease.
  - Cyanotic congenital lesions (TOF) → hypercyanotic (tet) spells from increased R→L shunt; knee‑chest increases systemic vascular resistance and reduces R→L shunt.
  - DKA cerebral oedema: osmolar/flow shifts during rehydration → rising intracranial pressure; early recognition critical. [BSPED 2020]
  - IO samples are useful for urgent access but may be unreliable for some analytes (calcium, some electrolytes, WCC/platelets). [LitFL]

Arabic (brief)
- الأطفال لهم فيزيولوجيا خاصة: تدهور أسرع وجرعات على أساس الوزن (WETFLAG ≈ 2×العمر+8). الأولوية للجوْف والتنفس والدورة. تمييز انسداد فوقي/سفلي مهم. عينات العظم قد تخطئ لبعض التحاليل.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Key presentations and discriminators
  - Limping child: transient synovitis vs septic arthritis vs Perthes vs fracture. Red flags for septic arthritis: non‑weightbearing, fever, high CRP/WBC/ESR → urgent ortho aspiration (Kocher criteria helpful; CRP most predictive). [MDCalc Kocher]
  - Neonate with collapse/poor feeding: consider early‑onset sepsis, congenital heart disease (duct‑dependent lesions), metabolic disorder.
  - High fever ≥5 days + conjunctivitis + mucosal changes + rash → Kawasaki disease (risk coronary aneurysm).
  - Palpable purpura on buttocks/legs ± abdominal pain → HSP (monitor renal function).
  - Sudden cyanosis/crying + hyperpnoea → tet spell (TOF).
  - Sudden choking with unilateral decreased air entry → airway foreign body; immediate theatre if compromised.
  - Paroxysmal very fast regular narrow‑complex tachycardia → SVT.
  - Barking cough, inspiratory stridor ± fever: croup (mild = no stridor at rest).
  - Fever + oral vesicles + palm/sole rash → hand‑foot‑mouth (supportive).
  - Acute severe asthma: severe tachypnoea, use of accessory muscles, poor air entry, hypoxia — escalate early.
  - Status epilepticus with likely hyponatraemia → treat hyponatraemia to stop seizure.
- Red flags (must admit/escalate)
  - Any infant <28 days with fever; any child with poor perfusion, persistent hypoxia, respiratory exhaustion, ongoing seizure after two benzodiazepine doses, suspected septic arthritis, lodged oesophageal button battery, signs of DKA cerebral oedema.

Arabic (brief)
- ملاحظات تحذيرية: الأطفال الصغار عرضة لعدوى خطيرة؛ الطفل الذي لا يضع وزنًا على الطرف أو الحمى المرتفعة أو CRP/WBC المرتفع يحتاج شكوكا في التهاب مفصلي خاثر. نباح الكحة = خناق؛ الاختناق المفاجئ قد يكون جسمًا غريبًا. أي رضيع <28 يوماً مع حمى يحتاج تقييم شامل.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Principles: bedside glucose in any post‑ictal/altered child; bloods (FBC, CRP) early in limp/fever; targeted imaging only when it affects immediate management.
- Specific pathways
  - Limping hip: exam → weightbearing status. If non‑weightbearing or fever → FBC + CRP ± ESR → pelvic AP + frog‑leg XR; urgent hip US for effusion; if effusion/high probability → ortho referral + aspiration. (Kocher criteria / CRP best predictor). [MDCalc Kocher]
  - Suspected foreign body aspiration: CXR for air‑trapping or lobar collapse (AP + lateral) but do NOT delay theatre if unstable — prepare rigid bronchoscopy.
  - Intussusception: abdominal ultrasound (target sign) is first‑line; AXR insensitive (reserve XR for perforation signs or if US delayed).
  - Febrile infant 1–3 months: low threshold for LP and full sepsis work‑up if labs abnormal; send blood culture and CRP; if <28 days, treat empirically and consider LP before antibiotics if stable. [NICE NG143 / NG195]
  - DKA: VBG/ABG, glucose, ketones, U&E, ECG; classify severity by pH/HCO3 to estimate fluid deficit (mild/moderate/severe guidance per BSPED). [BSPED 2020]
  - Seizure workup: glucose first, then VBG/biochemistry, infection screen if indicated; urgent neuroimaging only if focal signs or trauma.
  - IO samples: accept for urgent chemistry but confirm discrepant electrolytes/calcium with peripheral sample when possible. [LitFL]

Arabic (brief)
- نهج تشخيصي عملي: قياس جلوكوز فورًا بعد النوبة؛ لعلاج الورك أعد FBC+CRP وصور وUS؛ الشُبهة لابتلاع جسم غريب تتطلب صورًا لكن لا تؤخر المناظير إذا الطفل غير مستقر. رضيع محمى <28 يوماً غالبًا يحتاج علاج ومراجعة شاملة.

4. Emergency management algorithms
(Select concise stepwise algorithms — apply local protocol where flagged)
- Limping hip (suspected septic arthritis)
  1. ABCs, analgesia.  
  2. FBC + CRP ± ESR, pelvis XR, urgent ultrasound.  
  3. If effusion/high probability → urgent orthopaedic aspiration + start IV antibiotics. [MDCalc Kocher]
- Hypercyanotic (tet) spell
  1. Knee‑chest position; calm child; high‑flow O2.  
  2. IV/IO fluids if shocked.  
  3. Give morphine (or ketamine per local congenital cardiac protocol) and urgent cardiology/surgical review.
- Airway foreign body (compromised)
  - Immediate preparation for rigid bronchoscopy under GA; do not delay for imaging. If conscious with effective cough → encourage coughing. For infants: back blows then chest thrusts if ineffective; if unresponsive → CPR and theatre. [Resus guidance]
- Croup
  - Single dose dexamethasone (commonly 0.15 mg/kg PO) ± nebulised adrenaline for moderate/severe disease; observe for rebound. [RCEM/NG91]
- Severe acute asthma (escalation)
  1. Oxygen (target per local guidance), inhaled/nebulised salbutamol ± ipratropium, systemic steroid early.  
  2. If inadequate: IV salbutamol bolus 15 µg/kg over 5–10 min then infusion per local protocol (verify local practice) and/or aminophylline 5 mg/kg IV loading (follow local protocol). Consider IV magnesium sulphate 40 mg/kg (max 2 g) single dose for refractory severe attack. Escalate to PICU. [BNFC/NICE]
- Status epilepticus
  1. ABC, oxygen, capillary glucose.  
  2. Give benzodiazepine (weight‑based; e.g., lorazepam 0.1 mg/kg IV or buccal midazolam 0.3–0.5 mg/kg). Repeat once only.  
  3. If ongoing → second‑line AED (levetiracetam ≈30–40 mg/kg IV over 5 min or phenytoin 20 mg/kg IV slow with ECG).  
  4. If refractory after second‑line → secure airway, RSI and anaesthetic transfer to PICU. [Resus UK Mar 2022]
- Hyponatraemic seizure
  - Give 3% sodium chloride bolus (commonly 2–3 mL/kg is used; some local protocols use 3 mL/kg) to terminate seizure while arranging definitive care — verify your local PALS/Resus guidance. [RCUK Mar 2022; flagged]
- SVT (unstable vs stable)
  - If unstable → immediate synchronized cardioversion 1 J/kg (repeat 2 J/kg, escalate up to 4 J/kg).  
  - If stable → vagal manoeuvres (infants: ice to face; older: modified Valsalva) then adenosine per paediatric dosing (see Section 5). [RCUK Mar 2022]
- Cardiac arrest (shockable paediatric)
  - Defibrillation 4 J/kg initial shock (Resus UK/RCUK guidance). [RCUK Mar 2022]
- DKA with suspected cerebral oedema
  - Recognise early (falling GCS, headache, vomiting, Cushing signs). Immediate hyperosmolar therapy per local/PICU guideline — BSPED commonly recommends 3% hypertonic saline bolus (e.g., 5 mL/kg) or mannitol 0.5–1 g/kg if HTS unavailable — verify local protocol. Urgent PICU. [BSPED 2020 — FLAG]

Arabic (brief)
- خوارزميات سريعة: التهاب مفصل خاثر → US+شفط+مضاد حيوي؛ نوبة "tet" → وضع ركبة‑صدر، أكسجين، مهدئ وسوائل؛ جسم غريب مُعيق → منظار صلب فوري إذا يعاني الطفل؛ خناق → ديكساميثازون ± أدرينالين رذاذي؛ صرع مستمر → بنزوديازيبين ×2 → ليفيتيراسيتام/فينيتوين → RSI إذا مقاوم؛ جرعات HTS وادينوزين تحقق محليًا.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- High‑yield drug doses / procedural thresholds (well established or commonly used; verify local policies)
  - Weight estimation: WETFLAG ≈ (2 × age) + 8 kg.
  - Dexamethasone for croup: single dose commonly 0.15 mg/kg PO (range cited 0.15–0.6 mg/kg in different sources — verify local exam expectation). [RCEM]
  - Nebulised adrenaline for croup: dose per local guideline (observe for rebound).
  - Severe asthma: IV salbutamol loading 15 µg/kg over 5–10 min then infusion (local protocol); aminophylline loading 5 mg/kg IV if used. IV magnesium sulphate 40 mg/kg (max 2 g) single dose for refractory severe acute asthma. [BNFC/NICE]
  - Status epilepticus: buccal midazolam 0.3–0.5 mg/kg or lorazepam 0.1 mg/kg IV; levetiracetam commonly 30–40 mg/kg IV (max 3 g); phenytoin 20 mg/kg IV slow with ECG. [Resus UK Mar 2022]
  - Bradycardia: atropine 20 µg/kg (0.02 mg/kg) for vagal bradycardia; adrenaline for poor perfusion bradycardia 10 µg/kg (0.01 mg/kg) IV. [RCUK]
  - SVT: adenosine dosing per Resus UK paediatric algorithm — 1–11 years initial 100 µg/kg rapid IV bolus (increase incrementally), 12–17 years may start 3 mg; infants <1 year have different dosing (e.g., 150 µg/kg) — confirm local guideline. [Resus UK Mar 2022]
  - Defibrillation in paediatric arrest: 4 J/kg initial (RCUK). [RCUK Mar 2022]
  - Hyponatraemic seizure: 3% NaCl bolus commonly 2–3 mL/kg (some use 3 mL/kg) — verify local PALS. [RCUK Mar 2022 — flagged]
  - DKA cerebral oedema: BSPED commonly reports 3% HTS 5 mL/kg bolus (verify local policy). [BSPED 2020 — FLAG]
  - Neonatal empiric antibiotics for suspected early‑onset sepsis: benzylpenicillin + gentamicin (local neonatal guidance for age‑specific cephalosporin cautions). [NICE NG195]
  - Analgesia: intranasal diamorphine (where available/authorized) ~0.1 mg/kg via atomiser for severe pain if IV not available — check local licencing. Avoid NSAIDs in significant renal impairment. (Regulatory variance flagged.)
  - IVIG in ITP: regimes vary (e.g., 0.8–1 g/kg single dose or 0.4 g/kg ×2) — consult haematology/local protocol.
  - Maintenance IV fluids: Holliday‑Segar: 0–10 kg 4 mL/kg/hr; 10–20 kg 2 mL/kg/hr; >20 kg add 1 mL/kg/hr per kg above 20. [NICE NG29]
- Procedures / disposition
  - Urgent orthopaedic theatre for septic hip aspiration/drainage if septic arthritis suspected. Admit and liaise with ortho.
  - Rigid bronchoscopy in theatre for airway foreign body under GA.
  - PICU for: refractory status epilepticus, intubated severe asthma, DKA severe or suspected cerebral oedema, neonatal sepsis unstable, post‑resuscitation care.
  - Admit and arrange follow‑up: asthma discharge review by GP within 48 h; specialist review ~1 month for moderate‑severe attacks. For AOM use watchful waiting unless indicated for antibiotics (otorrhoea, bilateral <2 yrs, very unwell) [NICE NG91].
  - Safeguarding: unexplained injuries, inconsistent history, patterned bruises — follow local child protection procedures immediately.
- Follow‑up investigations
  - Septic arthritis: synovial fluid culture, blood cultures, CRP trend to guide therapy duration.  
  - HSP: monitor urine for haematuria/protein (baseline and follow‑up).  
  - UTI (young child): renal ultrasound and specialist referral for atypical UTIs (per local pathways). [NICE CKS]

Arabic (brief)
- أدوية وإجراءات: WETFLAG للوزن؛ ديكساميثازون خناق ≈0.15 mg/kg؛ ربو حاد: سالبيوتامول وريدياً/أمينوفيلين/ماغنسيوم 40 mg/kg؛ صرع مستمر: بنزو ×2 ثم ليفيتيراسيتام 30–40 mg/kg أو فينيتوين 20 mg/kg؛ جرعات HTS وPGE1 وجرعة ديامورفين أنفي تحتاج تحقق محلي. إحالة عاجلة إلى ortho/ENT/PICU حسب الحالة. متابعة الربو خلال 48 ساعة مع المريض.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Kocher criteria recall (original 4): non‑weightbearing, fever >38.5°C, WBC >12 ×10^9/L, ESR >40 mm/hr — presence of all four → high probability septic arthritis; modern practice: CRP is more predictive than ESR. (Exam trap: CRP was not in original Kocher score.) [MDCalc Kocher]
- Imaging pitfalls
  - Normal plain abdominal XR does NOT exclude intussusception — ultrasound is the diagnostic test of choice.  
  - AP chest/abdomen alone may misidentify stacked magnets as single — always get lateral view for suspected magnet ingestion (exam favourite). [RCEM magnet guidance 2021]
- Drug/management pitfalls
  - Do NOT give insulin bolus in DKA; use 0.1 U/kg/hr infusion after initial resuscitation (BSPED). Exam trap: some stems try to lure you into giving bolus. [BSPED 2020]
  - After two adequate benzodiazepine doses for an ongoing seizure, move promptly to second‑line AEDs; avoid further large benzodiazepine boluses without airway protection. (Common error in exam stems.)
  - Phenytoin infusion must be slow with ECG monitoring (cardiotoxic if rapid) — prefer fosphenytoin where available.
  - In AOM do not reflexively give antibiotics — memorize exceptions (otalgia + otorrhoea, <2 yrs bilateral, systemically unwell). [NICE NG91]
  - Foreign body airway: if child can cough effectively, do not attempt blind finger sweep or immediate intubation — encourage cough and call for theatre if ineffective.
  - IO blood values: potassium/calcium/WCC/platelets may be unreliable — confirm with venous sample before critical decisions.
- Practical exam mnemonics
  - Weight quick calc: WETFLAG = (2 × age) + 8 kg.  
  - ETT size (uncuffed) exam shortcut: (age/4) + 4 mm (subtract 0.5 mm for cuffed tube) — commonly tested.  
  - DKA severity for exam: mild pH 7.20–7.29, moderate 7.10–7.19, severe <7.10 (use to estimate fluid deficit per BSPED). [BSPED 2020]
- Frequent flagged items to double‑check before answering in exam or acting clinically
  - Exact bolus dose of 3% NaCl for hyponatraemic seizure (2 vs 3 mL/kg) — local PALS/Resus guidance varies. [RCUK Mar 2022 — flagged]  
  - Ketamine absolute contraindication under 12 months is debated — check local procedural sedation guidance (RCEM Feb 2020 flagged).  
  - Neonatal cephalosporin caution (ceftriaxone in neonates with hyperbilirubinaemia) — check local neonatal guidance.

Arabic (brief)
- حيل الامتحان: Kocher الأصلي = 4 معايير (CRP ليس جزءًا منها لكن مفيد عمليًا). صورة بطنية طبيعية لا تنفي الانغلاف — استخدم الموجات. لا تعطَ جرعة إقلاع للإنسولين في DKA. بعد بنزو ×2 انتقل للأدوية الثانية. تحقق من جرعات HTS وكيتامين محليًا قبل اختيار الإجابة.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (Modern Standard Arabic)
- مبادئ عامة: الأطفال = فيزيولوجيا خاصة؛ استعمل قواعد سريعة للوزن (WETFLAG = 2×العمر+8). ابدأ بـ ABCs.  
- أهم الحالات الطارئة: رضيع محمى <28 يوماً، انسداد مجرى الهواء، التهاب مفصلي خاثر، انتفاخ أحمر (انغلاف معوي)، نوبة صرع مستمرة، ربو حاد، DKA مع وذمة دماغية.  
- فحوص سريعة مفيدة: جلوكوز شعري فوراً بعد النوبة؛ FBC + CRP لتفريق الالتهاب النصفي عن التهاب المفصل؛ US للانغلاف؛ أشعة أمامية وجانبية للبطاريات/المغناطيس.  
- علاجات مهمة: ديكساميثازون خناق ≈0.15 mg/kg؛ ماغنسيوم ربو حاد 40 mg/kg (حد أقصى 2 g)؛ ليفيتيراسيتام في الصرع ≈40 mg/kg؛ فينيتوين 20 mg/kg (ببطء مع ECG).  
- ملاحظات سلامة: لا تعطِ جرعة إقلاع بالإنسولين في DKA؛ لا تفحص حلق المريض عند الشك في التهاب الحنجرة؛ أغلق على إزالة زر البطارية في المريء فوراً؛ تحقق محلياً من جرعات HTS وPGE1 وكيتامين وديامورفين الأنفي.  
(مادة للمذاكرة فقط — راجع الأدلة والممارسات المحلية قبل الاستخدام السريري.)

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Limping hip / septic arthritis vs transient synovitis (Kocher criteria; CRP)  
- Neonatal sepsis / early‑onset sepsis / PGE1 considerations  
- Kawasaki disease recognition and coronary risk  
- Henoch‑Schönlein purpura (HSP) recognition and renal follow‑up  
- Tetralogy of Fallot — hypercyanotic (tet) spell management  
- Foreign body airway obstruction (choking algorithms, rigid bronchoscopy) and button battery/magnet ingestion imaging rules  
- SVT recognition and management (adenosine dosing, synchronized cardioversion)  
- Croup (steroid + nebulised adrenaline)  
- Acute severe asthma (nebulisers → IV salbutamol/aminophylline/magnesium → PICU escalation)  
- Intussusception diagnosis (ultrasound) and reduction pathways  
- Septic arthritis hip algorithm and orthopaedic aspiration  
- Status epilepticus (benzodiazepines → second‑line AEDs → RSI if refractory) and hyponatraemic seizure HTS bolus considerations  
- DKA management and cerebral oedema recognition/management (BSPED fluid classification)  
- Bradycardia algorithms (atropine, adrenaline thresholds)  
- Paediatric resuscitation energy doses (defibrillation/cardioversion)  
- Neonatal/paediatric analgesia and sedation issues (ketamine guidance, intranasal opioids)  
- UTI in young child: urine testing, atypical features and imaging pathways  
- ITP management (IVIG vs steroids) and HUS recognition  
- IO sampling limitations and interpretation pitfalls  
- Safeguarding: patterns prompting immediate referral

Sources to verify
- Resuscitation Council UK / Resus UK paediatric algorithms (Mar 2022) [RCUK]  
- RCEM guidance (paediatric ketamine Feb 2020; magnet ingestion 2021) [RCEM]  
- BSPED DKA guideline (2020) [BSPED]  
- NICE guidelines: NG91 (Otitis media), NG29 (Fluids in children), NG143/NG195 (Fever/Neonatal infection), NG9 (Bronchiolitis) [NICE]  
- BNFC / BNF for Children dosing references (salbutamol, aminophylline, magnesium) [BNFC]  
- MDCalc Kocher criteria (paediatric septic arthritis) [MDCalc]  
- LitFL intraosseous access review [LitFL]  

Final note: This chapter collates high‑yield exam and ED‑practical points. Check local paediatric, neonatal and critical‑care protocols for any variable/flagged doses, licensing differences (e.g., intranasal diamorphine), and exact HTS/ketamine/antibiotic recommendations before clinical use.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 04: TRAUMA
============================================================================================

Chapter 04 — Trauma

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core principle: immediate life/limb/sight threats first. cABCDE (catastrophic haemorrhage, Airway with C‑spine control, Breathing, Circulation, Disability, Exposure). Do not delay life‑saving interventions for investigations. [ATLS 10th]
- Mechanism matters — energy transfer predicts injury patterns (e.g., anterior chest → RV contusion; high‑energy pelvis → major pelvic haemorrhage).
- Shock types: hypovolaemic (tachycardia, cool/clammy), neurogenic (bradycardia, warm/dry after spinal cord injury above T1–L2), cardiogenic (signs of poor cardiac output).
- Trauma physiology: permissive hypotension/limit crystalloids in uncontrolled haemorrhage; early balanced transfusion and haemostasis prioritised (damage‑control haemorrhage). [CRASH‑2 / ATLS principles]
- Paediatric differences: ligamentous laxity → SCIWORA risk; different imaging and immobilisation thresholds; buckle (torus) distal‑radius often managed with removable splint (evolving evidence — FORCE). [FORCE, NICE paediatric guidance]

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- الأولوية لإيقاف المخاطر المهددة للحياة فوراً باتّباع cABCDE. آلية الإصابة تحدد نمط الأذى. لدى الأطفال قواعد تصوير وعلاج مختلفة وكسور التورس غالباً تُعالج بجبيرة قابلة للنزع (دراسات جديدة).

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Airway compromise: blood pooling/gurgle, stridor, expanding neck haematoma, severe mid‑face fracture → sit up, suction, prepare definitive airway. Avoid nasopharyngeal airway if basilar skull fracture suspected. [ATLS]
- Breathing threats:
  - Tension pneumothorax: severe dyspnoea, hypotension, (late) tracheal deviation, absent breath sounds, hyperresonance → immediate decompression.
  - Open (sucking) pneumothorax: visible chest wound with air entry/bubbling → three‑sided occlusive dressing then chest drain.
  - Massive haemothorax: hypotension, dullness, poor air entry → large‑bore chest tube and MHP.
- Circulation / vascular injury:
  - Hard signs: pulsatile bleeding, expanding haematoma, audible bruit/thrill, absent distal pulses/acute limb ischaemia → urgent operative/angiographic management.
  - Pelvic instability/haemodynamic compromise → pelvic binder at greater trochanters and activate MHP. [BOA]
- Neurology / head injury red flags (CT within 1 hour): GCS <14 at 2 hours, focal neuro deficit, post‑traumatic seizure, suspected depressed/open skull fracture, basilar skull signs (CSF leak, Battle’s sign), repeated vomiting. CT within 8 hours for anticoagulated/antiplatelet patients unless earlier indicated. [NICE NG232]
- Eye/orbit: proptosis, RAPD, decreased vision, resistance to retropulsion → retrobulbar haemorrhage requiring immediate lateral canthotomy/cantholysis. [EyeWiki]
- Limb threats: knee dislocation → popliteal artery injury (absent pulses → urgent reduction ± CTA/vascular input); compartment syndrome: pain out of proportion and pain on passive stretch (pulses late).  
- Miscellaneous red flags: exposed bone (open fracture), evisceration, penetrating torso with peritonism, signs of spinal cord compression/herniation (one dilated pupil + dropping GCS).

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- علامات حمراء رئيسية: انسداد مجرى الهواء، ذبحة صدرية توتّريّة، رئة مفتوحة، نزف حوضى حاد، علامات عصبية بالجمجمة (تحتاج CT سريع)، نزيف خلف العين (يتطلب عمل جانبي عاجل)، خلع الركبة مع نبضات مفقودة وخطر متلازمة الحجرة (ألم زايد مع الشدّ السّلبى).

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- General: primary survey with simultaneous resuscitation. Use eFAST + portable CXR for unstable patients. Do not send unstable patients to CT. [NICE NG39]
- Head CT timing (NICE NG232):
  - Within 1 hour for red flags: GCS <13 initially, GCS <15 at 2 hours, focal neuro signs, suspected open/depressed skull fracture, seizure, repeated vomiting, basilar skull signs.
  - Within 8 hours for patients on anticoagulants or with less urgent features where CT still indicated.
- C‑spine: CT is first‑line in adults with high‑risk mechanism, midline tenderness or neurological signs; in children follow paediatric rules and consider MRI if neuro deficits with normal CT (SCIWORA).
- Vascular injury pathway:
  - Hard signs → urgent OR/IR ± immediate exploration.
  - Soft signs → CTA or serial exams; consider duplex/ABI as adjunct.
- Chest: unstable + suspected tension → immediate decompression (no imaging delay). Stable → CXR/eFAST then CT chest/CTA as indicated.
- Orbit/eye: do not delay lateral canthotomy if sight threatened; imaging after decompression if needed. [EyeWiki]
- Orthopaedic imaging: scaphoid suspicion → thumb axial loading positive = high specificity; if initial XR negative and high clinical suspicion immobilise and arrange repeat imaging or MRI/CT.
- Laboratory/POC: group & save, FBC/coag/ABG/lactate, ionised calcium during massive transfusion.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- المسح الأولي متزامن مع الإنعاش؛ eFAST و CXR للمرضى غير المستقرّين. تصوير الرأس حسب قواعد NICE (1 ساعة أو 8 ساعات). علامات الأوعية الدموية الحادة تحتاج تدخلاً فورياً؛ الإنعاش بالدم فوري عند النزف الحاد.

4. Emergency management algorithms
(A) Airway in facial/neck trauma
- Immediate: sit upright/lean forward, suction, jaw‑thrust with C‑spine control.
- If cannot maintain airway or failing oxygenation → RSI with surgical airway back‑up.
- If intubation fails and definitive surgical airway required:
  - Adults/older children: emergency surgical cricothyroidotomy.
  - Young children (<10–12 yrs) or anatomy/coagulopathy concerns: needle cricothyroidotomy as temporising measure until definitive airway (local protocols vary). [ATLS; LITFL flagged]

(B) Major haemorrhage / damage‑control haemorrhage
1. Control external catastrophic bleeding (direct pressure, tourniquet for limb exsanguination).
2. Two large‑bore IVs / IO if needed; limit crystalloids; activate MHP early for ongoing hypotension.
3. Transfuse balanced blood products per local MHP; massive transfusion commonly defined as ≥10 units PRBC/24 h (definitions vary).
4. Give TXA if within 3 hours: 1 g IV over ~10 min then 1 g over 8 h (CRASH‑2 regimen).
5. Apply pelvic binder at greater trochanter level for suspected unstable pelvic ring; then arrange pelvic X‑ray/CTA/IR/OR as indicated. [CRASH‑2; BOA]

(C) Raised ICP / impending herniation
- Immediate measures: head up 30°, ensure oxygenation/ventilation (avoid hypercapnia), brief hyperosmolar therapy while arranging neurosurgical care:
  - Hypertonic saline 3%: bolus 3–5 mL/kg (institutional practice varies); OR
  - Mannitol 0.5–1 g/kg if euvolaemic and renal function acceptable.
- Urgent neurosurgical notification and prepare for CT/surgery. Avoid prolonged hyperventilation unless imminent herniation.

(D) Tension pneumothorax / open pneumothorax
- Tension: immediate decompression — in trauma peri‑arrest preferred open finger thoracostomy / tube thoracostomy at the bedside; needle decompression only temporising if nothing else available. Then insert chest drain.
- Open pneumothorax: three‑sided occlusive dressing (or finger seal) immediately, high‑flow oxygen, chest drain definitive.

(E) Limb vascular compromise (e.g., knee dislocation)
- Immediate gentle reduction of dislocation → reassess distal perfusion.
- If pulses absent or ischaemia persists → urgent vascular surgical input ± CTA/OR. Don’t delay reduction/transfer.

(F) Orbital compartment syndrome (retrobulbar haemorrhage)
- If proptosis, severe pain, falling vision, RAPD, resistance to retropulsion → immediate lateral canthotomy and cantholysis at bedside; do not await imaging. [EyeWiki]

(G) Compartment syndrome
- Remove constrictive dressings/cast; keep limb at heart level (avoid excessive elevation).
- If clinical concern persists → measure compartment pressures; delta pressure (diastolic BP − compartment pressure) <30 mmHg or absolute pressure >30 mmHg supports fasciotomy (local thresholds variably used). Urgent fasciotomy if confirmed.

(H) Traumatic cardiac arrest (TCA)
- Prioritise reversible causes: catastrophic haemorrhage (MHP), tension pneumothorax (bilateral finger thoracostomies if chest trauma + arrest), cardiac tamponade (consider ED thoracotomy in selected patients with witnessed arrest and short downtime), airway obstruction.
- Standard ALS drugs/compressions are less effective until reversible causes addressed. Resuscitative thoracotomy reserved for very select patients (penetrating chest with short prehospital CPR / local protocol criteria). [RCEM 2019]

(I) Burns & frostbite
- Burns first aid: cool running water (not ice) for 20 minutes (if within 3 hours), analgesia, cover with cling film, estimate BSA, arrange referral if indicated.
- Frostbite: rapid rewarming in circulating water 40–42°C until tissue pliable (≈15–30 min); avoid refreeze. [RCEM frostbite guidance]

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- خوارزميات رئيسية: تأمين المجرى الهوائي وتهيئة العملية الجراحية للمجرى في الكسر الوجهي/الرقبي، تفعيل بروتوكول النزف الكبير مع نقل متوازن ومنح TXA ضمن 3 ساعات، تفريغ الضغط الدماغي بالمحاليل عالية التوتر أو المانيتول، تفريغ الرئة التوتّري فورياً، تقليل الخلع لإعادة الدورة الدموية عند خلع الركبة، عمل جانبي للعين عند نزف خلف العين، وشقّ الحجرة وإحالة فورية إذا لزم.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
Drugs / key doses (well‑established)
- Tranexamic acid (TXA): 1 g IV bolus over ~10 min then 1 g IV over 8 h — within 3 h of injury for major bleeding (CRASH‑2). [CRASH‑2]
- Hyperosmolar therapy for raised ICP:
  - Mannitol 0.5–1.0 g/kg IV (use if euvolaemic, check renal function).
  - Hypertonic saline 3% bolus ~3–5 mL/kg (institutional doses vary).
- Tetanus post‑exposure: for unvaccinated with dirty/deep wound give human tetanus immunoglobulin (HTIG) + active vaccination per local schedules.
- Bite prophylaxis: co‑amoxiclav first‑line for human/dog bites with skin break (IV for deep/hand bites; oral for minor prophylaxis) — verify local dosing. [NICE CKS/NG184]
- Haemophilia with bleeding/head injury: urgent specific factor replacement (haematology guidance) and early specialist contact.
- Avoid nasal airways in basilar skull fracture.

Procedures (indications & cautions)
- Emergency surgical cricothyroidotomy: definitive airway if failed intubation and cannot oxygenate (adult).
- Needle cricothyroidotomy: temporising in small children or when surgical cricothyroidotomy contraindicated; provides oxygenation but limited ventilation — escalate early. (Age cutoffs vary; verify local policy.)
- Chest drain: insert in safe triangle (5th ICS mid‑axillary line commonly) for pneumothorax/haemothorax; large bore for massive haemothorax per local practice. Beware diaphragmatic rupture — do not drain suspected intra‑abdominal viscera in chest.
- Lateral canthotomy / cantholysis: bedside for orbital compartment syndrome; do not await imaging. [EyeWiki]
- Fasciotomy: for clinically suspected compartment syndrome (do not wait for pulseloss).
- Pelvic binder: apply at greater trochanters; remove only after stabilisation and definitive imaging/definitive plan. [BOA]

Disposition & follow‑up
- Immediate OR/IR transfer: unstable torso with peritonism/evisceration, uncontrolled pelvic haemorrhage needing IR/OR, ongoing massive thoracic bleeding, ED thoracotomy indications.
- ICU/High‑dependency: airway protection after major trauma, ongoing MHP, raised ICP needing monitoring, major burns, multi‑system trauma.
- Observation/ward: minor injuries with safe discharge criteria and arranged follow‑up (orthopaedics/plastics/ENT/eye/neurosurgery as indicated).
- Head injury: follow local observation protocols; repeat CT if neurological deterioration; discharged head‑injury patients need clear return advice and arranged follow‑up as required.
- Orthopaedics/plastics/vascular referrals: open fractures, tendon/nerve injuries (digital nerve repairs ideally early, <24 h for clean wounds), vascular injury, compartment syndrome.

Pitfalls / safety notes
- Don’t remove pelvic binder pre‑imaging/stabilisation unless necessary; binder can mask fracture displacement on CT—plan post‑binder imaging. [BOA]
- Avoid excessive crystalloid in uncontrolled haemorrhage; early balanced transfusion is preferred.
- Verify antibiotic choices/durations and local MHP ratios — institutional variations exist.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- أدوية رئيسية: TXA (1 g ثم 1 g/8h ضمن 3 ساعات)، مانيتول 0.5–1 g/kg أو 3% محلول ملح عالي التوتر 3–5 mL/kg لخفض الضغط داخل الجمجمة. إجراءات رئيسية: تريكوثيرويدوتومي للطوارئ، درن صدري للحالات المناسبة، شق جانبي للعين عند النزف خلف العين، وفاسيوتومي عند متلازمة الحجرة. فرّق التنويم بين نقل عاجل للعمليات، رعاية مكثفة، ومتابعة مسارية.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
Exam tricks
- “Pelvic binder at greater trochanters” is a frequent MCQ answer — learn placement.
- Scaphoid: pain on axial thumb loading is highly specific (snuffbox tenderness is sensitive). Use “thumb axial loading” as exam cue.
- Anterior cord syndrome: motor + pain/temp loss with preserved proprioception/vibration — classic localisation.
- Retrobulbar haemorrhage: vision threat → immediate lateral canthotomy (don’t wait for imaging).
- For head injury CT timing memorise “1 hour vs 8 hours” split from NICE NG232 (1 h for red flags; 8 h for anticoagulated/less urgent features).

Common pitfalls
- Don’t nasal intubate or insert a nasogastric tube with suspected basilar skull fracture.
- Pelvic binder can mask fractures on CT — plan imaging accordingly; don’t remove binder until stable.
- In traumatic cardiac arrest prioritise reversible mechanical causes (tension pneumothorax, tamponade, massive haemorrhage) over routine ALS emphasis — RCEM emphasises cause‑directed treatment. [RCEM 2019]
- Pulses may remain present in early compartment syndrome — the earliest sign is disproportionate pain and pain on passive stretch.
- Do not delay definitive airway in facial/neck trauma for imaging; prepare surgical airway early.
- Beware statements that “completely avoid immobilisation for paediatric buckle fractures” — FORCE shows evolving evidence but practice may vary; exam answers often expect removable splint/cast.
- Know that many numeric thresholds (ED thoracotomy time cutoffs, MHP pack composition, chest drain sizes) vary by local policy — flag such numbers in the stem and default to local protocol where required.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- حِيَل امتحانية: وضع رباط الحوض على الشوك العظمي الأكبر، اختبار تحميل الإبهام للكسر الإسفيني، متلازمة الحبل الأمامية وصفاتها، عمل جانبي العين فوراً عند خطر البصر. مطبات شائعة: لا تدخل أنبوب أنفى عند كسر قاعدي، النبض لا ينفي متلازمة الحجرة، وركز على معالجة الأسباب القابلة للعكس في توقف القلب الرضحي.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (ملف سريع)
- قم أولاً بتأمين المجرى الهوائي مع حماية العمود الفقري، ثم التنفّس والدورة الدموية (cABCDE).  
- علّم تجنّب التصوير للمريض غير المستقر — عالج الأسباب القابلة للعكس فوراً (تفريغ صدرى توتّري، نزف حوضى، انصباب تامورى). [RCEM; NICE]  
- تصوير الرأس: اتبع NICE NG232 — تصوير خلال ساعة للعلامات الحمراء؛ خلال 8 ساعات للمخاطر المتوسطة مثل تناول مضادات التخثر. [NICE NG232]  
- نزف كبير: فعّل بروتوكول النزف الكبير، قلّل البلورات، أعطِ منتجات دموية متوازنة، وانقل TXA ضمن 3 ساعات (1 g + 1 g). [CRASH‑2]  
- إصابة خلف العين (retrobulbar) = عمل جانبي عاجل (lateral canthotomy) فوراً إذا انخفض البصر.  
- خلع الركبة مع نبضات مفقودة → تقليل فوري وإحالة جراحية للأوعية الدموية إن استمرت الآفات.  
- الحروق: تبريد جارى 20 دقيقة، تغطية بالحافظة، تقييم نسبة مساحة الجسم المصابة.  
- لدغات الإنسان/الكلاب العميقة أو اليدية → co‑amoxiclav مبدئياً وتقييم جرعة/طريق إعطاء محلى. [NICE CKS]

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered; original Q‑numbers not supplied)
- Head injury imaging and timing (NICE NG232): CT within 1 hour vs 8 hours — concepts from head‑injury stems.
