============================================================================================
CHAPTER 05: VASCULAR
============================================================================================

Chapter 05: Vascular

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Major vascular emergencies: acute aortic syndromes (dissection, rupture), ruptured abdominal aortic aneurysm (rAAA), acute arterial occlusion (embolus, in‑situ thrombosis), extensive venous thrombosis/phlegmasia, penetrating/iatrogenic arterial injury and pseudoaneurysm, carotid/vertebral artery dissection, vascular injury from procedures/central lines.
- Pathophysiology essentials:
  - Aortic dissection: intimal tear → false lumen propagation, organ malperfusion, aortic regurgitation, rupture. Type A needs surgery; Type B often medical unless complications.
  - rAAA: contained → free rupture, massive haemorrhage and shock.
  - Acute limb ischaemia (ALI): sudden arterial inflow loss → muscle/nerve ischemia; muscle viability commonly cited ~4–6 h.
  - Phlegmasia cerulea dolens: massive venous outflow obstruction → oedema, venous gangrene risk.
  - Iatrogenic arterial injury: hard signs (pulsatile/expanding haematoma, active arterial bleeding, bruit/thrill, ischaemia) predict need for urgent OR.

Arabic: لمحة وشرح المرضي — حالات أوعوية رئيسية: تمزق الأبهر/تمدد منهار، انسداد شرياني حاد، تخثر وريدي واسع، إصابات شريانية رضّية أو ناتجة عن إجراءات. تلف العضلات في انسداد الطرف الحاد يحدث بسرعة (~4–6 س).

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Aortic dissection: sudden severe ‘tearing’ chest/back pain; inter-arm BP difference >20 mmHg; new diastolic murmur (acute AR); syncope/neurology; widened mediastinum on CXR suggestive but not sensitive. CTA = definitive imaging. [RCEM/RCR 2025]
- rAAA: sudden abdominal/back pain ± hypotension ± pulsatile abdominal mass; unstable → immediate operative management.
- ALI: the “6 Ps” — pain, pallor, pulselessness, paresthesia, paralysis, poikilothermia. Sensory change and severe pain occur before pulse loss. Look for AF/embolic source.
- Phlegmasia: massively swollen, painful, cyanotic limb; pulses may be present early; high risk of venous gangrene.
- Carotid/vertebral dissection: recent neck trauma/manipulation, unilateral neck pain/headache, Horner’s syndrome, focal cerebral ischaemia.
- Iatrogenic/penetrating arterial injury: hard signs → immediate OR. Soft signs (history of penetrating injury, expanding haematoma without clear bleeding) → urgent imaging/vascular review.

Arabic: التعرف والتفريق — ألم صدري/ظهري تمزيقي لتمزق الأبهر، ألم بطني/ظهري شديد مع خفة ضغط للـ rAAA، 6P للانسداد الشرياني الحاد، ساق زرقاء ومؤلمة للـ phlegmasia، علامات «hard» للنزف الشرياني التداخلي تعني حاجة فورية للجراحة.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate bedside: ABC, ECG (search for AF/MI), focused vascular exam, handheld Doppler, ABPI (abnormal <0.9; borderline 0.9–1.0; non‑compressible >1.4 suggests calcification).
- Imaging selection:
  - CTA chest/whole‑aorta: first‑line for suspected thoracic aortic dissection and planning (fast, widely available). [RCEM/RCR 2025]
  - TTE/TEE: TTE for pericardial effusion/AR; TEE more sensitive for proximal aortic dissection if available.
  - Duplex US: first-line for femoral pseudoaneurysm and for lower-limb DVT; useful when CTA unavailable or for rapid bedside assessment.
  - CT angiography (arterial phase): for mesenteric/ischaemic limbs and operative planning.
- Laboratory utility: D-dimer nonspecific — may be raised in dissection but cannot reliably exclude high‑risk presentations; use only in low‑risk algorithms. Baseline bloods for ALI/reperfusion: CBC, U&E, creatinine, CK, lactate, coagulation, blood type & screen.
- Practical caveats: do NOT delay life‑saving operative transfer for CT in haemodynamically unstable rAAA; do not delay anticoagulation if ALI suspected.

Arabic: نهج تشخيصي — ECG، دوبلر يدوي، ABPI، CTA للأبهر، دوبلكس للأوردة/خراجات القسطرة؛ D‑dimer محدود الفائدة في الحالات عالية الخطورة. لا تؤخر العمليات الطارئة من أجل التصوير.

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. Suspected acute aortic dissection
- Immediate:
  1. ABC, high-dependency monitoring, IV access, analgesia (IV opiate).
  2. Start IV beta‑blocker (e.g., IV labetalol/esmolol) first to reduce shear; target HR <60, then add vasodilator (GTN/nicardipine) if SBP > target. Aim SBP ≈100–120 mmHg and HR <60 (guideline‑dependent). [RCEM/RCR 2025]
  3. Urgent vascular/cardiothoracic surgery for Type A; Type B medical unless malperfusion/rupture → urgent TEVAR/surgery.
  4. If tamponade/rupture → immediate surgery.

B. Suspected ruptured AAA (unstable)
- Immediate:
  1. ABCs, permissive hypotension (avoid large-volume crystalloids that may worsen bleeding) — target SBP ranges vary; aim to maintain perfusion while avoiding hypertension. [RCEM rAAA 2021]
  2. Activate vascular/trauma/anaesthesia and transfer to OR/IR immediately; do not delay for CT if unstable.

C. Acute limb ischaemia (ALI)
- Immediate algorithm:
  1. Start IV unfractionated heparin unless contraindicated (see section 5).
  2. Urgent vascular/IR consult and preparation for revascularisation (embolectomy/thrombectomy, bypass, or catheter‑directed thrombolysis depending on viability/time).
  3. Analgesia; limb flat and warm; avoid elevation; frequent neurovascular checks.
  4. Monitor for compartment syndrome → prepare for fasciotomy if clinical signs.

D. Phlegmasia cerulea dolens
- Immediate:
  1. IV heparin.
  2. Urgent vascular intervention for thrombectomy or catheter‑directed thrombolysis ± stenting.
  3. Monitor perfusion and compartment pressures; fasciotomy if required.

E. Penetrating/iatrogenic arterial injury with hard signs
- Immediate surgical control: direct pressure, proximal control; urgent transfer to OR. Do not delay for imaging if limb or life threatened.

F. Pseudoaneurysm after arterial access
- Confirm with duplex; treat by ultrasound‑guided thrombin injection or compression if appropriate; surgery if infected/large/expanding.

Arabic: خوارزميات الطوارئ — تمزق الأبهر: خفض ضربات القلب والضغط، Type A جراحة فورية. rAAA: انخفاض ضغط مقبول ونقل فوري للعمليات. ALI/phlegmasia: هيبارين فوري ومراجعة وعائية لإعادة التروية. إصابة شريانية مع علامات «hard» → إلى غرفة العمليات فوراً.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
Drugs (well‑established thresholds/doses where commonly used)
- Beta‑blockers in dissection: IV labetalol/esmolol titrated to HR <60 (drug choice and doses per local guidance). Give beta‑blocker before vasodilator. [RCEM/RCR 2025]
- Vasodilators: IV nicardipine/GTN added if BP remains above target after HR control.
- Unfractionated heparin (ALI/phlegmasia):
  - Bolus commonly 70–100 units/kg IV; many EDs use 5,000 units if weight unknown.
  - Infusion ~15–20 units/kg/hr, titrate to aPTT ~1.5–2.5× control per local protocol.
  - Institutional protocols vary—verify local anticoagulation guidance.
- Reversal agents: PCC/Vitamin K for urgent warfarin reversal per haematology/surgical advice.

Procedures
- Definitive: emergent thoracic aortic repair (Type A), TEVAR (complicated Type B), open repair or endovascular repair for rAAA, embolectomy/thrombectomy for ALI, catheter‑directed thrombolysis or mechanical thrombectomy for phlegmasia, fasciotomy for compartment syndrome.
- Emergency OR vs imaging: hard signs of vascular injury or unstable rAAA/tamponade → immediate OR without further imaging if required.
- Pseudoaneurysm: ultrasound‑guided thrombin injection, ultrasound compression, or surgical repair.

Disposition and follow‑up
- Admit to high‑dependency or ICU for most aortic syndromes, rAAA postoperative care in critical care.
- ALI: urgent admission to vascular theatre/IR; postop/after revascularisation monitor for reperfusion injury (hyperkalaemia, acidosis, rhabdomyolysis, renal failure).
- Phlegmasia: urgent vascular intervention; inpatient anticoagulation and thrombosis workup after stabilization.
- Carotid/vertebral dissection: admit for stroke workup, antithrombotic therapy and vascular/neurology follow‑up.
- Outpatient follow‑up: vascular clinic, surveillance imaging for aneurysms/pseudoaneurysms, review anticoagulation strategy and secondary prevention (AF management, antiplatelet/statins as indicated).

Arabic: أدوية وإجراءات ووجهة المريض — هيبارين: جرعات شائعة 70–100 وحدة/كجم (أو 5000 وحدة إذا مجهول الوزن)، ضخ ~15–20 وحدة/كجم/ساعة؛ بيتا‑بلوكر أولاً في التمزق الأبهري؛ إجراءات نهائية: جراحة الأبهر، TEVAR، استئصال الصمة/تفتيت الخثرة، دعامات/فاسيوتومي حسب الحاجة. متابعة في وحدة عالية الاعتماد ومراجعة الأوعية/أمراض الدم/الأعصاب.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Tricks:
  - “6 Ps” for ALI; remember sensory change and severe pain precede absent pulses.
  - Arm BP difference >20 mmHg is a strong red flag for dissection.
  - Beta‑blocker BEFORE pure vasodilator in dissection — exam favourite.
  - If AF present + sudden limb pain → think embolus → start heparin.
- Common pitfalls:
  - Normal CXR does NOT exclude aortic dissection—don’t be reassured by a normal film.
  - Don’t give a vasodilator alone in suspected dissection (can increase shear).
  - Do NOT delay UFH in ALI for imaging; imaging is for operative planning.
  - ABPI >1.4 suggests non‑compressible calcified arteries — false normal.
  - D‑dimer: may be raised in dissection but a negative result does not exclude high‑risk presentations; use only in low‑risk algorithms.
  - In unstable rAAA/tamponade: do NOT delay for CT — prioritise transfer to theatre.
  - Beware of reperfusion injury after limb revascularisation — monitor electrolytes, CK and urine output.

Arabic: خدع امتحانية ومزالق — 6P للانسداد الشرياني، فرق ضغط الذراع مفيد للتمزق، بيتا‑بلوكر قبل موسع للأوعية، لا تؤجل الهيبارين من أجل التصوير، CXR طبيعي لا ينفي التمزق، D‑dimer غير موثوق في الحالات عالية الخطورة.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary
- تمزق الأبهر: ألم صدري/ظهري مفاجئ تمزيقي، فرق ضغط بين الذراعين، ابدأ بيتا‑بلوكر ثم اضبط الضغط؛ Type A تحتاج جراحة فورية. [RCEM/RCR 2025]
- تمدد أبهري متمزق: ألم بطني/ظهري وعلامات صدمة → ضغط مقبول ونقل فوري إلى العمليات؛ لا تؤجل للتصوير إن غير مستقر. [RCEM rAAA 2021]
- انسداد شرياني حاد: 6P؛ ابدأ هيبارين ورافق للمراجعة الوعائية فوريًا؛ نافذة إنقاذ العضلة ~4–6 س.
- تخثر وريدي واسع (phlegmasia): ساق زرقاء ومتوّرة → هيبارين وفعل وعائي عاجل (خطر غنغرينا).
- إصابة شريانية «hard» → إلى غرفة العمليات فورًا؛ كدمة/وريقة كاذبة بعد قسطرة → دوبلكس ثم حقن ثومبين أو جراحة حسب الحالة.
- تحذيرات: D‑dimer وCXR محدودة؛ ABPI>1.4 يدل على أوعية متكلسة؛ راجع البروتوكولات المحلية للجرعات وحدود ضغط الدم.

8. Question coverage map (topics / source tags)
- Acute limb ischaemia (ALI) — clinical signs, immediate heparin, embolectomy/IR, reperfusion complications (Source: Q33; general ALI content)
- Aortic dissection — recognition (tearing pain, interarm BP difference), imaging (CTA/TEE), BP/HR targets, beta‑blocker before vasodilator [RCEM/RCR 2025]
- Ruptured AAA — recognition, permissive hypotension, immediate operative management [RCEM rAAA 2021]
- Phlegmasia cerulea dolens — presentation, heparin, urgent thrombectomy/thrombolysis
- Iatrogenic/penetrating arterial injury & pseudoaneurysm — hard signs → OR; duplex, thrombin injection/compression options
- Carotid/vertebral dissection — neck pain/headache after minor trauma, Horner’s, stroke risk
- Diagnostic tests — CXR, CTA, duplex US, TTE/TEE, ABPI, D‑dimer limitations
- Procedural/complication management — fasciotomy for compartment syndrome, reperfusion injury monitoring/treatment, central‑line removal precautions
- Practical dosing/targets flagged for verification — UFH dosing, aPTT targets, BP ranges for dissection and rAAA (see notes above)

Final verification note
- Statements about exact numeric BP targets, universal fixed heparin boluses, and D‑dimer utility are guideline‑dependent and flagged for local verification.

Sources to verify
- RCEM rAAA guideline 2021; RCEM/RCR guidance 2025; national vascular surgery guidelines; European Resuscitation Council (ERC); NICE; institutional anticoagulation protocols; current vascular surgery and interventional radiology guidance.

This is study material, not patient‑specific advice. Verify local protocols and latest guidelines before clinical use.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 06: UROLOGY
============================================================================================

Chapter 06 — Urology

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Time-critical urology emergencies: testicular torsion, ischaemic priapism, penile fracture, obstructing infected urinary stone — early action preserves function.  
- Presentations cluster around severe localized pain, urinary obstruction, haematuria, swelling, or systemic infection. Pathology may be vascular (torsion, priapism), traumatic (fracture), obstructive (stone, catheter blockage) or infective/inflammatory (epididymo‑orchitis, prostatitis, balanitis).  
- ED role: rapid recognition, bedside tests/imaging, prompt analgesia/antibiotics, urgent specialist referral or immediate procedural intervention when indicated.

Arabic: لمحة وباثوفيزيولوجيا — الطوارئ البولية قد تكون وعائية أو رضّية أو عائقاً أو التهابياً؛ التدخل المبكر مهم للحفاظ على الوظيفة.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Flank/groin pain: stone (colic) vs pyelonephritis vs AAA. Haematuria common with stones but not required. Red flags: sepsis signs, anuria, AKI, solitary kidney or bilateral obstruction.  
- Acute scrotum: torsion = sudden severe pain, high‑riding/ horizontal testis, absent cremasteric reflex, nausea/vomiting. Epididymo‑orchitis = gradual onset, urinary symptoms, intact cremasteric reflex, may improve with scrotal elevation (Prehn’s sign unreliable). Blue‑dot sign suggests torsion of testicular appendix. Use TWIST score to stratify risk [MDCalc]. Red flags: fever/sepsis, skin changes, testicular viability threatened.  
- Priapism: ischaemic (painful, rigid erection >4 h) vs non‑ischaemic (painless, partially rigid). Red flags: sickle cell disease, prolonged erection >4 h, severe pain — needs urgent decompression.  
- Penile trauma: “pop”, immediate detumescence, swelling/ecchymosis = penile (tunica) fracture — surgical emergency.  
- Balanitis/balanoposthitis: local erythema, white patches (candida), pruritus.  
- Acute prostatitis: systemic/voiding symptoms + tender boggy prostate on DRE. Avoid prostatic massage.  
- Suprapubic catheter (SPC) problems: blocked/new tract risk — do not change new SPC in ED without urology.

Arabic: التعرّف والتمييز — ميز بين مغص كلوي، الصفن الحاد، بريابيْسِم ناقص التروية، وكسور القضيب؛ راقب علامات الخطر مثل الحمى، فشل الكلوي، أو قثطرة فوق العانة حديثة مسدودة.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Renal/ureteric stone: low‑dose non‑contrast CT KUB = gold standard in adults; renal ultrasound for pregnancy/young females or to detect hydronephrosis if CT unsuitable [NICE CKS]. Urine dipstick for blood/leucocytes supportive but neither sensitive nor specific.  
- Acute scrotum: high TWIST → immediate exploration; intermediate → urgent Doppler scrotal ultrasound; low → consider conservative management. Do not delay theatre if clinical suspicion high.  
- Priapism: clinical diagnosis. Cavernosal blood gas differentiates low‑flow (acidotic, hypoxic) from high‑flow (oxygenated).  
- Epididymo‑orchitis: Doppler ultrasound to exclude torsion if uncertain; urine culture and STI testing guided by age/sexual history.  
- Painless macroscopic haematuria in older adults → urgent urology referral for malignancy workup [NICE CKS].  
- SPC blockage: try sterile saline flush first (do not change SPC <7 days old in ED). If flushing fails and tract matured, consult urology for exchange/cystoscopy.

Arabic: الخطة التشخيصية — اعتمد CT منخفض الجرعة للحصى عند البالغين، الموجات فوق الصوتية للخصية عند الشك؛ الفحص السريري غالباً حاسم في تورشن وبريابيْسِم.

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. Renal/ureteric colic (suspected obstructing stone)
- Step 1: rapid assessment for sepsis, AKI, solitary kidney/bilateral obstruction. If present → urgent admission/urology.  
- Step 2: analgesia immediately — NSAID first‑line unless contraindicated (e.g., diclofenac PR 100 mg single dose or equivalent) [RCEM]. Antiemetic and IV fluids if vomiting/dehydration.  
- Step 3: imaging (CT KUB) if diagnosis uncertain or to guide intervention. Admit if sepsis, uncontrolled pain/vomiting, obstructing solitary kidney, bilateral obstruction, or AKI risk [NICE CKS].

B. Acute scrotum (suspected torsion)
- Step 1: rapid clinical assessment (TWIST score). If high probability (TWIST ≥5) → immediate urology/surgical exploration; DO NOT wait for ultrasound.  
- Step 2: intermediate probability → urgent Doppler US; negative/uncertain but persistent concern → explore.  
- Step 3: if epididymo‑orchitis suspected → analgesia, scrotal support, bed rest, antibiotics guided by age/sexual history (see section 5).

C. Ischaemic priapism (painful, >4 h)
- Step 1: analgesia + dorsal penile nerve block. Obtain cavernosal blood gas if available.  
- Step 2: aspirate corpora cavernosa blood with saline irrigation. If detumescent → observe.  
- Step 3: if persistent → intracavernosal phenylephrine titrated per protocol (see doses). Monitor BP/HR; continuous monitoring advised.  
- Step 4: if refractory, urgent urology for shunt procedures; for sickle cell consider oxygen, IV fluids, urgent haematology/urology discussion for exchange transfusion.

D. Penile fracture
- Step 1: clinical diagnosis; immediate urology/surgical exploration and repair; do not delay for imaging if classic exam/history.

E. Paraphimosis
- Step 1: analgesia/local block, attempt manual reduction with compression/osmotic measures (sugar, mannitol).  
- Step 2: if fails → dorsal slit or other surgical release (urology).

F. SPC blockage
- Step 1: attempt sterile saline flush and repositioned patient; if SPC <7 days old do not change; contact urology if persists for exchange under guidance.

G. Post‑obstructive diuresis
- Step 1: monitor urine output hourly, replace fluids/electrolytes to match losses, monitor electrolytes and renal function; consider observation/admission if large volumes.

Arabic: خوارزميات الطوارئ — خطوات سريعة لكل حالة: مغص كلوي → NSAID أولاً + تصوير؛ الصفن الحاد → استكشاف فوري عند اشتباه قوي؛ بريابيْسِم ناقص التروية → تخفيف، شفط، فينيلإفرين، استدعاء المسالك البولية؛ كسر القضيب → جراحة عاجلة.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
Drugs (examples; verify local formularies)
- Analgesia for renal colic: NSAID first line (e.g., diclofenac PR 100 mg single dose) unless contraindicated [RCEM]. Opiates if NSAID contraindicated or inadequate.  
- Epididymo‑orchitis (likely STI, sexually active younger men): ceftriaxone 1 g IM single dose + doxycycline 100 mg PO twice daily for 10–14 days (age/sexual history dependent; check local guidance) [NICE CKS].  
- Acute bacterial prostatitis: ciprofloxacin 500 mg PO twice daily or ofloxacin 200 mg PO twice daily for 14 days (verify local guidance and consider urine culture) [NICE CKS].  
- Priapism: intracavernosal phenylephrine 100–500 µg every 3–5 min, titrate to effect with cardiovascular monitoring — follow local/urology protocol and contraindications (cardiovascular disease). Verify dosing/cumulative limits locally.  
- Antiemetics/IV fluids as required.

Procedures (ED-performed vs specialist)
- Testicular exploration/orchidopexy: surgical — urgent urology/surgery. ED should arrange transfer; do not delay for ultrasound if high suspicion.  
- Cavernosal aspiration and intracavernosal phenylephrine: may be initiated in ED with appropriate training and monitoring; always involve urology early.  
- Penile fracture repair: surgical exploration/repair by urology.  
- SPC flush: sterile saline flush in ED; exchange by urology when indicated.  
- Doppler scrotal ultrasound: ED-requested urgent imaging when indicated.

Disposition and follow-up
- Admit: sepsis, obstructing infected stone, AKI/oliguria/anuria, solitary-kidney obstruction, uncontrolled pain/vomiting, priapism >4 h or refractory, penile fracture, failed SPC flush when urology input needed.  
- Discharge with safety net: uncomplicated renal colic with analgesia, arranged outpatient urology/urology clinic, clear return precautions (fever, anuria, increasing pain, persistent erection). Provide STI testing/follow up where appropriate.  
- Arrange urgent outpatient urology for painless macroscopic haematuria in older adults.

Arabic: أدوية وإجراءات ومتابعة — استخدم مضادات الالتهاب للألم؛ المضادات الحيوية وفق العمر وتاريخ جنسي؛ فينيلإفرين داخل الكهف وفق بروتوكول؛ إجراءات مثل استكشاف الصفن وكسر القضيب وجراحة الشنت هي لمتخصصي المسالك؛ ادخل حالات العدوى الحادة والانسداد والخطر الكلوي.

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- TWIST score: useful triage tool — high score (≥5) → operate; do not let a “low” score delay action if exam strongly suggests torsion [MDCalc].  
- Prehn’s sign: poor sensitivity/specificity — do not rely on it to exclude torsion.  
- Haematuria: absence does NOT exclude ureteric stone; presence is non‑specific. Avoid overreliance on dipstick alone.  
- Imaging: CT KUB gold standard for adult stones but avoid in pregnancy — use ultrasound. Do not delay transfer for CT if immediate surgery required.  
- Prostate massage: contraindicated in suspected acute bacterial prostatitis (risk of bacteraemia/sepsis).  
- SPC care: do not replace a newly placed suprapubic catheter in ED; flush first and consult urology.  
- Phenylephrine: effective but can cause hypertension/arrhythmia — monitor; verify dosing/cumulative limits locally.  
- Antibiotic regimens: tailor to age and sexual risk — ceftriaxone + doxycycline is for likely STI‑related epididymo‑orchitis in younger men, not blanket therapy for all cases.  
- Exam trick: “pop + immediate detumescence” strongly suggests penile fracture — surgical repair is required; don’t be reassured by absent external bleeding.

Arabic: حِيَل الامتحان والأخطاء — TWIST مفيد لكن لا تعوّل عليه فقط؛ لا تستبعد الحصى بغياب الدم؛ لا تمارس تدليك البروستاتا في التهاب حاد؛ لا تغيّر قثطرة فوق العانة حديثة في الطوارئ.

7. Arabic rapid-review summary
- توقعات سريعة: التواء الخصية، بريابيْسِم ناقص التروية، وكسر القضيب طوارئ جراحية زمنية؛ التصوير بالموجات فوق الصوتية للصفن أو CT منخفض الجرعة لحصى الكلية حسب الحالة.  
- الإجراءات العاجلة: NSAID للمغص الكلوي، شفط الجوف الكهفي ثم فينيلإفرين لِـ priapism، استكشاف جراحي لكسر القضيب/التواء الخصية، غسيل قثطرة فوق العانة المسدودة أولاً.  
- مضادات حيوية: اضبطها حسب العمر والتاريخ الجنسي (سيفترياكسون + دوكسيسيكلين للحالات المشتبهة بالـSTI؛ سيبروفلوكساسين للبروستاتيت؛ تحقق من البروتوكولات المحلية).  
- تحذيرات: لا تجرّب تدليك البروستاتا عند الاشتباه بالتهاب حاد؛ لا تغيّر قثطرة فوق العانة حديثة في ED؛ راقب الضغط قبل/أثناء إعطاء فينيلإفرين.

Arabic: ملخص سريع — تعرف سريعًا، اعمل بسرعة، واستشر المسالك البولية عند الحاجة؛ اتّبع بروتوكولات المضادات والجرعات المحلية.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Renal/ureteric colic: presentation, analgesia, CT vs US, red flags (sepsis/AKI), disposition.  
- Acute scrotum: torsion vs epididymo‑orchitis vs appendage torsion, TWIST score, Doppler, exploration thresholds.  
- Priapism: ischaemic vs non‑ischaemic, cavernosal aspiration, intracavernosal phenylephrine, sickle‑cell considerations.  
- Penile fracture: recognition and urgent surgical repair.  
- Paraphimosis and balanitis/balanoposthitis: recognition, conservative reduction, topical therapy.  
- Acute bacterial prostatitis: recognition, DRE cautions, antibiotic choices.  
- Epididymo‑orchitis (STI vs non‑STI): antibiotics and imaging.  
- Suprapubic catheter issues: blocked SPC management, timing for exchange.  
- Post‑obstructive diuresis: monitoring and fluid/electrolyte replacement.  
- Exam pitfalls/tricks: Prehn’s sign, haematuria interpretation, CT use in pregnancy, SPC management, phenylephrine cautions.

Sources to verify: RCEM guidance, NICE CKS (renal/ureteric colic; epididymo‑orchitis; acute prostatitis; balanitis; suprapubic catheter management), MDCalc (TWIST score), ERC/local urology protocols, relevant urology guidance and NCBI/urology literature.

(Note: This chapter is study material, not patient‑specific advice. Verify specific drug doses and procedural techniques with local/urology protocols before clinical use.)


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 07: ULTRASOUND
============================================================================================

Chapter 07: Ultrasound

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Role: POCUS = focused bedside extension of clinical exam; ask one targeted question per scan (free fluid? tamponade? pneumothorax? AAA? DVT?). Use correct probe, window, and limited protocol (FAST/eFAST, FOCUS, lung, vascular). [POCUS101]
- Probes and windows:
  - Curvilinear or phased‑array (2–5 MHz): abdomen, FAST, cardiac (penetration).  
  - Linear high‑frequency: pleura, superficial vessels, DVT compression.  
  - Standard cardiac windows: subxiphoid (subcostal), parasternal long‑axis (PLAX), parasternal short‑axis (PSAX), apical 4‑chamber (A4C). [POCUS101]
- Orientation: pick one screen convention and learn it (e.g., in A4C left-sided chambers appear on the right of the screen). Marker orientation matters (PLAX marker → patient’s right shoulder). [POCUS101]
- Physiology signposts:
  - AAA: maximal transverse diameter measured outer wall → outer wall, perpendicular to vessel axis. Report maximal transverse diameter. [RCEM]
  - RV dysfunction: TAPSE <17 mm suggests reduced RV longitudinal function (use clinically, not in isolation). [POCUS101]
  - Lung sliding: present = pleural apposition; M‑mode seashore sign = normal; barcode/stratosphere = absent sliding (pneumothorax or mimic). [POCUS101]

Arabic (الفصحى):  
POCUS أداة مركّزة للتشخيص السريع؛ اختر المسبار والنوافذ المناسبة (قلب، بطن/FAST، رئة، أوعية). قِس AAA عبر القطر العرضي من جدار إلى جدار. تعرف نوافذ القلب وسمات انزلاق الرئة. [POCUS101] [RCEM]

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- FAST/eFAST (trauma):
  - Positive = anechoic free fluid in Morrison (RUQ), splenorenal (LUQ), pelvis (rectovesical/rectouterine), or pericardial sac. RUQ most sensitive in supine trauma. False negatives: small volumes, retroperitoneal bleed, obesity, poor windows, immediate post‑injury. [POCUS101]
- Pleural fluid vs empyema:
  - Simple effusion: anechoic, dependent, meniscus; lung may float.  
  - Complex/empyema: echogenic fluid, septations, loculations, pleural thickening; aspirate/drain planning needs US. [POCUS101]
- Pneumothorax:
  - Absent lung sliding + absent B‑lines + barcode on M‑mode = likely pneumothorax. Lung point (transition between sliding and no sliding) = diagnostic. Mimics: pleurodesis, extensive adhesions, mainstem intubation, apnea. [POCUS101]
- Cardiac tamponade:
  - Pericardial effusion ± haemodynamic compromise. US signs: RA collapse (earliest), RV diastolic collapse (more specific), large effusion with swinging heart, plethoric IVC with reduced inspiratory collapse. Interpret with clinical context. [POCUS101]
- Pulmonary embolism (PE) sonographic clues:
  - RV dilation, septal bowing (D‑sign), reduced TAPSE (<17 mm), dilated non‑collapsing IVC, possible visible right‑heart thrombus — supportive but not diagnostic. [POCUS101]
- Vascular:
  - DVT = non‑compressible vein segment on compression US; Doppler waveform adjunct. Two‑point scans miss calf DVTs. Gentle perpendicular pressure required. [POCUS101]
- Red flags:
  - Hemodynamically unstable + positive FAST/pericardial effusion → urgent surgical/vascular intervention.
  - Large AAA on bedside scan in unstable patient → do not delay definitive care for CT. [RCEM]
  - Accidental arterial (e.g., carotid) cannulation with dilatation/large bore → do NOT remove catheter blindly; leave in situ and get vascular/IR advice. [POCUS101]

Arabic (الفصحى):  
مظاهر إيجابية FAST: سائل أنيكويك في المرايا التشريحية المعروفة. استرواح الصدر يظهر بشريط الـM‑mode (barcode) وغالبًا يتطلب إفراغًا عاجلاً إذا كان هناك ضغط توتري. الانخماص الأذيني الأيمن والإفراط في توسع الوريد الأجوف السفلي توحيان بالانضغاط التاموري. [POCUS101] [RCEM]

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- FAST algorithm (unstable trauma):
  1. Scan RUQ (Morrison) → LUQ → pelvis (transverse + longitudinal fan) → subxiphoid pericardial.  
  2. If any view shows free intraperitoneal/pericardial fluid in unstable patient → activate surgical/trauma team and arrange immediate operative care/transfer. If FAST negative but instability persists → repeat FAST, extend views, or CT if stabilised. [POCUS101]
- Suspected ruptured AAA:
  - Unstable + bedside US shows large/symptomatic AAA → minimise delays: urgent operative transfer/vascular surgery; do not delay for CT if diagnosis clear and immediate surgery available. Stable or equivocal → CTA for planning. [RCEM]
- Pneumothorax workflow:
  - Absent sliding + barcode → search for lung point (specific). If tension physiology → immediate decompression (do not wait). If stable → chest X‑ray/CT according to trauma pathway. [POCUS101]
- Cardiac tamponade assessment:
  - Identify pericardial effusion → look for RA collapse (sensitive) and RV diastolic collapse (specific) → assess IVC (plethoric, low inspiratory collapse). Correlate with BP and clinical signs. Unstable → immediate pericardiocentesis or operative drainage per protocol. [POCUS101]
- DVT pathway:
  - Whole‑leg or at minimum common femoral and popliteal compression. Non‑compressible → commence anticoagulation per local/NICE guidance and arrange follow‑up imaging/vascular advice. Negative + high clinical suspicion → repeat in 5–7 days or perform whole‑leg. [NICE]
- Image optimization basics:
  - If dark → increase gain; if structure not seen → increase depth or change probe; for pleura use high‑frequency linear probe; for deep abdominal/cardiac use curvilinear/phased array. Fan and rotate to sample recesses. [POCUS101]

Arabic (الفصحى):  
خوارزمية FAST: RUQ → LUQ → حوض → تحت القص؛ إيجابيات في مريض غير مستقر تعني تحريك المريض للجراحة فورًا. AAA واضح في مريض غير مستقر → لا تؤخر العلاج الجراحي لإجراء CT. عند الاشتباه في استرواح صدر، ابحث عن lung‑point لتأكيد التشخيص إن أمكن. [POCUS101] [RCEM]

4. Emergency management algorithms
- Unstable trauma with positive FAST:
  - Immediate resuscitation (ABCDE), urgent surgical/trauma activation, prepare for theatre or transfer. Consider repeat FAST during resuscitation. [POCUS101]
- Ruptured/symptomatic AAA:
  - Unstable + clear bedside diagnosis → expeditious vascular surgery/OR transfer; avoid CT delay. Use haemorrhage control strategies; permissive hypotension often used but exact BP targets vary—follow local protocols. [RCEM] (Flag: verify BP targets locally)
- Cardiac tamponade (compromised patient):
  - Resuscitate (oxygen, IV access, IV fluids as temporising to improve preload), immediate definitive drainage:
    - Medical/non‑traumatic tamponade: ultrasound‑guided pericardiocentesis (temporising) or surgical pericardial window as indicated.  
    - Traumatic tamponade with arrest/near‑arrest (penetrating) → emergency department thoracotomy or operative sternotomy per trauma protocols. [POCUS101]
- Tension pneumothorax:
  - Unstable: immediate decompression — current in‑hospital guidance favors open thoracostomy/chest tube insertion (4th–5th ICS mid‑axillary line) rather than needle decompression where possible; then formal chest drain. Stable → image and plan definitive management. [NICE NG39]
- Pleural empyema:
  - Complex/loculated US appearance → chest drain placement ± surgical/IR referral; start appropriate antibiotics. [POCUS101]
- DVT:
  - Non‑compressible vein → start anticoagulation (DOACs first line for most adults per NICE/local guidance); pregnancy, severe renal impairment, or other contraindications → LMWH/warfarin per local protocols. [NICE]
- Accidental arterial catheterisation:
  - If large bore/dilated catheter in carotid/large artery → leave catheter in place, secure, and obtain immediate vascular/IR surgical advice; removal without expert support risks catastrophic bleeding/stroke. [POCUS101]

Arabic (الفصحى):  
مريض مصاب + FAST إيجابي = تحضير عاجل للعمليات الجراحية. AAA ظاهر وغير مستقر = نقل فوري للجراحة الوعائية؛ لا تنتظر CT. الانصباب التاموري الحرج يتطلب تصريفًا فوريًا (بواسطة القثطرة الموجهة بالموجات أو جراحة الطوارئ حسب الآلية). استرواح صدر توتري = إفراغ فوري (قثطرة صدرية/فتحة صدرية) ثم درن. [POCUS101] [RCEM] [NICE NG39]

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Drugs:
  - Anticoagulation for DVT: DOACs are first‑line for most uncomplicated DVTs per NICE/local guidance (select choice/dose from protocols). LMWH/warfarin for pregnancy, severe renal impairment, or other contraindications. [NICE]
  - Fluids/pressors in tamponade/AAA: small IV boluses may temporise preload in tamponade; permissive hypotension used in ruptured AAA—specific BP targets vary and must follow local guidance. (Flag: verify BP targets locally.) [RCEM]
- Procedures (practical pearls):
  - Ultrasound‑guided pericardiocentesis: temporising in medical tamponade if immediate surgery not available — use subxiphoid or apical approach guided by effusion location. [POCUS101]
  - Emergency department thoracotomy: consider for penetrating chest trauma with cardiac arrest in appropriate candidates per trauma protocols. [POCUS101]
  - Chest decompression: open thoracostomy/chest tube at 4th–5th ICS mid‑axillary line in hospital; needle decompression is temporising if necessary prehospital. [NICE NG39]
  - DVT ultrasound: compress vein every few millimetres through CFV to popliteal; calf veins may require whole‑leg scan. [POCUS101]
  - AAA ultrasound measurement: transverse plane, outer‑to‑outer wall, perpendicular to vessel axis; report maximal outer diameter. [RCEM]
- Disposition:
  - FAST positive + instability → theatre/specialist centre (surgical/vascular) or transfer per local trauma network.  
  - AAA unstable → immediate vascular operative care or urgent transfer to vascular centre. Stable AAA → CTA and planned definitive care. [RCEM]
  - DVT stable → discharge with anticoagulation and outpatient follow‑up unless high‑risk features. [NICE]
- Follow‑up and documentation:
  - Document scope of exam, views obtained, positive/negative findings, image saves, limitations (body habitus, windows), and clinical correlation. Escalate to senior/consultant when findings alter management. [POCUS101]

Arabic (الفصحى):  
العلاج الدوائي الرئيسي للمُثلّ: مضاد التخثر لـ DVT حسب البروتوكول المحلي (غالبًا DOAC). إجراءات الطوارئ: تصريف التامور بالموجات إرشاديًا كحل مؤقت، فتح صدر طارئ في الصدمات الانتقالية، درن صدري في الاسترواح التوتري. قِس AAA عبر عرضي من جدار إلى جدار. وثّق الصور والقياسات وحدود الفحص. [POCUS101] [RCEM] [NICE]

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Quick exam tricks:
  - FAST: RUQ (Morrison) = most sensitive in supine trauma; always fan and rotate pelvic probe (longitudinal then transverse). [POCUS101]
  - AAA measurement: always measure maximal transverse diameter outer wall → outer wall perpendicular to axis. (Exam favourite) [RCEM]
  - Cardiac views: in A4C, left heart appears on right of screen — memorise one convention. PLAX marker → patient right shoulder. [POCUS101]
  - Lung M‑mode: seashore = sliding; barcode = absent sliding. Look for lung point for high specificity. [POCUS101]
  - DVT: compressibility is primary sign; gentle pressure and perpendicular probe to avoid false‑positives. Two‑point scan may miss calf DVTs (exam trap). [POCUS101]
- Common pitfalls:
  - Absent lung sliding isn't specific for pneumothorax (adhesions, pleurodesis, mainstem intubation). Do not call pneumothorax without lung‑point or contralateral comparison when clinical context permits. [POCUS101]
  - False‑negative FAST with small/retroperitoneal bleeds, early post‑injury, obesity, operator inexperience. Repeat or move to CT when stable. [POCUS101]
  - RA vs RV collapse timing: literature descriptions vary. RA collapse usually occurs during late diastole (sensitive); RV diastolic collapse is later and more specific — be cautious answering timing questions. (Exam trick: emphasise clinical correlation.) [POCUS101]
  - TAPSE and RV size: reduced TAPSE (<17 mm) suggests RV dysfunction but is not diagnostic for acute PE alone (chronic changes or poor windows can mislead). [POCUS101]
  - Arterial catheterisation: removing a dilated arterial catheter (e.g., carotid) without vascular support is hazardous—leave in place and call vascular/IR. [POCUS101]
  - Technical first steps: if image too dark → increase gain (first); if structure not visible due to depth → increase depth or change probe. Don’t over‑filter with Doppler when basic B‑mode is inadequate.
- Exam practice tip: verbalise your clinical question, probe choice, views obtained, image optimisation steps, and limitations — examiners look for focused reasoning, not just images.

Arabic (الفصحى):  
نصائح سريعة للممتحَن: قِس AAA من جدار إلى جدار عرضياً؛ RUQ هو النافذة الأكثر حساسية في FAST؛ استخدم M‑mode لتأكيد انزلاق الرئة؛ لا تعتمد على عدم انزلاق الرئة وحدها لتأكيد استرواح صدر. احذر الإسقاط الخاطئ عند تفسير الانخماص الأذيني/البطيني. [POCUS101] [RCEM]

7. Arabic rapid-review summary
- POCUS = فحص موجات فوق صوتية موجّه وسريع بجانب السرير: أسئلة محددة لكل فحص (سائل؟ انصباب؟ استرواح؟ AAA؟ جلطة وريدية؟).  
- المسابير: منحني/مصفوفة منخفضة للتعمق (قلب/بطن)، خطي عالي للتجاويف السطحية والرئة والأوعية.  
- FAST: RUQ → LUQ → تحت القص → الحوض؛ إيجابيات في مريض غير مستقر = تحضير للعمليات.  
- AAA: قِس القطر العرضي من جدار إلى جدار بزاوية عمودية على محوره.  
- استرواح الصدر: barcode على M‑mode = غياب انزلاق؛ lung‑point تشخيصي.  
- تامبوناد: انصباب حول القلب + انخماص الأذين الأيمن ± انخماص البطين الأيمن + IVC ممتلئ.  
- DVT: عدم انضغاط الوريد = جلطة؛ ابدأ مضاد تخثر واتّبع بروتوكول المتابعة.  
- مخاطر لا تتصرف بمفردك: لا تخرج قثطارًا شريانيًا كبيرًاُ دون استشارة جراحية؛ تحقّق دومًا من إرشادات المركز المحلية. [POCUS101] [RCEM]

8. Question coverage map (sources → concepts covered)
- POCUS101 (cardiac, eFAST, lung, DVT modules): FAST sequence and windows; eFAST pneumothorax signs (sliding, M‑mode seashore/barcode, lung‑point); cardiac views and tamponade signs; RV dysfunction and TAPSE interpretation; DVT compression technique and pitfalls; image optimisation basics. [POCUS101]
- RCEM learning modules/guidance: AAA measurement technique (outer wall → outer wall, transverse), transfer/operative pathway decisions for ruptured AAA, training/competency notes. [RCEM]
- NICE NG39 / Major trauma guidance: chest decompression strategy in tension pneumothorax (chest drain/open thoracostomy site and approach), trauma assessment triage and imaging pathways. [NICE NG39]
- Exam/concept checkpoints included: FAST positive management; false‑negative FAST scenarios; pneumothorax ultrasound signs and mimics; tamponade US signs and timing caveats; AAA recognition and immediate actions; DVT compression and anticoagulation pathway; accidental arterial cannulation management. (Use local Q‑banks to map to specific question numbers.)

Arabic (الفصحى):  
مخطط التغطية: وحدات POCUS101 تغطي FAST، رئة، قلب، DVT؛ RCEM يغطي قياس AAA ومسارات النقل؛ NICE NG39 يغطي استرواح صدر توتري وإدارة الصدمات الكبرى.

Sources to verify
- POCUS101 modules (eFAST, cardiac, pleural)  
- RCEM guidance and learning modules (AAA, transfer, training)  
- NICE guideline NG39: Major trauma (assessment and initial management)  
- Local hospital/trauma/vascular/IR protocols and current resuscitation guidance (ERC/AHA) — verify procedural indications, permissive hypotension targets, and role of pericardiocentesis vs thoracotomy per institution.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 08: STATISTICS/ QUALITY IMPROVEMENT
============================================================================================

Chapter 08 — Statistics and Quality Improvement (compact FRCEM final chapter)

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Purpose: statistics/quality-improvement (QI) translate trial/test evidence into safe, reproducible ED decisions and system change.
- Core diagnostic measures (2×2): sensitivity = TP/(TP+FN); specificity = TN/(TN+FP); PPV = TP/(TP+FP); NPV = TN/(TN+FN). Likelihood ratios: LR+ = sens/(1−spec); LR− = (1−sens)/spec.
- Effect measures: absolute risk reduction (ARR) = AR_control − AR_treatment; NNT = 1/ARR (round up). Relative measures (RR, OR) need translation to absolute effects for clinical use.
- Precision & inference: 95% CI width = precision; p-values ≠ clinical importance; power = 1−β (target commonly ≈0.8).
- Diagnostic discrimination: ROC curve plots sens vs 1−spec; AUC = discrimination (0.5 = chance; 0.7–0.8 acceptable; 0.8–0.9 good; ≥0.9 excellent) [Radiopaedia].
- Meta-analysis visuals: forest plot = study boxes (point estimates) + horizontal CIs; diamond = pooled estimate; box size ∝ study weight (inverse variance); I² quantifies heterogeneity (guidance: 0–40% might not be important; 30–60% moderate; 50–90% substantial; 75–100% considerable) [Cochrane Handbook].
- Publication/small‑study bias: funnel-plot asymmetry suggests bias or heterogeneity — interpret cautiously.
- Agreement/reliability: Cohen’s kappa (two raters), Fleiss’ kappa (>2); kappa affected by prevalence.
- Trial validity basics: randomisation, allocation concealment, blinding, intention‑to‑treat (ITT), handling of missing data.
Arabic (مختصر):  
الغرض: تحويل الأدلة الإحصائية إلى قرارات سريرية وتحسينات نظامية. حساسية/نوعية من 2×2، ARR→NNT للفائدة السريرية، AUC لمنحنى ROC، مخطط الغابة والـI² للتغايرية، ومخطط القمع لتحيُّز النشر. التحقق من التعمية والـITT وجودة التصميم.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Quickly identify plot type: forest (meta‑analysis), ROC (diagnostic accuracy), funnel (publication bias), Cates pictogram (absolute effects).
- Forest-plot red flags: pooled diamond crosses null → non‑significant; many wide/overlapping CIs → imprecision; small boxes with large effect → small-study effect.
- Heterogeneity red flags: I² >50% → investigate differences in population/method/thresholds; inconsistent direction of effects → avoid simple pooling.
- ROC red flags: AUC near 0.5 or CIs spanning 0.5 → poor discrimination; chosen threshold not justified by Youden’s index or clinical consequence.
- Funnel-plot red flags: asymmetry may mean publication bias, selective reporting, true heterogeneity, or chance — do not assume bias without extra checks.
- Study/population red flags: case‑control diagnostic designs with extreme cases/healthy controls (spectrum bias), non‑consecutive sampling, lack of blinded reference standard, large loss to follow-up, fixed small block randomisation without concealment (predictable allocation).
Arabic (مختصر):  
تعرف نوع الرسم فوراً، وراقب إذا ما قطعت الماسة خط اللا تأثير، أو كان I² مرتفعاً أو AUC ضيقاً/غير كافٍ. عدم تماثل القمع ليس دليلاً قاطعاً على تحيُّز؛ تحرّ الدوافع الأخرى مثل التغايرية أو أخطاء منهجية.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Stepwise appraisal checklist:
  1. Question & population: is PICO clear and similar to your ED patients?
  2. Design & internal validity: randomisation/allocation concealment/blinding/ITT (for trials); consecutive sampling and blinded independent reference standard (for diagnostic studies).
  3. Metrics & precision: point estimates with 95% CI — does CI cross the null? compute/confirm sens/spec/PPV/NPV/LR+/LR−, AUC and CIs.
  4. Heterogeneity & bias: I², forest-plot spread, funnel-plot symmetry, registered protocol vs report, selective outcome reporting.
  5. Applicability: spectrum bias, setting (ED vs tertiary), resource/turnaround feasibility.
  6. Clinical translation: convert RR → ARR → NNT/NNH; use LRs to convert pretest → posttest probability (Fagan nomogram or odds calculation).
- Practical 5‑box quick algorithm:
  Box 1: read abstract → identify population/outcome.  
  Box 2: meta-analysis? check forest + I²; if I² >50% favour random‑effects and explore subgroups.  
  Box 3: does pooled diamond cross no‑effect? yes → non‑significant.  
  Box 4: diagnostics → check AUC (use thresholds), chosen cut‑off, LR+/LR−.  
  Box 5: translate relative to absolute (ARR → NNT/NNH), consider harms.
- Youden’s index = sensitivity + specificity − 1 to select optimal ROC cut‑off (but choose thresholds with clinical risk tolerance).
Arabic (مختصر):  
قُم بفحص التصميم والصدق الداخلي ثم حجم الأثر وثقتَه (CI). تحقق من التغايرية/I² والقابلية للتطبيق. استخدم معاملات الإحتمال لتحويل الاحتمالات قبل/بعد الاختبار، وARR→NNT لتفسير الأثر السريري.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- Applying evidence/diagnostic tests in ED (decision algorithm):
  1. Estimate pretest probability (clinical gestalt or decision rule).  
  2. If low and validated rule‑out exists → discharge/avoid testing.  
  3. If intermediate → perform high‑sensitivity test; use LR− to rule‑out safely if ≤ threshold (LR− <0.1 strong rule‑out; 0.1–0.2 moderate).  
  4. If high → proceed to definitive imaging/treatment; use high‑specificity test or LR+ (>10 strong rule‑in).
- New-test adoption algorithm:
  1. Check published discrimination (AUC ≥0.8 preferred) and external validation in ED population.  
  2. Confirm acceptable LR+/LR− and decision thresholds.  
  3. Pilot locally (small prospective audit) if heterogeneity or different prevalence.  
  4. Implement with PDSA cycles, pre-specified safety/performance targets, and SPC/run charts for monitoring.
- QI (ED-friendly PDSA) quick steps:
  Plan: define SMART aim and measures (process, outcome, balancing).  
  Do: small‑scale change.  
  Study: analyse using run-chart/SPC (distinguish common vs special cause).  
  Act: adopt/adjust/abandon; repeat cycles.
- Communicating risk: prefer absolute formats (ARR, NNT, Cates pictograms) for shared decision-making.
Arabic (مختصر):  
في الطوارئ ابدأ بتقدير الاحتمال القبلي، استخدم اختبارات حساسة للسماح بالخروج واختبارات نوعية للتأكيد، واطبق بروتوكول اعتماد محلي (تحقق خارجي، تجربة أولية، PDSA، مراقبة بالرسوم البيانية الرقابية). استخدم الأثر المطلق لشرح الخطر للمرضى.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Drugs/procedures: no new numeric dosing thresholds to invent here; apply evidence by translating RCT results into ARR/NNT and balancing NNH and harms. Present absolute benefits and harms with timeframes.
- Disposition rules driven by statistics:
  - Rule‑out safe discharge: high sensitivity test + low LR− + high NPV in local prevalence (document pretest probability and threshold used).
  - Rule‑in for invasive procedures: require high specificity and high LR+ or confirmatory imaging.
- Procedural adoption/follow-up steps:
  1. Pre‑implementation: define clinical threshold and safety metrics (miss rate, adverse events).  
  2. Implementation: pilot with prospective data collection (consecutive patients), blinded endpoints where feasible.  
  3. Post‑implementation: monitor with run charts/SPC for outcome measures (time-to-diagnosis, missed cases, adverse events, resource use). Reassess ARR/NNT periodically.
- Audit and reporting: record denominator (consecutive sampling), timings, inter-rater reliability (kappa), and unexpected harms; feed results back into PDSA.
Arabic (مختصر):  
لا تُخترع عتبات جديدة؛ استخرج فوائد/أضرار مطلقة (ARR/NN T/NNH) من الأدلة. قرار الخروج يعتمد على اختبار عالي الحساسية/LR− منخفض وNPV مناسب للسياق المحلي. نفّذ التغييرات تجريبياً مع قياس متكرر ومراقبة للمخاطر والأداء.

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Memory mnemonics: SPIN (Specific → Positive rules IN); SNOUT (Sensitive → Negative rules OUT).
- Quick formulas to memorise:
  - Sens = TP/(TP+FN); Spec = TN/(TN+FP).  
  - PPV = TP/(TP+FP); NPV = TN/(TN+FN).  
  - LR+ = sens/(1−spec); LR− = (1−sens)/spec.  
  - ARR = AR_control − AR_treatment; NNT = 1/ARR (round up).
- Forest plot exam cues: box = point estimate (size = weight), horizontal line = CI (width = precision), diamond = pooled estimate (tips = pooled CI). Diamond crossing null → pooled non‑significant.
- Heterogeneity: quote Cochrane I² guidance; high I² → explore heterogeneity, do not pool blindly [Cochrane Handbook].
- ROC/AUC traps: AUC = discrimination across thresholds, not single-threshold accuracy. AUC 0.5 = useless; 1.0 = perfect [Radiopaedia]. Use LRs for bedside decisions.
- Kappa pitfalls: kappa can be negative; affected by prevalence — do not assert 0–1 range without caveat.
- NNT/NNH: compute from absolute risk difference (not from relative risk); always state time horizon and CI; round NNT up to whole patient.
- P‑value cautions: p<0.05 ≠ clinical importance; non‑significance ≠ no effect (possible type II error).
- Funnel‑plot caution: asymmetry ≠ definitive proof of publication bias; consider heterogeneity and small‑study effects.
- Jadad score: quick quality screen (randomisation, blinding, withdrawals) but limited — do not equate high Jadad with absence of all bias [Jadad 1996].
Arabic (مختصر):  
احفظ SPIN/SNOUT والمعادلات الأساسية. في مخطط الغابة: حجم الصندوق = الوزن، الماسة تقطع خط اللا تأثير → غير ذا دلالة إحصائية. لا تعتمد على p‑value وحدها؛ احسب NNT من الفرق المطلق ودوّن الإطار الزمني.

7. Arabic rapid-review summary (مراجعة سريعة باللغة العربية)
- المفاهيم الأساسية: الحساسية = TP/(TP+FN) للتساؤل عن الاستبعاد؛ النوعية = TN/(TN+FP) للتأكيد. PPV/NPV تتأثر بالانتشار. معاملات الإحتمال (LR) مفيدة لتحويل الاحتمال قبل/بعد الاختبار.  
- مخططات: مخطط الغابة لميتاتحليل وI² للتغايرية؛ منحنى ROC وAUC للدقة؛ مخطط القمع لتحيُّز النشر (تفسير حذر).  
- تحويل الأثر إلى قرار: ARR → NNT/NNH قبل تبنّي علاج/إجراء؛ استخدم عتبات معقولة (LR+>10 rule‑in, LR−<0.1 rule‑out).  
- تحسين الجودة في الطوارئ: خطط SMART، اختبر تغييرات صغيرة (PDSA)، راقب بالرسوم البيانية الرقابية، وأكد فعالية الاختبارات في السياق المحلي قبل النصح بالاعتماد.  
- نصيحة امتحان: لا تخلط بين الحساسية/النوعية وPPV/NPV؛ لا تفسر عدم تماثل مخطط القمع كدليل قاطع على تحيُّز؛ احسب NNT من الفرق المطلق ودوّن الفترات الزمنية.
(ملاحظة: مادة دراسية فقط — ليست نصيحة علاجية مخصصة للمرضى.)

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Core diagnostic metrics and 2×2 interpretation (sensitivity, specificity, PPV, NPV, LR) — (concept cluster).
- ROC curve and AUC interpretation; Youden’s index for cut‑offs — (concept cluster).
- Meta‑analysis visuals: forest plots, pooled diamonds, box size/weight, I² heterogeneity interpretation — (concept cluster) [Cochrane Handbook; Cochrane 2016].
- Publication bias and funnel plot caveats — (concept cluster).
- Effect measures and translation to clinical practice: ARR, RRR, NNT/NNH and time horizons — (concept cluster).
- Trial validity basics: randomisation, allocation concealment, blinding, ITT, Jadad score limits — (concept cluster) [Jadad 1996].
- Agreement/reliability measures: Cohen’s/Fleiss’ kappa and limitations — (concept cluster) [Landis & Koch 1977].
- Quality improvement methods in ED: PDSA, run charts/SPC, pilot implementation, local validation — (QI cluster).
- Common biases and pitfalls: spectrum bias, verification/incorporation bias, selection bias, measurement bias, small‑study effects — (concept cluster).
- Exam tricks and calculation mnemonics: SPIN/SNOUT, formula recall, NNT rounding, interpretation of CIs & p‑values — (exam skills cluster).
