============================================================================================
CHAPTER 09: RESUSCITATION
============================================================================================

Chapter 09 — Resuscitation (compact FRCEM final chapter)

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology (core points)
- Aim: secure airway, restore oxygenation/ventilation, maintain circulation/organ perfusion, reverse treatable causes (Hs & Ts), deliver time‑critical interventions (defibrillation, thrombolysis, ECLS) when indicated. Prioritise physiology over tests. [Resuscitation Council UK; ERC/AHA]
- “Treat first what kills first”: hypoxia, massive haemorrhage, tension pneumothorax, tamponade, severe hyper/hypokalaemia, hypoglycaemia, massive PE, malignant hyperthermia.
- Special contexts modify thresholds and approach: neonatal, paediatric, pregnancy (>20 weeks → aortocaval compression concerns and perimortem hysterotomy), hypothermia (rewarming ± ECLS), tracheostomy patients, drowning, massive PE/arrest.
- Monitoring: continuous waveform capnography is gold standard for ETT confirmation and CPR quality (sudden ETCO2 rise → ROSC; sustained low ETCO2 → poor perfusion). [Resuscitation Council UK]

خلاصة بالعربية: الهدف تأمين مجرى الهواء ثم دعم التهوية والدورة ومعالجة الأسباب القابلة للعكس. اضبط النهج طبقًا للفئات الخاصة (مواليد، حوامل، انخفاض حرارة الجسم، تراكيشومى). استخدم موجة ETCO2 لمراقبة الأنبوب والإنعاش.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Airway obstruction (adult): effective cough = partial; ineffective cough/inability to speak or breathe = severe obstruction → intervene. Loss of consciousness raises arrest risk.
- Post‑ROSC hypotension: consider myocardial dysfunction, ongoing ischaemia, hypovolaemia, tension pneumothorax, PE, tamponade, arrhythmia, sepsis. Initial MAP target ≥65 mmHg. [ERC/AHA]
- Massive PE: sudden dyspnoea ± pleuritic pain, tachycardia, hypoxia, hypotension (SBP <90 or large drop) — arrest often presents as PEA. Low ETCO2 supports but is non‑specific. Treat as unstable PE if clinical probability high.
- Neonates: poor tone/blue/gasping → prematurity, birth asphyxia, airway obstruction, surfactant deficiency, sepsis, anomalies. Initial rapid assessment: tone, breathing, heart rate (NOT SpO2).
- Hypothermia: bradycardia, J waves (Osborn), ventricular arrhythmias. Consider ECLS transfer if core <30°C with instability, arrest, or refractory arrhythmia.
- Post‑intubation sudden desaturation: mainstem intubation, tube obstruction/kink, cuff leak, equipment failure, pneumothorax, bronchospasm, pulmonary oedema.

خلاصة بالعربية: علامات الخطر: سعال غير فعال في الاختناق، هبوط ضغط بعد ROSC يعنى اسباب ميكانيكية أو قلبية أو نقص حجم، صمة رئوية كبرى تعطي PEA غالبًا، وحديثي الولادة يقيمون باللون والتنفس والنبض لا SpO2. انخفاض حرارة الجسم الحاد يتطلب تفكير بتحويل ECLS.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation (rapid, goal‑directed)
- Immediate bedside priorities: airway look/listen/feel, SpO2, ECG, capnography (if ventilated), glucose, IV/IO access, VBG/ABG, bedside echo/US for tamponade, RV strain, pneumothorax/FAST.
- Airway confirmation: direct visualisation + continuous waveform capnography. Don’t rely on misting or auscultation alone. [Resuscitation Council UK]
- Post‑ROSC: ABG, 12‑lead ECG (act on clear ischaemia; routine immediate cath not mandatory), lactate, glucose, temperature, haemodynamic support to MAP ≥65 mmHg. [ERC/AHA; Resus UK]
- Massive PE diagnostics: bedside echo for RV strain when unstable; CTPA if stable; treat immediately if unstable and diagnosis likely.
- Neonatal diagnostics: preductal SpO2 targets guide O2 titration; initial decisions use heart rate and breathing — inflation breaths first if apnoeic/gasping. Preterm <32 wks: plastic wrap + radiant heat.
- Ventilator alarm/ETCO2 patterns: sudden ETCO2 fall → disconnection, loss of circulation, massive PE; sudden rise → ROSC or CO2 retention; “shark‑fin” waveform → bronchospasm/obstruction.

خلاصة بالعربية: فحوص فورية: موجة ETCO2 لتأكيد الأنابيب والإنعاش، جلوكوز سريع، ECG، صدى سريري للتامبوناد/صمة رئوية. حديثي الولادة تُقَيّم بقلب ونفس وليس SpO2 أولياً؛ القِمم قبلية للحجم الصغير.

4. Emergency management algorithms (practical concise algorithms)
- Adult choking (conscious)
  1. Effective cough/able to speak → encourage cough.
  2. Ineffective cough/airway compromise → up to 5 back blows; if ineffective → up to 5 abdominal thrusts (chest thrusts if pregnant/obese). If becomes unconscious → start CPR and inspect for visible obstruction before ventilations. [Resuscitation Council UK]
- Post‑ROSC immediate care
  1. Airway/oxygenation (capnography), ventilate as needed.
  2. Circulation: IV/IO access, MAP target ≥65 mmHg (fluids ± vasopressors; noradrenaline first‑line where needed). Treat reversible causes; obtain 12‑lead ECG and act on clear ischaemia per local policy. [ERC/AHA]
- Massive PE with haemodynamic compromise
  1. If SBP <90 or persistent hypotension → give systemic thrombolysis immediately unless contraindicated (alteplase per local protocol). If contraindicated or failed → consider surgical/percutaneous embolectomy or ECLS. After thrombolysis, continue CPR for prolonged periods (often ≥60–90 min) if indicated. [Resus UK]
- Tracheostomy emergency (green algorithm)
  1. Call for help; look/listen/feel at mouth & stoma; attach capnography.
  2. Remove speaking valve/cap, remove inner cannula, suction, deflate cuff if indicated.
  3. If obstruction persists → remove tracheostomy tube and oxygenate via face/over stoma; consider oral intubation or surgical airway. [RCEM]
- Neonatal resuscitation essentials
  1. Initial assessment: tone, breathing, heart rate.
  2. If apnoeic/gasping: 5 inflation breaths (term ≈30 cmH2O, preterm lower per local protocol) with PEEP 5–6 cmH2O if available; start ventilation and reassess heart rate.
  3. HR <60 after 30 s of effective ventilation → start chest compressions (3:1) and escalate per neonatal algorithm; adrenaline 20 µg/kg IV (1:10,000) via UVC preferred if available. [NLS 2021; Resus UK]
- Hypothermic arrest
  1. Continue CPR while rewarming. Consider transfer to ECLS centre if core <30°C with instability, arrhythmia, or arrest. Limit futile defibrillations when severely hypothermic until rewarmed as per guidance. [Resus UK]
- Intubated patient sudden desaturation
  1. Disconnect ventilator → bag‑ventilate; assess compliance and bilateral breath sounds.
  2. Check ETT depth (withdraw ~2 cm if right mainstem suspected), patency, cuff, circuit, oxygen source; look for pneumothorax and treat immediately (decompression) if suspected.
- Cardiac arrest (brief ALS)
  1. Call for help → high‑quality CPR → attach monitor/defib → shockable rhythms: defibrillate, continue CPR, give adrenaline/antiarrhythmics per ALS; use ETCO2 to guide quality/ROSC detection.
  2. Consider reversible causes and advanced options (eCPR/PCI) in selected refractory cases.

خلاصة بالعربية: خوارزميات رئيسية: الاختناق (تشجيع السعال إن فعال → 5 صفعات ظهر → 5 ضغطات بطنية → إغماء → CPR)، ما بعد ROSC (MAP≥65، ECG، علاج المسببات)، صمة رئوية كبرى → ثرومبوليز حتى تتوفر بدائل، ألم التراشي والتعامل الواضح بالخطوات المذكورة، رعاية حديثي الولادة بالتهوية أولًا و20 µg/kg أدرينالين IV عند الحاجة.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
Drugs / key, well‑established doses
- Noradrenaline: first‑line vasopressor for septic shock to target MAP ≥65 mmHg (titrate per local protocols).
- Adrenaline (neonatal): 20 µg/kg IV (1:10,000) via UVC if available. [NLS]
- Magnesium for Torsades: 2–4 g IV bolus (then consider infusion). (Well established.) 
- Hyperkalaemia (principle): stabilise membrane first with IV calcium (gluconate or chloride per local stock), then shift K+ intracellularly (insulin 10 U IV + 25 g dextrose adjunct), nebulised β2‑agonist as adjunct; arrange urgent dialysis for removal.
- Thrombolysis for massive PE: alteplase per local dosing (give immediately if unstable and no contraindication). Continue CPR prolonged if given.
- Dantrolene for malignant hyperthermia: 2.5 mg/kg initial IV (repeat as required) per MH protocols.
- Hypoglycaemia: treat immediately with IV dextrose per local protocol (e.g., 10% 100–200 ml depending on local guidance) — follow local guidance and monitoring.
Procedures / priorities
- Airway: escalate mask → supraglottic device → ETT (RSI) → surgical front‑of‑neck access (CICO/cricothyroidotomy) when cannot oxygenate/ventilate. [DAS]
- Tracheostomy emergency steps: speaking valve off → inner cannula out → suction → cuff deflate → remove tube if obstruction persists → oxygenate and secure airway.
- Perimortem hysterotomy: consider when pregnancy >20 weeks and no ROSC by ~4 minutes; aim to deliver by 5 minutes when indicated. [Resus UK]
- ECLS transfer: consider for hypothermic arrest, refractory cardiac arrest, or massive PE when available.
Disposition & follow‑up
- Post‑ROSC: ICU/HDU admission for monitoring, hemodynamic support, targeted temperature management as per local protocols, investigate/treat cause (PCI if clear STEMI or local pathway). Routine immediate coronary angiography is NOT mandatory without ischaemic ECG/clinical indicators. [ERC/AHA]
- Neonates: escalate to NICU for ongoing support and monitoring; follow neonatal unit guidelines for oxygen targets and glucose monitoring.
- Tracheostomy patients: avoid discharge without specialist review; arrange ENT/ICU follow‑up and tracheostomy care plan.
- Drowning: observe clinically stable asymptomatic patients ~6 hours with normal CXR/ABG prior to discharge per local guidance. [RCEM]

خلاصة بالعربية: أدوية أساسية: نورأدرينالين للصدمة الإنتانية (MAP≥65)، ماغنسيوم 2–4 غ لTorsades، أدرينالين حديثي الولادة 20 µg/kg IV، معايرة جرعات الكالسيوم والتحقق محليًا. إجراءات رئيسية: إطار التسلسل للتهوية، خوارزمية التراشي، تفكير سَلَس لـ ECLS أو جراحة طارئة. توجيه لمستويات أعلى ومتابعة في العناية المركزة بعد ROSC.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Do NOT intervene if choking adult is coughing effectively or can speak — encourage cough (exam favourite distractor).
- Neonatal initial rapid assessment = tone, breathing, heart rate (SpO2 is NOT for the initial decision). 5 inflation breaths then assess.
- Post‑ROSC: routine immediate coronary angiography is not automatic — act on ECG/clinical signs.
- Suxamethonium pitfalls: causes transient bradycardia (first‑dose) and can provoke life‑threatening hyperkalaemia in burns/crush/denervation — exam trap to avoid sux in these contexts.
- Capnography: recognise normal four‑phase waveform; “shark‑fin” = bronchospasm/obstruction; sudden ETCO2 rise = ROSC; sudden fall = disconnection or loss of circulation.
- Tracheostomy exam sequence: speaking valve off → inner cannula out → try suction → deflate cuff → remove tube if obstruction persists. Failure to follow sequence is common pitfall.
- Right mainstem intubation: if unilateral breath sounds post‑intubation, withdraw the ETT ~2 cm and reassess (don’t immediately extubate).
- Massive PE arrest: thrombolysis during CPR is indicated when PE is strongly suspected; expect prolonged CPR (≥60–90 min) — often tested.
- Verify calcium and other drug concentrations/volumes locally — exam banks vary; when asked state preparation (gluconate vs chloride) if specified.
- IO insertion absolute contraindication: fracture of target bone — common exam fact.
- APNOEA ventilator alarms during transfer are frequently connection/ETCO2 problems — “disconnect & bag” is standard immediate response.

خلاصة بالعربية: حيل الامتحان: اشجع السعال الفعال، لا تعتمد على SpO2 لحديثي الولادة في التقييم الأولي، احذر سوكس مع الحروق/التحلل، اتبع ترتيب خوارزمية التراشي، واستخدم ETCO2 لتقييم جودة الـCPR وROSC.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (موجز سريع بالعربية)
- المبادئ: مجرى الهواء أولاً ثم التنفّس والدورة الدموية؛ عالج الأسباب القابلة للعكس فورًا (الصمة الرئوية، التوتر الرئوي، التامبوناد، نقص السكر، اضطراب الإلكتروليت).  
- الاختناق: سعال فعال→ شجعه؛ سعال غير فعال→ 5 صفعات ظهر ثم 5 ضغطات بطنية؛ فقدان الوعي→ ابدأ CPR.  
- بعد ROSC: هدف MAP ≥65 ممHg؛ ECG وفحص الأسباب؛ القسطرة التاجية ليست روتينية إلا بوجود دليل إقفاري.  
- صمة رئوية كبرى: ثرومبوليز فورى إن كان المريض غير مستقر واللا مانع; استمر في الإنعاش لفترة طويلة بعد العلاج.  
- جهاز التنفّس/التنبيب: إذا هبوط الأكسجة فجأة افصل الجهاز وابدأ كبس كيس يدوي وقيّم الأنبوب والطنين/انفجارات الرئة (pneumothorax).  
- ترشستومي: أزل الصمام/الأنبوب الداخلي وجرب الشفط وأزل الكفة؛ إذا انسداد استمر—أزل أنبوب التراشي وأمّن التهوية.  
- حديثي الولادة: التنفّس أولًا (5 نفخات)، HR <60 بعد 30 ثانية تهوية فعالة → ضغطات صدرية 3:1 + أدرينالين 20 µg/kg IV عبر الحبل السري إن أمكن.  
- جرعات مهمة: ماغنيسيوم 2–4 غ لTorsades؛ أدرينالين حديثي الولادة 20 µg/kg IV؛ نورأدرينالين لصدمة إنتانية (هادفو MAP≥65).  
- تحقّق دائمًا من تحضيرات/تركيزات الأدوية محليًا (Ca2+، K+، ماغنيسيوم).  

8. Question coverage map (sources / Q‑numbers / concepts covered)
- Q503–Q505: malignant hyperthermia signs, suxamethonium effects and contraindications (bradycardia, hyperkalaemia risk).
- Q506, Q509, Q520: hyperkalaemia and calcium dosing issues; urgent membrane stabilisation and downstream therapies.
- Q507, Q526: refractory VF/pVT, defibrillation strategy, escalation and eCPR/PCI considerations.
- Q510: intraosseous access contraindications (fracture).
- Q511: Allen’s test for radial cannulation.
- Q512: hypothermia recognition, Osborn waves, ECLS transfer thresholds.
- Q513, Q515, Q525: ETCO2 interpretation, ventilator alarms (APNOEA/high pressure) and immediate responses (disconnect & bag).
- Q516, Q521, RCEM tracheostomy material: tracheostomy emergency (green algorithm) sequence and management.
- Q518: septic shock fluid resuscitation and vasopressor bridging (noradrenaline target MAP ≥65).
- Q520: hyperkalaemia algorithm (calcium, insulin+dextrose, nebulised salbutamol).
- Q522–Q524, Q504, Q523: neonatal resuscitation (inflation breaths, 3:1 compressions, preterm care) and NLS 2021 points.
- Q526, Q527: airway confirmation (capnography), failed intubation strategies (SAD, surgical airway) and DAS guidance.
- Q503–Q527 (general): drowning, post‑intubation desaturation, chest decompression in tension pneumothorax, massive PE thrombolysis, perimortem hysterectomy timing, MAP targets post‑ROSC, magnesium for torsades, hypothermia algorithms, practical ventilatory rates during paediatric CPR.

Sources to verify
RCEM; Resuscitation Council (UK); European Resuscitation Council (ERC); American Heart Association (AHA); National Institute for Health and Care Excellence (NICE); Difficult Airway Society (DAS); Neonatal Life Support / PALS (2021).


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 10: SLO7 - DEAL WITH COMPLEX OR CHALLENGING SITUATIONS IN THE WORKPLACE
============================================================================================

Chapter 10 — SLO7: Deal with complex or challenging situations in the workplace

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Systems safety > individual blame: recognise that iatrogenic and organisational failures (e.g. wrong gas connections) are system events requiring immediate patient stabilisation, remediation and formal reporting (PSIRF; never‑event pathways). [PSIRF]  
- Legal/ethical interfaces: confidentiality has lawful exceptions (e.g. name/address disclosure for alleged driving offences; terrorism, court order, public protection) — most other disclosures require legal basis or patient consent.  
- Organ donation is a systems pathway triggered by catastrophic brain injury/brainstem death; SNOD coordinates clinical assessment, registry check and family approach. [NHS Organ Donation]  
- Safeguarding: domestic abuse and child protection are common complex presentations — pathophysiology is social/forensic rather than biological but causes repeated physical/psychological injury requiring multidisciplinary response.  
- Capacity/consent: decision‑specific capacity (Mental Capacity Act) — children assessed by Gillick competence; incapacity (reduced GCS, intoxication, severe mental disorder) obliges best‑interests treatment.  
- Forensic considerations: samples and evidence chain require FHCP involvement and lawful authority; do not bypass patient competence. [BMA/FFLM 2023]  
- Major incident principles: scene safety, command/control and early declaration (METHANE) precede detailed clinical care.

Arabic: التركيز على سلامة النظام أهم من اللوم الفردي؛ هناك استثناءات قانونية للسرية؛ عند إصابة دماغية قاتلة اتصل بـ SNOD؛ حماية الأطفال والعنف المنزلي مسؤولية متعددة التخصصات؛ قيّم الأهلية قراراً بقرار؛ لا تأخذ عينات جنائية دون صلاحية؛ أعلن الحادث الكبير وقدم تقرير METHANE.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Immediate safety/red flags:
  - Sudden hypoxaemia or unexplained deterioration after equipment change → suspect oxygen/equipment misconnection.  
  - Multiple casualties, hazards, access compromise, or overwhelmed resources → consider major incident and METHANE report.  
  - Reduced consciousness (GCS ↓), severe intoxication, incoherent answers → probable lack of capacity.  
  - Injuries of different ages, evasive answers, partner‑presence blockade, threats/weapon mention → suspect domestic abuse; always ask about children.  
  - Intubated/unstable patient with suspected RTC involvement → police may seek name/address only for driving offences; do not volunteer clinical info.  
  - Neurological exam compatible with brainstem death or catastrophic brain injury → trigger organ donation pathway; contact SNOD early. [FICM 2021; NHS Organ Donation]  
  - Pregnant suspected internal drug trafficker (body packing) → observe and image selectively; intervene only for clinical indications. [RCEM 2020]

Arabic: راقب نقص التأكسج المفاجئ، الحوادث الكبرى، انخفاض الوعي، دلائل العنف المنزلي، طلبات الشرطة، الاشتباه في موت دماغي، والناقل الداخلي للمخدرات في الحامل.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate priorities: ABCs, stabilise life‑threatening physiology, treat hypoxaemia before investigating equipment/connection errors.  
- Equipment misconnection: correct supply, reassess, monitor SpO2 and observations; involve biomedical/senior staff and document sequence. Report via PSIRF. [PSIRF]  
- Capacity assessment (adult): (1) Define specific decision; (2) Provide info and make reasonable adjustments; (3) Check understanding, retention, weighing, and ability to communicate choice. If unable → treat in best interests. For <16 apply Gillick competence.  
- Police disclosure checklist: confirm officer identity/authority and reason for request; lawful minimal disclosure: name/address for alleged driver; otherwise consult Caldicott guardian/MDU/legal.  
- Organ donation screening: on suspected brainstem death: contact SNOD; check registry, residency (local rules), active absolute contraindications (verify current NHS list), infectious risk; SNOD leads family approach and donor suitability assessment. [NHS Organ Donation; FICM 2021]  
- Forensic samples: if patient competent and refuses → do not take; advise police to arrange FHCP. If incapacitated follow legal/FFLM/BMA guidance for lawful sampling. [BMA/FFLM 2023]  
- Domestic abuse assessment: private, non‑judgemental history; document injuries with body maps/photographs if consented; ask about children; assess immediate risk and threshold for MARAC/IDVA referral.  
- Toxicology/overdose: use standard tools—e.g. paracetamol single acute ingestion assessed by Rumack‑Matthew nomogram and local NAC protocol. Do not invent thresholds beyond local guidance.

Arabic: حافظ على ABC أولاً؛ أصلح الأخطاء بالأجهزة وسجّلها؛ قيّم الأهلية قراراً بقرار (أطفال ≡ غيليك)؛ تحقق من قانونية طلبات الشرطة؛ عند موت دماغي اتصل بـ SNOD وافحص السجل والموانع؛ عينات جنائية فقط عبر FHCP إن رفض المريض؛ للعنف المنزلي دوّن الإصابات واطلب عن الأطفال؛ استعمل منحنى Rumack لمعالجة الأسيتامينوفين.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- Oxygen/equipment misconnection (one‑line algorithm): restore correct gas → immediate ABCs and monitoring → senior/biomed escalation → document events and remediate → report under PSIRF/never‑event pathway. [PSIRF]  
- Police request algorithm: verify officer and lawful basis → if alleged driving offence give name/address only (no clinical details) → if other request or doubt consult Caldicott/MDU/legal → document disclosure decision.  
- Catastrophic brain injury / organ donation algorithm: stabilise & neuro‑support → neurosurgery/ICU review → consider brainstem death testing per local protocol → contact SNOD early → SNOD checks registry/residency/contraindications → SNOD leads family discussion and donation logistics. [FICM 2021; NHS Organ Donation]  
- Domestic abuse / safeguarding algorithm: see patient privately → assess immediate danger (weapons/threats? yes → call police) → document injuries/timeframe → ask about children and immediate child safety → offer IDVA/MARAC referral if high risk → arrange follow‑up and safeguarding referral.  
- Capacity/consent algorithm (acute): is patient oriented & decision‑specific capable? (Yes → respect choice) (No → treat in best interests; for minors consider parental consent if appropriate; for <16 assess Gillick). Emergently incapacitated patients may be treated without consent to prevent harm.  
- Forensic sampling algorithm: competent refusal → STOP; advise FHCP; incapacitated with lawful grounds → follow FFLM/BMA procedures for sampling and documentation; maintain chain‑of‑custody. [BMA/FFLM 2023]  
- Jehovah’s Witness / transfusion refusal: confirm competence/advance directive → discuss alternatives (cell salvage, topical haemostats, IV iron/EPO where appropriate) → recognise alternatives are slower — for life‑threatening haemorrhage document discussions, escalate senior/legal, and follow local policies.

Arabic: خوارزميات الطوارئ: أصلح إمداد الأكسجين وبلغ؛ للتحقيقات الشرطية أعطِ الاسم/العنوان فقط إن كان السائق المشتبه؛ عند إصابة دماغية قاتلة اتصل بـ SNOD؛ للعنف المنزلي اسأل خصوصاً عن الأطفال واطلب الشرطة إذا خطر فوري؛ عينات جنائية عبر FHCP أو طبق إجراءات FFLM؛ اشرح رفض نقل الدم وقدم بدائل ودوّن النقاش.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- Drugs/antidotes:
  - Paracetamol overdose → use Rumack‑Matthew nomogram and give N‑acetylcysteine per local protocol (follow toxicology guidance).  
  - Naloxone for opioid overdose as per standard resuscitation dosing (follow local/advanced life support protocols).  
- Procedures:
  - For suspected body packing: imaging (per local protocol) only if clinical deterioration or to confirm packing; avoid coercive invasive procedures without clinical need. [RCEM 2020]  
  - Brainstem death testing and donor management follow ICU/local protocols; SNOD coordinates organ preservation steps. [FICM 2021]  
  - Forensic sampling should be performed by FHCPs; if staff must collect, follow chain‑of‑custody and FFLM/BMA guidance. [BMA/FFLM 2023]  
- Disposition:
  - Immediate admission to HDU/ICU for catastrophic brain injury, airway compromise, major haemorrhage, or if required for forensic medical examination.  
  - Safe discharge considered only if no ongoing safety risk (domestic abuse planning in place; capacity intact; arrangements for follow‑up and safeguarding).  
- Follow‑up:
  - Safeguarding referrals, IDVA/MARAC, GP and community services as indicated.  
  - Governance reporting: PSIRF / local incident reporting for system errors; document contemporaneously and escalate to senior/governance.

Arabic: الأدوية والإجراءات: استخدم منحنى Rumack وNAC للأسيتامينوفين؛ ناكسون عند جرعات الأفيون؛ تفحّص الناقلين بالمخدرات بالتصوير عند الحاجة؛ اختبارات موت الدماغ وإجراءات الحفظ بقيادة ICU/SNOD؛ العينات الجنائية حسب FFLM/BMA؛ ضع المريض في HDU/ICU إذا ضروري؛ أرسل تحويلات حماية ومتابعة للمجتمع ودوّن الحوادث للإدارة.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Always report obvious system failures — never choose “do not report” for oxygen misconnection/never‑events. [PSIRF]  
- Police disclosure traps: exam vignettes often test the driver exception — name/address must be given for alleged driving offences; do not release clinical details without clear lawful basis.  
- Organ donation traps: don’t over‑simplify family “veto” — family views matter operationally but clinicians must follow SNOD/legal guidance; verify current NHS criteria for exclusions (policies change). [NHS Organ Donation]  
- Domestic abuse Qs: always ask “are there children?”; immediate police contact is required if weapons/threats or immediate danger. MARAC is for high‑risk referrals.  
- Capacity in minors: apply Gillick competence — it’s decision‑specific. Reduced GCS/intoxication → lacks capacity.  
- Forensic sampling: clinical staff should avoid collecting forensic samples from competent refusers — request FHCP. Do not mix clinical care and forensic evidence collection without training. [BMA/FFLM 2023]  
- Jehovah’s Witness: alternatives (IV iron/EPO) are slow — do not present them as immediate substitutes in major haemorrhage. Document discussions and escalate.  
- Major incident questions: the tested sequence is declaration + METHANE → command → triage/treatment; don’t start detailed individual resource planning before declaring.

Arabic: نصائح للامتحان: بلّغ الأخطاء النظامية؛ للشرطة أعطِ الاسم/العنوان فقط إن تعلق الأمر بالقيادة؛ تحقق من سياسات التبرع بالأعضاء؛ في العنف المنزلي اسأل عن الأطفال؛ غيليك قرارية؛ العينات الجنائية عبر FHCP؛ بدائل نقل الدم بطيئة؛ أعلن الحادث وقدم METHANE أولاً.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary
- أصلح المخاطر الحرجة فوراً (أكسجين/أجهزة) وبلغ عبر PSIRF.  
- السرية لها استثناءات محددة — اسم/عنوان فقط للسائق المشتبه؛ استشر قانونياً في غير ذلك.  
- عند موت دماغي اتصل بـ SNOD، افحص السجل وناقش العائلة بمرافقة SNOD.  
- اسأل عن الأطفال في حالات العنف المنزلي؛ اتصل بالشرطة إذا خطر فوري؛ استخدم MARAC/IDVA.  
- قيم الأهلية قراراً بقرار (غيليك للأطفال)؛ إن فقدت الأهلية عالج بمصلحة المريض.  
- لا تاخذ عينات جنائية من مريض رافض إن كان قادراً؛ اطلب FHCP وإجراءات FFLM/BMA.  
- أعلن الحادث الكبير وقدم تقرير METHANE قبل تفاصيل الإدارة السريرية.

Arabic rapid review: أصلح أخطاء النظام فوراً وأبلغ؛ للشرطة أعطِ الاسم/العنوان فقط؛ اتصل بـ SNOD عند موت دماغي؛ حماية الأطفال أولوية؛ عينات جنائية عبر FHCP؛ أعلن الحادث وقدم METHANE.

8. Question coverage map (source concepts covered)
- PSIRF / patient safety incidents: oxygen/equipment misconnection; never‑events; incident reporting and remediation. [PSIRF]  
- RCEM (2020): management and assessment of suspected internal drug trafficker (body packing) in pregnancy and adults. [RCEM 2020]  
- FICM (2021) / organ donation: catastrophic brain injury, brainstem death pathway, SNOD contact, donor assessment and family discussion. [FICM 2021; NHS Organ Donation]  
- BMA / FFLM (2023): forensic blood sampling, consent, incapacitated patients, chain‑of‑custody requirements. [BMA/FFLM 2023]  
- Safeguarding/domestic abuse: identification, documentation, IDVA/MARAC referral, child protection triggers.  
- Capacity/Gillick: adult capacity algorithm; minors’ consent; best‑interests decisions.  
- Police disclosure/legal exceptions: disclosure of name/address for alleged driving offences; when to consult legal/MDU/Caldicott.  
- Major incident: declaration, METHANE, command and triage sequencing.  
- Clinical toxicology basics: paracetamol overdose assessment (Rumack‑Matthew) and NAC use.  
- Jehovah’s Witness / transfusion refusal: consent issues, alternatives, documentation.

Sources to verify
RCEM, Faculty of Intensive Care Medicine (FICM), Patient Safety Incident Response Framework (PSIRF) / NHS England, NHS Organ Donation, BMA, FFLM, Medical Defence Union guidance, ERC, NICE (where applicable).


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 11: SEXUAL HEALTH
============================================================================================

Chapter 11 — Sexual Health

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core syndromes in ED: urethritis/cervicitis (Neisseria gonorrhoeae, Chlamydia trachomatis), pelvic inflammatory disease (PID), trichomoniasis, genital/oral herpes simplex (HSV‑1/2), acute HIV seroconversion, consequences of sexual assault (trauma, pregnancy risk, STI risk, forensic needs).  
- Pathophysiology essentials: ascending infection → endometritis/adnexal inflammation/TOA in PID (C. trachomatis, N. gonorrhoeae ± anaerobes); urethral epithelial infection causes dysuria/discharge; trichomonas = protozoan vaginitis; HSV causes mucocutaneous vesicles→ulcers with neural latency; acute HIV — systemic viraemia before seroconversion.  
- ED priorities: ABCs, pain control, pregnancy test, limit harm (emergency contraception + HIV PEP assessment), preserve evidence, start empiric therapy when indicated, arrange SARC/SANE and sexual‑health follow‑up. [NICE CKS; RCEM]

ملخص عربي: المتلازمات الشائعة: التهابات الإحليل وعنق الرحم، PID، تريكوموناس، هربس، عدوى HIV الحادة، تبعات الاعتداء الجنسي. أولويات الطوارئ: استقرار الحالة، اختبار حمل، منع الحمل الطارئ/PEP للحماية، الحفاظ على الأدلة، وإحالة لمراكز متخصصة. [NICE CKS; RCEM]

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- PID: clinical diagnosis — lower abdominal pain + cervical motion tenderness (CMT) ± uterine/adnexal tenderness, purulent discharge, fever. Differentials: ectopic pregnancy, appendicitis, ovarian torsion, UTI, Mittelschmerz. Red flags requiring admission/imaging: high fever, peritonitis, vomiting, suspected TOA, pregnancy, inability to tolerate oral therapy. [NICE CKS]  
- Urethritis / epididymo‑orchitis (men): dysuria, urethral discharge, scrotal/testicular pain — think gonorrhoea, chlamydia; consider urinary causes and torsion (acute severe pain).  
- Genital ulcers: painful grouped vesicles/ulcers → HSV (primary more systemic); painless single ulcer → syphilis differential.  
- Trichomoniasis: frothy green‑yellow discharge, vulvovaginal itch; wet mount shows motile flagellates.  
- Acute HIV seroconversion: 2–4 weeks after exposure — fever, sore throat, lymphadenopathy, maculopapular rash; early Ab tests may be negative — consider RNA if high suspicion.  
- Sexual assault: assess for acute life‑threats, pregnancy risk, STI exposure, and need for forensic collection. Do not undertake forensic sampling unless trained; prioritise medical care and referral. [RCEM]

ملخص عربي: PID = ألم أسفل البطن + حساسية حركة عنق الرحم؛ احذر الحمل خارج الرحم، التواء المبيض، وخراج المبيض. الهربس ألماني ومجموعة من الفقاعات؛ HIV الحاد قد يعطي أشباه أمراض فيروسية قبل إيجابية الاختبار. عند الاعتداء الجنسي: لا تجري فحصًا جنائيًا دون تدريب، قدّم الرعاية الطارئة وأحِل للـSARC. [NICE CKS; RCEM]

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Baseline ED tests: pregnancy test (urine/serum) for all reproductive‑age females with pelvic/genital complaints; urine/first‑void NAAT for chlamydia/gonorrhoea; genital swabs (endocervical/high‑vaginal/prescription site) for NAAT/culture; wet mount microscopy for trichomonas/clue cells/yeast; HSV PCR from lesion swab; HIV 4th‑generation Ag/Ab as baseline — consider HIV RNA PCR if very early suspected infection. Send gonococcal culture when possible for sensitivity. [NICE CKS]  
- Imaging: pelvic/transvaginal ultrasound when ectopic pregnancy, tubo‑ovarian abscess (TOA), or torsion suspected. TOA usually needs admission and IV therapy/intervention. [NICE CKS]  
- Interpretation caveats: negative 4th‑gen HIV does not exclude acute infection in the first days — use RNA if indicated; NAAT from first‑void urine is reliable for urethral/C. trachomatis detection; wet mount sensitivity varies. Forensic evidence windows vary by exposure/site — local forensic guidance required. [RCEM]

ملخص عربي: اختبارات أساسية: اختبار حمل، NAAT لعقد الكلاميديا/السيلان، مسحة HSV PCR، اختبار HIV رباعي الجيل (إذا اشتباه مبكر استخدم RNA). التصوير عبر السونار عند اشتباه خراج أو حمل خارج الرحم. احذر أن سلبية اختبار HIV المبكر لا تنفي العدوى الحادة. [NICE CKS; RCEM]

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. PID decision algorithm
- Suspected PID → pregnancy test + assess severity.  
  - If mild–moderate, tolerating oral, reliable follow‑up → outpatient therapy: ceftriaxone 1 g IM once + doxycycline 100 mg PO bd ×14 days + metronidazole 400–500 mg PO bd ×14 days. Arrange sexual‑health follow‑up and advise abstinence until completed treatment. [NICE CKS]  
  - Admit if: TOA on imaging, pregnancy, severe systemic illness/peritonitis, vomiting/inability to take oral, high fever, or clinical deterioration. Inpatient: IV cephalosporin ± doxycycline ± metronidazole; surgical/IR input for abscess drainage. [NICE CKS]

B. Urethritis / chlamydia
- Empiric if clinical features: doxycycline 100 mg PO bd ×7 days (first‑line) and NAAT. Partner notification and treatment. [BASHH]

C. Gonorrhoea
- Empiric parenteral therapy per current local guidance (commonly ceftriaxone IM single dose — check local dose). Send NAAT + culture for sensitivity; treat partners. [BASHH/local guidance]

D. Trichomoniasis
- Metronidazole 2 g PO single dose (or 400–500 mg PO bd ×7 days). Treat partner. [NICE CKS]

E. HSV (genital/oral)
- Symptomatic therapy: oral aciclovir (e.g., 200 mg five times daily for ~5 days or 400 mg TDS for 5 days). Severe or immunocompromised → IV aciclovir (specialist-led). [BNF/NICE]

F. Acute HIV exposure and PEP
- Assess exposure risk, time since exposure (PEP indicated ≤72 hours for significant risk) and baseline tests. Start local PEP protocol promptly if indicated and arrange urgent specialist follow‑up. [local/RCEM]

G. Sexual assault immediate bundle
- ABCs → analgesia → pregnancy test → offer emergency contraception (copper IUD most effective ≤5 days; ulipristal 30 mg ≤120 h; levonorgestrel 1.5 mg ≤72 h) → assess HIV PEP eligibility (≤72 h) → offer STI prophylaxis per local protocol → hepatitis B vaccination/IG if indicated → preserve evidence (avoid washing/urination if possible), document, contact SARC/SANE, arrange forensic exam by trained staff. [RCEM; NICE CKS]

ملخص عربي: خوارزميات رئيسية: PID → قرر دخول المستشفى أم علاج عن طريق الفم (سيفترياكسون IM + دوكسيسيكلين + ميترونيدازول). الكلاميديا = دوكسيسيكلين 7 أيام؛ تريكوموناس = ميترونيدازول؛ الهربس = أسيكلوفير؛ PEP للـHIV في خلال 72 ساعة إذا مؤهل؛ اعتداء جنسي = حزمة طوارئ شاملة وإحالة متخصصة. [NICE CKS; RCEM]

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Key drug regimens (well established in guidance):  
  - PID outpatient: ceftriaxone 1 g IM once + doxycycline 100 mg PO bd ×14 days + metronidazole 400–500 mg PO bd ×14 days. [NICE CKS]  
  - Chlamydia: doxycycline 100 mg PO bd ×7 days. [BASHH]  
  - Gonorrhoea: ceftriaxone IM single dose (confirm current local dose). [BASHH/local guidance]  
  - Trichomonas: metronidazole 2 g PO single dose or 400–500 mg PO bd ×7 days. [NICE CKS]  
  - HSV: aciclovir oral regimens (e.g., 200 mg five times daily ×≈5 days). [BNF]  
  - Emergency contraception: copper IUD (best ≤5 days), ulipristal 30 mg ≤120 h, levonorgestrel 1.5 mg ≤72 h. [NICE CKS]  
  - HIV PEP: follow local protocol; begin ≤72 h when indicated and arrange prompt specialist review. [local/RCEM]

- Procedures and practical actions: pregnancy testing; collect appropriate NAAT/swabs; send gonococcal culture for sensitivities; transvaginal ultrasound for TOA/ectopic/torsion; do not perform forensic sampling unless trained — preserve clothing and avoid washing when possible; provide analgesia and wound care for genital trauma; consider Hepatitis B vaccination/IG if indicated. [RCEM; NICE CKS]

- Disposition: admit for TOA, pregnancy, severe systemic illness, peritonitis, inability to tolerate oral, suspected ectopic, or failed outpatient therapy. Discharge with written advice, partner notification instructions, arranged sexual‑health follow‑up, and clear return advice for worsening features. [NICE CKS]

- Follow‑up: arrange sexual health clinic review for test results, partner notification, counsel for safer sex, further HIV testing per window periods and local protocol, specialist input for recurrent/complicated infections. Test‑of‑cure and timing vary by organism and local guidance—follow local recommendations. [BASHH; local guidance]

ملخص عربي: الأدوية الأساسية مذكورة أعلاه؛ إجراءات مهمة: فحوص الحمل، مسحات NAAT، زرع للسيلان، سونار للحوض، حفاظ على الأدلة عند الاعتداء، تطعيم التهاب الكبد B إذا لزم. الإحالة للمراكز المتخصصة والمتابعة ضرورية. [NICE CKS; RCEM; BASHH]

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- PID “triad” for exam stems: lower abdominal pain + cervical motion tenderness (CMT) ± adnexal tenderness — if present, treat empirically while excluding ectopic pregnancy. (Exam shorthand: CMT = cervical motion tenderness.) [NICE CKS]  
- HIV testing trap: a negative 4th‑gen test early after exposure does not exclude acute infection — consider RNA PCR if classic seroconversion syndrome.  
- HSV trick: primary episodes are systemic and longer; recurrent episodes are milder. Don’t mistake aphthous ulcers (painful but non‑viral) for HSV — HSV ulcers usually follow vesicles.  
- Discharge vs admission pitfalls: do not discharge a woman with suspected TOA, high fever, peritonitis signs, pregnancy, or inability to tolerate oral meds.  
- Drug resistance caveat: azithromycin is no longer preferred first‑line for chlamydia in many updated guidelines — many exam stems still quote older regimens; check question date/context. Always check current gonorrhoea dosing in local guidance (may vary). [BASHH]  
- Forensic pitfalls: do not collect forensic samples unless trained; do not delay urgent medical care for evidence; document consent and findings precisely.  
- Microbiology interpretation: wet mount sensitivity is operator‑dependent; negative microscopy does not exclude trichomonas — rely on NAAT when available.

ملخص عربي: نقاط للامتحان: CMT = علامة PID مهمة؛ الاختبارات المبكرة قد تعطي سلبية مزيفة للـHIV؛ تجنّب استخدام الأزيثرومايسين كخيار افتراضي دون مراجعة الإرشادات الحديثة؛ لا تفعل فحصًا جنائيًا دون تدريب. [NICE CKS; BASHH; RCEM]

7. Arabic rapid-review summary
- أولويات الطوارئ: ABC، اختبار حمل، تخفيف ألم، منع الحمل الطارئ، تقدير حاجة PEP للـHIV، حفظ الأدلة، وإحالة لمراكز الطوارئ الجنسي/الاعتداء.  
- PID: تشخيص سريري (ألم أسفل البطن + CMT)؛ علاج خارجي: سيفترياكسون IM + دوكسيسيكلين 100 mg bd ×14 يوماً + ميترونيدازول ×14 يوماً؛ ادخل للمستشفى إذا خراج أو حالة شديدة.  
- الكلاميديا: دوكسيسيكلين 100 mg bd ×7 أيام. السيلان: سيفترياكسون IM (راجع الجرعة المحلية). تريكوموناس: ميترونيدازول 2 g جرعة واحدة. الهربس: أسيكلوفير فموي.  
- عند الاعتداء الجنسي: حافظ على الأدلة، عرض PEP خلال 72 ساعة إذا مؤهل، اعرض وسائل منع الحمل الطارئة، أحِل إلى SARC/SANE والمتابعة.  
- تحقّق من التوجيهات المحلية والوطنية حول مقاومة الميكروبات ونوافذ الأدلة الجنائية.

الملخص العربي المختصر أعلاه يغطي الأدوية الأساسية والإجراءات الطارئة. [NICE CKS; RCEM]

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Concepts covered (source Q‑numbers not supplied):  
  - PID diagnosis, management, outpatient vs inpatient criteria, TOA.  
  - Urethritis/epididymo‑orchitis (gonorrhoea, chlamydia) diagnosis and first‑line therapy.  
  - Gonorrhoea — NAAT and culture, empiric parenteral therapy and resistance caveats.  
  - Chlamydia — doxycycline regimen.  
  - Trichomoniasis — clinical features and metronidazole regimens.  
  - Genital/oral HSV — recognition, aciclovir dosing, primary vs recurrent.  
  - Acute HIV seroconversion and testing windows; HIV PEP assessment and timing (≤72 h).  
  - Sexual assault — emergency bundle, evidence preservation, forensic considerations, emergency contraception options.  
  - Diagnostic tests: pregnancy test, NAAT, wet mount, HSV PCR, HIV 4th‑gen and RNA, pelvic ultrasound indications.  
  - Forensic and medicolegal pitfalls and referral pathways (SARC/SANE).  
- Note: specific Q‑number ranges were not supplied with the chunk; map lists concepts likely tested across typical FRCEM question banks.

Sources to verify
RCEM; NICE CKS; BASHH; BNF; local public health / sexual‑health guidance; SARC/SANE protocols; ERC for general emergency practice.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 12: RESPIRATORY
============================================================================================

Chapter 12 — Respiratory

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core pattern recognition: respiratory emergencies cluster around
  - Hypoxaemia (failure of oxygenation; low SpO2),
  - Hypercapnia (ventilatory failure; rising PaCO2 ± acidosis),
  - Airway compromise (obstruction, reduced effort, aspiration),
  - Pleural complications (pneumothorax, effusion, tension physiology).
- Clinical approach principle: triage → stabilise physiology (ABC, targeted O2) → focused bedside tests (SpO2, PEFR, ABG) → imaging/definitive therapy.
- Physiology keys:
  - V/Q mismatch → hypoxaemia (PE, consolidation, pleural effusion).
  - Shunt → refractory hypoxaemia not corrected by supplemental O2.
  - Hypoventilation → hypercapnia (neuromuscular disease, sedative overdose, severe COPD).
  - Dynamic hyperinflation / auto‑PEEP → obstructive disease complications (asthma/COPD) and risk during ventilation.
- Use validated pathways: BTS for pleural disease/NIV, NICE/BNF for asthma/COPD, CURB‑65 for CAP severity.

Arabic summary (مختصر بالعربية):
- الطوارئ التنفسية تتمركز حول: نقص التأكسج، فشل التهوية، انسداد المجارى الهوائية ومضاعفات الجنب. مبدأ العمل: فرز → تثبيت فيزيولوجي → فحوص سريعة → علاج موجه.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Rapid red flags (require immediate action):
  - Respiratory arrest, unresponsive, severe hypotension, silent chest (asthma), rising/normalising PaCO2 in severe asthma, SpO2 <90% refractory to O2 (condition‑dependent), tension pneumothorax signs (tracheal deviation, severe hypotension), haemodynamic shock with suspected PE.
- Asthma severity (practical thresholds):
  - Moderate: PEFR 50–75% predicted/best; speaks in sentences; RR <25; HR <110.
  - Severe: PEFR <50% (eg ~40%); RR ≥25; HR ≥110; difficulty speaking.
  - Life‑threatening: SpO2 <92% (or per local target), silent chest, cyanosis, exhaustion, arrhythmia, hypotension, PaO2 <8 kPa, PaCO2 normalising or rising; PEFR <33% predicted. (NICE/BNF)
- COPD exacerbation: look for baseline hypercapnia, altered consciousness, marked dyspnoea, sputum purulence; hypercapnic acidosis suggests need for NIV consideration.
- Pneumothorax types and features:
  - Primary spontaneous (young, tall, smokers) vs secondary (older, COPD, underlying lung disease).
  - Symptoms: sudden pleuritic pain, unilateral reduced air entry, hyperresonance.
  - High‑risk features (favor intervention): underlying lung disease, age (guidelines commonly cite ≥50 with smoking history), physiological compromise.
- PE suspicion: acute dyspnoea ± pleuritic pain, tachycardia, risk factors (recent immobility, surgery, malignancy, OCP). Use pre‑test probability → D‑dimer/CTPA strategy.
- Pitfalls:
  - Normal SpO2 does not exclude CO poisoning (pulse oximetry unreliable).
  - Normal or rising PaCO2 in severe asthma = impending respiratory failure.
  - “Small” pneumothorax definitions vary by measurement method—check the stem.

Arabic summary (مختصر بالعربية):
- علامات الخطر: الصدر الصامت، PaCO2 مرتفع أو طبيعى في الربو الشديد، نفخ صدري ضاغط، صدمة مع انصمام رئوي مشتبه. قيّم شدة الربو بالـPEFR، واضبط تدخلات النفاخ/الانسداد حسب وجود مرض رئوي أساسي.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate bedside tests: SpO2, PEFR (asthma), ABG if ventilatory failure suspected, ECG (PE/MI), CXR when it will change management (do not delay life‑saving therapy).
- PE pathway (stable patient):
  - Assess stability → estimate pre‑test probability (Wells/Geneva).
  - Low/intermediate → D‑dimer → if positive → CTPA.
  - High pre‑test probability → proceed to CTPA; in major delays consider anticoagulation per local policy. (NICE NG158)
- Pneumothorax:
  - Unstable/tension → immediate decompression (needle/finger), then chest drain.
  - Stable → erect CXR to estimate size; use guideline measurement method (apex or interpleural distance per local/BTS).
  - Consider CT if complicated, recurrent or to guide chest drain.
- Asthma/COPD:
  - Measure PEFR and SpO2 for triage.
  - ABG if severe illness, poor response, decreased consciousness, or before/after NIV/intubation.
- NIV decision and monitoring:
  - Indications: acute hypercapnic respiratory failure with acidosis (COPD) after initial medical therapy; monitor ABG ~1 hour after initiation.
  - Contraindications: inability to protect airway, severe vomiting, facial trauma, respiratory arrest; relative: severe agitation, uncontrolled secretions.
- Cylinder duration quick formula: duration (min) = cylinder volume (L) ÷ flow (L/min). (Example UK CD ≈460 L → 460 ÷ 15 L/min ≈30 min) — verify local cylinder sizes.

Arabic summary (مختصر بالعربية):
- الفحوص السريعة: SpO2 وPEFR وABG. في الاشتباه بالانصمام استخدم تقدير الاحتمال المسبق قبل الـD‑dimer. النفاخ: CXR إذا سيؤثر على الإدارة؛ النفاخ الضاغط لا ينتظر تصويراً.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- General respiratory failure (quick algorithm):
  1. ABC: secure airway, high‑flow O2 as indicated, treat shock.
  2. Targeted SpO2: COPD 88–92% (unless other instruction); otherwise aim for normalisation as clinical context.
  3. ABG if ventilatory failure suspected or severe disease.
  4. Treat cause: bronchodilators/steroids (asthma), NIV/intubation (ventilatory failure), chest drain/aspiration (pneumothorax), antibiotics (CAP), thrombolysis (massive PE).
- Acute severe asthma (triage → treatment ↑ escalation):
  1. Immediate: high‑flow inhaled β2 agonist (nebuliser or MDI+spacer), supplemental O2 to target (see above), systemic corticosteroid early.
  2. Add ipratropium for severe attacks; give frequent β2 agonist dosing.
  3. If poor response → IV magnesium sulphate 2 g over 20 min.
  4. If life‑threatening (silent chest, rising PaCO2, exhaustion) → prepare for intubation; ventilate with low Vt (~6 ml/kg), low RR, long expiratory time (I:E ≈1:4), consider neuromuscular blockade.
  5. Consider IV salbutamol bolus (commonly 250 µg over 5–10 min) if inhaled therapy inadequate (verify local protocol).
- Pneumothorax decision tree (simplified):
  - Stable small PSP, minimal symptoms → conservative outpatient observation and repeat imaging (48–72 h or local timeframe).
  - Symptomatic or moderate PSP seeking rapid relief → attempt needle aspiration.
  - Failed aspiration OR secondary pneumothorax OR large/physiologically compromised → insert small‑bore Seldinger chest drain (commonly 10–14 Fr per BTS).
  - Tension pneumothorax → immediate needle decompression then chest drain.
  - Post‑drain monitoring: immediate, 15 min, then every 30 min for 1 hour, then 4‑hourly checks early on. (BTS pleural procedures)
- NIV initiation and escalation (COPD/hypercapnic failure):
  1. Indication: hypercapnic acidosis after initial medical therapy (use local pH cutoffs).
  2. Initial settings: IPAP ≈15 cmH2O / EPAP ≈3 cmH2O; increase IPAP to improve ventilation; adjust EPAP to improve oxygenation.
  3. Reassess clinically and by ABG ~1 hour; if no improvement consider increasing IPAP (do not exceed IPAP ~30 cmH2O without specialist input) or escalate to invasive ventilation.
  4. Contraindications/relative contraindications as above. (BTS NIV guidance)
- PE emergencies:
  - Haemodynamically unstable with suspected massive PE → resuscitate and consider immediate systemic thrombolysis if no contra‑indication.
  - Stable patients follow pre‑test stratification → imaging/anticoagulate per results.

Arabic summary (مختصر بالعربية):
- خوارزمية عامة: ABC ثم أهداف تشبع مؤقتة (COPD 88–92%) → ABG إن اشتبه بفشل تهوئي → علاج السبب (ربو: بروجنيستات+كورتيزون+ماغنسيوم، التهاب رئوي: مضادات حيوية، نفاخ: تفريغ/درين).  
- NIV: IPAP بدءياً ~15 / EPAP ~3؛ زِدِ IPAP لتحسين إزالة CO2؛ راجع بعد ساعة؛ لا تتجاوز ~30 دون خبرة.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Key drugs and emergency doses (verify local policy):
  - Oxygen: target sats by condition (COPD 88–92%; others higher as clinically indicated).
  - Inhaled β2 agonists: frequent/nebulised or MDI+spacer.
  - Ipratropium: add in severe asthma exacerbations.
  - Systemic corticosteroids: give early in asthma and COPD exacerbations (route/dose per local guideline).
  - IV magnesium sulphate: 2 g over 20 min for life‑threatening asthma.
  - IV salbutamol bolus: commonly 250 µg over 5–10 min when inhaled therapy inadequate (check local guidance).
  - Antibiotics for CAP: guided by CURB‑65; severe (CURB‑65 ≥3) → hospitalisation and IV therapy (eg co‑amoxiclav ± macrolide per NICE).
  - Anticoagulation/thrombolysis: follow risk/benefit and NICE/locally agreed pathways for PE.
- Procedures:
  - Needle aspiration for PSP (first‑line for symptomatic primary in many guidelines).
  - Small‑bore Seldinger chest drain commonly 10–14 Fr for chest drainage (BTS guidance — confirm local practice).
  - Urgent needle decompression for tension pneumothorax followed by chest drain.
  - NIV (mask or helmet per local availability) with reassessment at ~1 hour.
  - Intubation/controlled ventilation for impending respiratory arrest or failed NIV.
- Disposition:
  - ICU/level 2 for: need for invasive ventilation, NIV requiring close monitoring, life‑threatening asthma/near‑fatal events, massive PE requiring thrombolysis.
  - Admit for: secondary pneumothorax, CAP with CURB‑65 ≥2 (local nuance), COPD exacerbation with failure of initial therapy.
  - Consider outpatient follow‑up for small PSP managed conservatively; plan imaging review (CXR/CT) and pneumothorax follow‑up clinic.
- Follow‑up specifics:
  - Pneumothorax: repeat imaging at 48–72 h for conservative care; allow air travel only after radiological resolution plus guideline‑recommended interval (commonly cited: 7 days after complete resolution; check BTS).
  - COPD/asthma: arrange primary care/pulmonary follow‑up, inhaler review, smoking cessation, vaccination as per guidance.
  - After PE: anticoagulation follow‑up, thrombophilia only if indicated; arrange follow‑up imaging/echo for RV dysfunction if relevant.

Arabic summary (مختصر بالعربية):
- أدوية رئيسية: أكسجين وفق الهدف، موسعات براونكوسجيل، كورتيزون نظامي، ماغنسيوم 2 غ عبر 20 دقيقة، سالبوتامول وريدياً 250 ميكروغرام (تحقق من البروتوكول المحلي).  
- إجراءات: شفط إبرة للـPSP، درين صدر صغير 10–14Fr للثانوي أو الفاشل، تفريغ فوري للضغط. المتابعة بتصوير بعد 48‑72 ساعة أو مكانياً حسب الدليل.

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Read stem for thresholds: age cutoffs and cylinder volumes vary — many questions use ≥65 for CURB‑65 and commonly ≥50 as higher risk for pneumothorax with smoking history. Don’t assume 45 unless stated.
- Use the cylinder formula rather than memorising volumes: duration (min) = cylinder volume (L) ÷ flow (L/min). If a CD = 460 L is given in the stem, use it; otherwise verify.
- D‑dimer only useful if pre‑test probability is low/intermediate; high probability → image directly.
- Silent chest = life‑threatening asthma irrespective of SpO2 readout.
- Rising or normalising PaCO2 in a worsening asthmatic patient is a poor prognostic sign — treat as impending failure.
- Do not delay NIV for CXR in a patient in severe distress; many exam stems test this principle.
- Small‑bore chest drains (10–14 Fr) are commonly first line for PSP; large bore is often a distractor.
- In ventilated severe asthma allow permissive hypercapnia and long expiratory times — increasing RR to normalise PaCO2 causes dynamic hyperinflation.
- Beware of pulse oximetry in carbon monoxide poisoning and of over‑reliance on SpO2 alone.

Arabic summary (مختصر بالعربية):
- خدع الامتحان الشائعة: راجع عمر المريض والحدود، لا تستخدم D‑dimer عند احتمالية ما قبل الفحص عالية، لا تؤخر NIV بفعل تصوير صدر، الصدر الصامت في الربو حالة حرجة، استخدم حساب زمن الأسطوانة لا الحفظ.

7. Arabic rapid-review summary
- فرز سريع: ABC ثم تثبيت فيزيولوجي — تمارين: SpO2، PEFR، ABG.  
- الربو الحاد: استنشاقي فوري + كورتيزون نظامي؛ صدري صامت أو PaCO2 متزايد = تهديد للحياة؛ ماغنسيوم 2 غ في الحالات الشديدة؛ تحضير للتهوية عند فشل العلاج.  
- النفاخ (Pneumothorax): صغير ومستقر → مراقبة؛ معتدل → شفط إبرة؛ ثانوي/كبير/فشل → درين صدر صغير (10–14Fr عادةً)؛ نفاخ ضاغط → تفريغ فوري. تجنب السفر الجوي حتى الشفاء الشعاعي + فترة انتظار موصى بها.  
- COPD وفشل CO2: جرّب NIV مبكراً (IPAP ~15 / EPAP ~3)، راجع بعد ساعة، لا تتردد بالتصعيد إن فشل.  
- الانصمام الرئوي: استخدم تقدير الاحتمال المسبق ثم D‑dimer إن مناسب أو CTPA مباشرة إذا احتمال عالٍ؛ الصدمة بسبب PE قد تتطلب ثرومبوليزا.  
- تحقق محلياً من أحجام الأسطوانات، أحجام الدرين، وحدود IPAP القصوى.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Asthma: acute severity criteria, PEFR thresholds, life‑threatening signs, bronchodilator/steroid/MgSO4 use, IV salbutamol bolus, intubation/ventilation strategies. (stems referencing NICE/BNF)
- COPD: hypercapnic respiratory failure, indication/initial settings/contraindications for NIV, monitoring and escalation. (BTS NIV guidance)
- Pneumothorax: primary vs secondary distinctions, measurement‑dependent size definitions, conservative vs aspiration vs chest drain (10–14 Fr), tension pneumothorax immediate decompression, post‑procedure monitoring and air travel guidance. (BTS pleural procedures)
- Pulmonary embolism: pre‑test probability, D‑dimer use, CTPA pathway, massive PE and thrombolysis considerations. (NICE NG158)
- CAP: CURB‑65 use for disposition and antibiotic thresholds. (NICE)
- Emergency diagnostics: use and timing of SpO2, PEFR, ABG, CXR/CT, co‑oximetry for CO poisoning.
- Oxygen and equipment: oxygen cylinder duration formula (CD ≈460 L example), oxygen targets (COPD 88–92%).
- Ventilation physiology: auto‑PEEP, permissive hypercapnia, ventilatory settings for severe asthma (low Vt, long expiratory time).
- Exam pitfalls: D‑dimer misuse, cylinder volume traps, age cutoff traps, silent chest significance, “normal” PaCO2 in severe asthma.
Note: No original question numbers were supplied with the chunk notes; the map lists concepts/topics represented in the supplied material and typical FRCEM question areas.

Sources to verify
- BTS (British Thoracic Society) pleural procedures and NIV guidance; NICE guidance (asthma, CAP, PE — NG158); BNF; UKHSA antiviral / influenza guidance; RCEM and local hospital/ICU protocols for NIV and ventilator limits; Thorax/BMJ pleural and pneumothorax guidance.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 13: PHARMACOLOGY & POISONING
============================================================================================

Chapter 13 — Pharmacology & Poisoning (final)

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core principle: ABC first (secure airway — intubate if GCS ≤8 or loss of airway reflexes), high‑flow O2, circulation support; stop ongoing exposure (remove contaminated clothing, irrigate skin) before non‑urgent interventions.
- Toxidrome‑driven approach: patterns of autonomic/neuromuscular/mental state signs suggest groups of toxins and guide empiric antidote (opioid, cholinergic, anticholinergic, sympathomimetic, serotonergic, sedative‑hypnotic, TCA, cyanide, CO, CCB/β‑blocker).
- Mechanisms to recall:
  - Membrane Na‑channel blockade → QRS widening, arrhythmia risk (TCAs).  
  - ETC/mitochondrial inhibition → profound lactic acidosis with normal PaO2 (cyanide).  
  - Impaired O2 carriage (CO, methemoglobin) → normal PaO2 but tissue hypoxia.  
  - Mixed respiratory alkalosis + metabolic acidosis → salicylates.
- Antidotes/enhanced elimination: specific agents (NAC for paracetamol, digoxin‑Fab, hydroxocobalamin for cyanide, methylene blue for methemoglobinaemia, fomepizole for toxic alcohols), MDAC for enterohepatic recirculators, haemodialysis for dialysable toxins.
- Monitoring essentials: ECG, ABG/VBG with lactate, glucose, electrolytes, renal/liver function, drug levels if available; continuous cardiac/respiratory monitoring for unstable patients.

Arabic (موجز): ابدأ دوماً بـ ABC، تمييز التوكسدروم يوجّه العلاج الفعّال، واعلم ترياقات محددة (مثل NAC للباراسيتامول، Hydroxocobalamin للسيانيد، ميثيلين بلو للمِتهيموغلوبين).

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Major toxidromes (key discriminators):
  - Opioid: pinpoint pupils, hypoventilation → naloxone.  
  - Cholinergic (organophosphate): SLUDGE, miosis, bronchorrhoea → atropine ± oxime.  
  - Anticholinergic: dry, flushed, mydriasis, hyperthermia, delirium → physostigmine only if safe (no TCA cardiotoxicity).  
  - Sympathomimetic/cocaine/amphetamine: agitation, mydriasis, diaphoresis, hypertension → benzodiazepines.  
  - Serotonin syndrome: hyperreflexia, clonus (ocular clonus high‑yield), hyperthermia.  
  - NMS: gradual onset, severe rigidity after neuroleptics — dantrolene.  
  - TCA: anticholinergic + seizures + hypotension + QRS widening (>100 ms risk; ≥160 ms severe).  
  - Cyanide (fire, burning plastics): very high lactate, shock, preserved PaO2/SpO2.  
  - Carbon monoxide (closed space): headache/confusion, normal SpO2/PaO2; measure COHb (co‑oximetry).  
  - Methemoglobinaemia: cyanosis, chocolate blood, SpO2 ≈85% that does not correct with O2; normal PaO2 on ABG.
- Red flags (act urgently):
  - GCS ≤8 or airway compromise → intubate.  
  - QRS >100 ms (TCA) — give sodium bicarbonate; ≥160 ms = high risk arrhythmia.  
  - Rapidly rising lactate in smoke inhalation → suspect cyanide.  
  - Severe hyperkalaemia with suspected digoxin toxicity (e.g., K+ >6.5 mmol/L) or life‑threatening arrhythmia → consider digoxin‑Fab.  
  - Refractory hypotension/bradycardia after CCB/β‑blocker ingestion → escalate to HIET/ECMO.

Arabic (موجز): تعرّف التوكسدرومات الأساسية؛ مؤشرات الخطر: GCS ≤8، QRS المتّسع في TCA، حماض لبني كبير بعد حريق → سيانيد.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate tests: ECG (QRS/QTc, arrhythmias), capillary glucose, ABG/VBG with lactate, pulse oximetry + co‑oximetry if CO/metHb suspected, electrolytes, renal/liver function, coagulation, CK.
- Targeted toxicology tests: paracetamol level (plot on nomogram if time of ingestion known; sample at ≥4 h post single acute ingestion), salicylate level, digoxin, lithium, COHb, methemoglobin, drug levels as available.
- Useful lab patterns:
  - Mixed respiratory alkalosis + metabolic acidosis → salicylates.  
  - Very high lactate with normal PaO2 → cyanide.  
  - High anion gap metabolic acidosis + osmolar gap → consider methanol/ethylene glycol (toxic alcohols); look for visual symptoms (methanol) and renal/oxalate crystals (ethylene glycol).  
  - Chocolate‑brown blood + SpO2 ~85% unchanged with O2 → methemoglobinaemia.
- ECG interpretation pointers: measure QRS width in overdose (TCA); identify hyperkalaemic changes and digitalis changes (arrhythmia types).
- Decontamination decision: activated charcoal generally useful within ~1 hour of ingestion for many oral drugs; MDAC for substances with significant enterohepatic/enteroenteric recirculation (e.g., carbamazepine, theophylline, phenobarbital, dapsone).

Arabic (موجز): فحوص عاجلة: ECG، غازات، لاثات، مستويات دواء مخصصة (باراسيتامول، ساليسيلات، ديوكسين). نمط الـABG يساعد في التشخيص التفريقي.

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. General poisoned patient (algorithm)
1. Airway: protect; intubate if GCS ≤8 or airway reflex loss.  
2. Breathing: high‑flow O2 for all hypoxic/exposed patients; consider hyperbaric O2 for severe CO poisoning per local criteria (e.g., LOC, neuro deficit, cardiac ischaemia, pregnancy) [local protocols vary].  
3. Circulation: IV fluids, titrated vasopressors (noradrenaline) for refractory hypotension.  
4. Stop exposure: remove contaminated clothing; irrigate skin/eyes; protect staff.  
5. Decontaminate: activated charcoal if appropriate (see section 3). MDAC for selected toxins.  
6. Antidote/enhanced elimination give without delaying life‑saving care when clearly indicated. Consult toxicology early.

B. Key toxin‑specific algorithms (concise, include well‑established doses only)
- Paracetamol (acetaminophen)
  - If single acute ingestion and level above treatment line on nomogram → start NAC promptly. SNAP regimen commonly used: 100 mg/kg over 2 h then 200 mg/kg over 10 h (12 h total) [SNAP protocol]; 21‑hour regimens still used in some centres — follow local guideline. [SNAP]
- Tricyclic antidepressant (TCA)
  - QRS >100 ms or hypotension/arrhythmia/seizure → IV sodium bicarbonate boluses and infusion to narrow QRS and treat acidosis (standard teaching: 1–2 mEq/kg bolus or commonly cited 100 ml 8.4% in many references) — verify local dosing protocols. Benzodiazepines for seizures. Avoid phenytoin. [Toxbase]
- Opioid overdose
  - Naloxone titrate IV: initial 0.4 mg IV, increase (0.8 mg etc.) to restore adequate ventilation not full wakefulness; monitor for recurrence. Local protocols vary.  
- Organophosphate/cholinergic poisoning
  - Decontaminate first; atropine IV titrated to drying of secretions/adequate ventilation (initial adult doses often 0.6–1 mg, larger/severe doses repeated rapidly until effect); give oxime (pralidoxime) when indicated. [Toxbase]
- Cyanide (suspected in smoke inhalation from burning plastics)
  - Suspect with very high lactate/normal PaO2. Give hydroxocobalamin (first‑line in many regions) and supportive care; sodium thiosulfate adjunct in some protocols. Early airway/circulatory support required. [source: hydroxocobalamin references]
- Carbon monoxide
  - High‑flow O2 immediately; consider hyperbaric oxygen per local criteria (availability/guidelines differ). Measure COHb via co‑oximetry.  
- Salicylates
  - Early charcoal if indicated; IV sodium bicarbonate to alkalinise plasma/urine and enhance renal excretion. Indications for dialysis include very high levels or severe metabolic acidosis/coma/renal failure — thresholds vary by guideline (e.g., often cited severe level ≈900 mg/L but verify local thresholds).  
- Calcium‑channel blocker (severe)
  - Initial: IV fluids, calcium (IV calcium gluconate/ chloride), vasopressors; for refractory cases start high‑dose insulin‑euglycaemia therapy (HIET) under ICU/toxicology guidance (typical starting bolus 1 U/kg then 1 U/kg/hr titratable up to higher rates; monitor glucose/K+ closely) and consider lipid emulsion and ECMO if refractory. Dosing protocols vary — verify locally.  
- Local anaesthetic systemic toxicity (LAST)
  - Lipid emulsion 20%: bolus 1.5 ml/kg IV over 1 min, then infusion 0.25 ml/kg/min; repeat bolus 1–2× if needed; continue ACLS with modifications. [LitFL]
- Methemoglobinaemia
  - Methylene blue 1–2 mg/kg IV over 5 min (repeat once if needed). Use caution in G6PD deficiency (risk hemolysis). Consider exchange transfusion or hyperbaric oxygen if methylene blue contraindicated.  
- Digoxin toxicity
  - Digoxin‑specific Fab for life‑threatening arrhythmia, severe hyperkalaemia (e.g., >6.5 mmol/L) or haemodynamic instability — dose per product guidance and toxicology consult.  
- Lipid rescue
  - For severe lipophilic drug toxicity (e.g., local anaesthetic, some CCBs, β‑blockers): Intralipid 20% bolus 1.5 ml/kg then infusion 0.25 ml/kg/min; repeat/extend per guidance. [LitFL]  
- Toxic alcohols (methanol/ethylene glycol)
  - Fomepizole first‑line; urgent dialysis for severe acidosis, visual disturbance (methanol), renal failure; bicarbonate for severe acidosis.  
- Isoniazid seizures
  - Benzodiazepines; administer pyridoxine (vitamin B6) IV gram per gram of INH ingested when known, or empiric large dose (e.g., 5 g) in life‑threatening seizures if ingestion unknown.  
- Sulfonylurea hypoglycaemia
  - Bolus glucose + continuous dextrose; give octreotide (e.g., 50 mcg) to prevent recurrent insulin secretion; monitor prolonged hypoglycaemia.
- Hemodialysis/extracorporeal
  - Consider for dialysable toxins (e.g., lithium, severe salicylates, methanol/ethylene glycol, severe theophylline, phenobarbital, some valproate cases). Use EXTRIP and local toxicology to decide. [EXTRIP]

Arabic (موجز الإجراءات): خوارزمية عامة: ABC → ازالة التعرض → الفحم المنشط إذا مناسب → ترياق محدد → غسيل دموي/MDAC حسب السم. جرعات محددة: NAC SNAP 100 mg/kg over 2 h then 200 mg/kg over 10 h؛ Intralipid 20% 1.5 ml/kg bolus + 0.25 ml/kg/min infusion؛ ميثيلين بلو 1–2 mg/kg.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- Well‑established emergency doses/starts (verify local protocol before use):
  - NAC (SNAP) for paracetamol: 100 mg/kg IV over 2 h then 200 mg/kg over 10 h (12 h total) [SNAP].  
  - Naloxone: start 0.4 mg IV (titrate); escalate per local guidance.  
  - Atropine (organophosphate): initial adult 0.6–1 mg IV; repeat rapidly until atropinisation (severe poisoning often needs large/titrate doses) [Toxbase].  
  - Sodium bicarbonate (TCA): teach weight‑based 1–2 mEq/kg bolus or commonly cited 100 ml 8.4% bolus in many references — confirm local dosing.  
  - Lipid emulsion (20% Intralipid): bolus 1.5 ml/kg then 0.25 ml/kg/min infusion [LitFL].  
  - Methylene blue: 1–2 mg/kg IV (repeat once if needed).  
  - Hydroxocobalamin for cyanide: regionally recommended first‑line antidote (dose per product/local policy).  
- Procedures and advanced support:
  - Early intubation for airway compromise; RSI with appropriate drug choices (avoid suxamethonium if hyperkalaemia risk).  
  - Continuous cardiac monitoring; early ICU/HDU involvement for unstable patients or those requiring HIET, digoxin‑Fab, or haemodialysis.  
  - Use extracorporeal life support (ECMO/CRRT) for refractory shock or severe poisonings not responsive to medical therapy where available.  
- Disposition:
  - Discharge only after toxicology assessment: asymptomatic low‑risk single ingestions may be observed and discharged following local protocols.  
  - Admit to ICU for airway support, haemodynamic instability, arrhythmia, seizures, need for antidote infusion/HIET, or dialysis.  
  - Psychiatry/safeguarding referral for intentional self‑poisoning as per local procedure; involve poisons/toxicology service early.  
- Follow‑up:
  - Monitor for rebound toxicity (e.g., enterohepatic recirculation, delayed gastric emptying, extended‑release preparations).  
  - Arrange hepatic/renal follow‑up after paracetamol toxicity; repeat levels as indicated.  
  - Counsel regarding return precautions and link to mental‑health services where relevant.

Arabic (موجز): اعطاء الترياق والعناية المركزة حسب الحاجة؛ راجع سموم محلّي‑وطني لجرعات تفصيلية؛ لا تخرج المرضى المختاة النفسية دون تقييم/إحالة.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Pulse oximetry pitfalls: normal SpO2 does NOT exclude CO poisoning; methaemoglobinaemia often gives SpO2 ≈85% irrespective of PaO2. Use co‑oximetry when suspected.  
- Lactate clue: very high lactate with normal PaO2 in a fire victim → think cyanide, not primary hypoxia.  
- TCA exam focus: QRS width is key. QRS >100 ms → increased risk of seizures/arrhythmia; treat with sodium bicarbonate. Avoid phenytoin for seizures. QRS ≥160 ms = severe.  
- Salicylate exam stem: mixed respiratory alkalosis + metabolic acidosis (early) with tinnitus and hyperventilation. Aim urine pH >7.5 when alkalinising. Dialysis if very high levels or severe acidosis/renal failure — verify threshold locally.  
- Cocaine chest pain: avoid non‑selective β‑blockers in sympathomimetic toxicity (unopposed α‑effect). Use benzodiazepines and nitrates; labetalol is nuanced — check local guidance.  
- MDMA: severe hyponatraemia from SIADH is a classic stem — use hypertonic saline for symptomatic severe hyponatraemia.  
- Suxamethonium pitfall: prolonged paralysis/apnoea → pseudocholinesterase deficiency; continue ventilatory support. Avoid sux in hyperkalaemia risk states.  
- Paracetamol timing: measure level at 4 h for single acute ingestion to use nomogram; if time unknown or staggered ingestion err on side of treatment.  
- Activated charcoal: typical effective window ~1 h; some sustained‑release formulations or large ingestions may extend this — agent specific. Do not give for corrosive or hydrocarbon aspiration risk.  
- Methylene blue caution: can precipitate haemolysis in G6PD deficiency and may interact with serotonergic drugs (risk of serotonin syndrome) — weigh risks but do not delay life‑saving treatment in severe metHb.

Arabic (موجز نصائح للامتحان): التمييز بين التوكسدرومات (SLUDGE، QRS في TCA، SpO2 مخادع في CO) مفاتيح للسرعة. QRS >100 ms → بيكربونات في TCA. حافظ على TIMING لباراسيتامول (4 ساعات).

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (مراجعة سريعة بالعربية)
- الأساس: ABC ثم إزالة المسبب → ترياق محدد → زيادة نزع السم (فحم منشط/MDAC/غسيل دموي) → مراقبة مستمرة.  
- دلائل شائعة: QRS واسع = TCA → بيكربونات؛ دم بني شوكولا + SpO2 ~85% = ميت‑هيموغلوبين → ميثيلين بلو؛ PaO2 طبيعي + حماض لبني عالي بعد حريق = سيانيد → Hydroxocobalamin.  
- ترياقات ذات نفع فوري: NAC للباراسيتامول (SNAP أو 21h وفق المستشفى)، نالوكسون للأفيونات، أتروبين للكولينيرجي، فورمبيزول للكحوليات السامة، ميثيلين بلو للمِتهيموغلوبين، Intralipid للسموم الدهنية، ديفيروكسامين للحديد.  
- تحقق من بروتوكولاتك المحلية قبل إعطاء الجرعات الدقيقة أو بدء غسيل دموي.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Paracetamol overdose and NAC regimens (SNAP vs 21‑hour) — paracetamol nomogram timing.  
- Tricyclic antidepressant cardiotoxicity — QRS thresholds and sodium bicarbonate.  
- Cyanide poisoning in smoke inhalation — lactate clue, hydroxocobalamin.  
- Carbon monoxide poisoning — co‑oximetry and hyperbaric oxygen considerations.  
- Organophosphate/cholinergic toxicity — atropine and oxime.  
- Opioid overdose — naloxone dosing and monitoring.  
- Calcium‑channel blocker and beta‑blocker poisoning — calcium, glucagon, HIET, vasopressors.  
- Salicylate poisoning — mixed ABG, urine alkalinisation, dialysis indications.  
- Methemoglobinaemia — methylene blue dosing and G6PD caution.  
- Lipid emulsion therapy (LAST and lipophilic drug overdose).  
- MDAC indications (theophylline, carbamazepine, dapsone, phenobarbital).  
- Toxic alcohols (methanol/ethylene glycol) — fomepizole and dialysis.  
- Digoxin toxicity and digoxin‑Fab indications.  
- Isoniazid seizures — pyridoxine.  
- Malignant hyperthermia / NMS / Serotonin syndrome differentiation and antidotes (dantrolene, cyproheptadine).  
- Metal toxicities: iron (deferoxamine), lead (EDTA/dimercaprol basics).  
- Pseudocholinesterase deficiency after succinylcholine — prolonged apnea management.  
- General items: ABC priorities, decontamination, key diagnostic patterns (anion/osmolar gaps), pulse oximetry pitfalls, activated charcoal timing, haemodialysis indications (EXTRIP).  
Note: specific exam question numbers were not supplied; mapping above lists all distinct concepts represented in the supplied material.

Sources to verify
- Toxbase (UK toxicology database) [Toxbase]  
- Literature for lipid rescue (Life in the Fast Lane / LitFL) [LitFL]  
- SNAP paracetamol regimen publications / local hospital protocols [SNAP]  
- EXTRIP workgroup recommendations for extracorporeal treatments in poisoning [EXTRIP]  
- RCEM guidance (nitrous oxide, stimulant/serotonergic toxicity) [RCEM 2023/2024]

This chapter is study material, not patient‑specific advice. Verify doses and thresholds with current local/national toxicology guidance before clinical application.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 14: PALLIATIVE AND END OF LIFE CARE
============================================================================================

Chapter 14: Palliative and End‑of‑Life Care — Final revision

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core goals: prioritise patient comfort, symptom control and respect expressed wishes/advance decisions; avoid burdensome interventions unless they change outcomes or align with goals of care. Document treatment escalation plans. (BNF; NICE CKS; NHS England 2017)
- Symptom mechanisms — match to pharmacology:
  - Breathlessness: hypoxaemia, airway obstruction (tumour/lymphangitis), pleural effusion, pulmonary oedema, bronchospasm, PE, anaemia, metabolic causes, anxiety-driven hyperventilation.
  - Noisy breathing (terminal secretions): pooling of saliva/bronchial secretions in an obtunded patient rather than pulmonary oedema.
  - Agitation/delirium: multifactorial — hypoxia, pain, medications, urinary retention, metabolic disturbance, CNS pathology.
  - Nausea: vestibular/raised ICP, bowel obstruction, drug‑induced, metabolic.
  - Pain types: nociceptive vs neuropathic (burning, electric shocks, dermatomal).
- Principle: use lowest effective pharmacologic dose; prioritise non‑pharmacological measures and reversible causes first.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- الأهداف: راحة المريض والسيطرة على الأعراض واحترام رغبات المريض/العائلة. اربط الدواء بآلية العرض واستخدم أدنى جرعة فعّالة.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Rapid recognition:
  - Dyspnoea: assess SpO2, work of breathing, respiratory rate, speech, accessory muscle use. Red flags: severe hypoxaemia, stridor (upper airway obstruction), haemodynamic instability, signs of tension physiology.
  - Noisy breathing: if oxygenation preserved and chest clear → terminal secretions; if crackles/oedema → pulmonary oedema.
  - Agitation: look for reversible precipitants (hypoxia, pain, constipation/urinary retention, infection, drug withdrawal).
  - Nausea/vomiting: red flags include signs of obstruction (colicky pain, distension), raised ICP (headache, focal neuro signs), uncontrolled vomiting causing dehydration.
  - New focal neurological signs or rapidly progressive neurology = urgent imaging/neurology review.
- Differential pearls:
  - Anxiety vs organic breathlessness: preserved SpO2 and normal chest exam suggest anxiety; opioids treat refractory sensation but benzodiazepines helpful for anxiety component.
  - Bowel obstruction: tinkling bowel sounds, distension; do not use prokinetics if high-grade mechanical obstruction.
- Legal/administrative red flags: verify presence/validity of advance decision to refuse treatment; confirm DNACPR/status of treatment escalation.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- تمييز سريع للأعراض: تفحّص علامات الخطر (نقص أكسجة شديد، خَناق، علامات انسداد معوي أو ضغط دم غير مستقر). تحقق من وجود قرارات مسبقة موقعة.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Focused bedside assessment only — investigations if they will change immediate care or align with goals:
  - Immediate: ABCs, SpO2, vital signs, targeted exam (respiratory, cardiac, neuro, abdominal).
  - Tests to consider selectively: CXR (pleural effusion/airway/aspiration), bedside ultrasound (effusions, pneumothorax, cardiac failure), ABG if respiratory failure suspected, point-of-care glucose, basic bloods if sepsis or reversible metabolic cause suspected.
  - Avoid blanket tests in terminal phase that do not alter management; always weigh burden vs benefit.
- Interpretation tips:
  - SpO2 threshold: consider supplemental O2 for symptomatic hypoxaemia (SpO2 ≤92% in most patients) [NICE CKS].
  - Chest crackles + peripheral oedema → consider diuresis for pulmonary oedema when consistent with goals.
  - Normal O2 with noisy breathing → treat secretions, not oxygen.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- اطلب فحوصات فقط إن ستؤثّر على الاختيارات العلاجية الفورية. عتبة مؤاتية للأكسجين عادة SpO2 ≤92% للمرضى المصابين بضيق التنفس الأعراضي.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- General initial steps for all palliative emergencies:
  1. Assess goals of care, check advance decisions/DNACPR/treatment escalation plan.
  2. ABC approach; stabilise reversible life‑threats only if aligned with goals.
  3. Treat symptoms for comfort while investigating reversible causes if appropriate.

Algorithm A — Dyspnoea in palliative patient
1. ABCs; treat severe hypoxaemia first (supplemental low‑flow O2 for symptomatic hypoxaemia, usually SpO2 ≤92%). (NICE CKS)
2. If oxygenation adequate but distress persists → consider low‑dose opioid for refractory breathlessness (first‑line symptomatic therapy). Titrate to comfort; monitor sedation and secretions. (BNF/NICE)
3. If anxiety predominates (tachypnoea, fear, preserved SpO2) → add benzodiazepine (short acting).
4. Assess for reversible causes (treat infection, drain large pleural effusion if appropriate and desired, consider steroids for lymphangitic spread/airway inflammation).
5. If bronchospasm → bronchodilator therapy; if pulmonary oedema with goals permitting → diuretics.

Algorithm B — Noisy breathing (terminal secretions)
1. Confirm likely terminal secretion (unresponsive, normal oxygenation/clear chest).
2. Reposition patient; mouth care.
3. Antimuscarinic therapy (e.g., glycopyrronium) SC to reduce secretions; avoid routine suctioning in terminal phase.
4. Explain to family that sound ≠ necessarily distress.

Algorithm C — Agitation/delirium at end of life
1. Search and treat reversible causes (pain, hypoxia, urinary retention, constipation, medication effects).
2. For severe distress: use short‑acting benzodiazepine (midazolam titrated SC/IV) for sedation; consider antipsychotic if delirium (haloperidol or other per local guidance) if consistent with goals.
3. Consider palliative sedation only with multidisciplinary and preferably prior consent/documentation.

Algorithm D — Nausea and vomiting
1. Identify mechanism: vestibular/ICP → cyclizine; bowel obstruction → anticholinergic (hyoscine butylbromide) and consider antisecretory measures; chemo/post‑op → 5‑HT3 antagonists or dopamine antagonists as indicated.
2. Avoid prokinetics in high‑grade mechanical obstruction.

Algorithm E — Pain (including neuropathic)
1. Assess type: nociceptive → opioids titrated; neuropathic features → add gabapentinoid/antidepressant as adjuvant.
2. Consider route: oral if possible, else subcutaneous or syringe driver for continuous control.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
- نقاط عامة: تقيّم الأهداف أولًا. ضيق التنفّس: أكسجين إن نقص تشبّع، وإلا مرفين لخفض الشعور؛ إفرازات نهاية الحياة: غليكوبيررونيوم وموضعى/تموضع جيد؛ تيّبس/هيجان: استبعد الأسباب القابلة للعلاج ثم هدئ بالميدازولام؛ غثيان: ضع العلاج بحسب الآلية (سيكلزين للأسباب الدهليزيّة/ضغط الدماغ).

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- Key drugs and typical palliative starting ranges (verify local formularies):
  - Oxygen: for symptomatic hypoxaemia (SpO2 threshold commonly ≤92%) [NICE CKS].
  - Glycopyrronium (terminal secretions): commonly 200–400 mcg SC bolus (or syringe driver infusion per local protocol). (BNF)
  - Hyoscine (butylbromide or hydrobromide): alternative antimuscarinic for secretions/antisecretory effect.
  - Midazolam (acute severe agitation): typical palliative SC bolus 2.5–5 mg titrated to effect. (BNF)
  - Lorazepam (anxiety-related dyspnoea): e.g., small oral doses (local variation).
  - Opioids for refractory breathlessness: titrate low doses (local protocols/BNF).
  - Cyclizine for vestibular/raised‑ICP nausea; avoid prokinetic in mechanical obstruction.
  - Gabapentin/pregabalin or amitriptyline/duloxetine for neuropathic pain adjuncts.
- Procedures/actions:
  - Pleural drainage, paracentesis or bowel decompression only if aligned with goals and likely to relieve symptoms.
  - Avoid invasive life‑prolonging procedures if inconsistent with advance decisions.
  - Consider SC syringe driver for continuous symptom control when repeated dosing needed and per local palliative team guidance.
- Disposition:
  - If symptom control achieved and goals of care documented → consider discharge to hospice/inpatient palliative care or home with community support.
  - If reversible cause requiring admission or intervention and aligned with goals → admit.
  - If imminent dying and family support available → discuss community/hospice/ward management as appropriate.
- Follow‑up:
  - Ensure clear handover to palliative care team/community nursing; document medications given, response, and escalation preferences.

شرح موجز بالعربية:
