  4) Labs: CBC, CRP/ESR (CRP correlates with infection/inflammation), renal function (for drug dosing), consider uric acid only as baseline (not diagnostic in acute attack).  
  5) Imaging: plain X‑ray to exclude fracture/advanced OA; US to guide aspiration or detect effusion; MRI if deep infection/adjacent osteomyelitis suspected.
- Synovial fluid interpretation:
  - Non‑inflammatory: <2,000 WBC/mm³.  
  - Inflammatory: ~2,000–50,000 WBC/mm³.  
  - Suspicious for septic arthritis: commonly >50,000 WBC/mm³ with predominance of neutrophils, but overlap exists — clinical context and Gram stain/culture are decisive.
- Hand infections: X‑ray for foreign body/osteomyelitis; ultrasound to detect abscess/foreign body; consider CT/MRI for deep space or fascial plane involvement.
- Tendon/nerve diagnostics: ultrasound for tendon tears/tenosynovitis; EMG/nerve conduction for persistent nerve palsies; MRI for complex rotator cuff pathology or preoperative planning.
- PMR vs polymyositis: PMR → raised ESR/CRP, normal/mildly raised CK; polymyositis → markedly raised CK, proximal muscle weakness, EMG/autoantibodies.

النهج التشخيصي (عربي مختصر)
- ابدأ بالتاريخ والفحص الموجّه. بزل المفصل ضروري عند مفصل ساخن. تحليل سائل المفصل (خلايا، صبغة جرثومية، بلورات، مزرعة) يحدد العدوى مقابل النقرس. استخدم الأشعة والسونار لتحديد الحاجة للجراحة أو لتوجيه البزل.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- Suspected septic arthritis (monoarthritis) — concise ED algorithm:
  1) ABCs, analgesia, immobilize joint; septic work‑up (blood cultures, lactate if septic).  
  2) Urgent arthrocentesis (cell count, Gram stain, culture, crystals) — do before antibiotics if safe.  
  3) Start empiric IV antibiotics after aspirations and blood cultures; choose agents per local guidance to cover Staphylococcus aureus (consider MRSA where prevalent) and Streptococci; add Gram‑negative coverage based on risk factors.  
  4) Early orthopedic/rheumatology/surgical discussion for washout (arthroscopic/open) especially for large joints, prosthetic joints, or failure to respond.  
  5) Monitor for sepsis; tailor antibiotics to culture results.
- Hot swollen joint where crystals identified:
  - If crystals present and low clinical suspicion of sepsis, treat for gout/pseudogout: NSAIDs (if not contraindicated), low‑dose colchicine regimens per current local guidance, or systemic/intra‑articular corticosteroid if appropriate. Do not withhold aspiration if concern for infection.
- Flexor tenosynovitis / deep hand infection:
  - Immediate IV antibiotics (cover Staph/Strep; add Pasteurella coverage for cat bites; add anaerobic/human‑bite coverage as indicated) and urgent surgical/hand service consult for washout/drainage. Do not delay operative management if Kanavel signs or fluctuance.
- Overuse/tendinopathy (ED management):
  - Rest/activity modification, relative immobilization (appropriate splint), ice, NSAIDs, provider‑directed physiotherapy referral (eccentric strengthening). Consider corticosteroid injection only when infection excluded and usually after conservative measures fail; surgical referral for refractory cases.
- De Quervain:
  - Thumb‑spica splint, rest, NSAIDs; consider corticosteroid injection at 4–6 weeks if persistent; surgical release if refractory.
- Adhesive capsulitis:
  - Early physiotherapy and analgesia; consider intra‑articular steroid injection or hydrodilatation for severe functional loss; refer for formal physio/orthopedics.
- PMR:
  - Start prednisolone 15 mg daily if clinical picture consistent and arrange urgent rheumatology follow‑up; assess and treat for giant cell arteritis if GCA features present (higher dose steroids needed).
- Bites:
  - Clean, consider prophylactic antibiotics for human/cat/dog bites and puncture wounds; amoxicillin‑clavulanate commonly recommended for Pasteurella and mixed flora (verify local guidance). Tetanus status updated per local policy.

الادارة الطارئة (عربي مختصر)
- مفصل مشتبه بالتهاب إنتاني: بزل عاجل، مزراعة دم، بدء المضاد الحيوي الوريدي بعد البزل، استدعاء جراحة/مفصل.  
- غمد أوتار فلكسوري/خراج اليد: مضاد حيوي وريثمي وعمليّة عاجلة.  
- دقرفان: جبيرة إبهام وراحة، حقن ستيرويد إذا استمرت الحالة.  
- PMR: بريدنيزون 15 ملغ يومي ومتابعة روماتولوجية طارئة.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- Drugs (ED‑relevant highlights):
  - Analgesia: paracetamol ± opioid for severe pain; avoid NSAIDs if contraindicated (renal failure, anticoagulation, peptic ulcer disease) — choose per patient risk.  
  - NSAIDs: first‑line for many inflammatory/overuse conditions if not contraindicated; verify local dosing.  
  - Colchicine: use current low‑dose regimens per local/national guidance — older high‑dose regimens are toxic (VERIFY before prescribing).  
  - Steroids: intra‑articular or systemic for gout/PMR/adhesive capsulitis as indicated; do not inject into infected fields.  
  - Bites: amoxicillin‑clavulanate typical empiric choice for cat/dog/human bites (covers Pasteurella, anaerobes) — check allergy status and local guidance.
- Procedures:
  - Arthrocentesis: sterile technique, record appearance, send appropriate tests; ultrasound guidance if difficult or small joint. Do before antibiotics if possible.  
  - Incision and drainage/operative washout: for abscesses, deep hand space infection, septic joints not controlled by aspiration alone — arrange urgent surgical care.  
  - Splinting: thumb spica for De Quervain; wrist splint for ECU/intersection syndromes; sling for acute significant shoulder pain.
- Disposition:
  - Admit: confirmed/probable septic arthritis, deep hand infection requiring surgery, systemic sepsis, uncontrolled pain, significant neurovascular compromise, PMR needing assessment when GCA suspected, or significant comorbidity affecting outpatient care.  
  - Discharge with arranged follow‑up: uncomplicated tendinopathies, stable gout/pseudogout after ED treatment and clear safety net, non‑infected olecranon bursitis with advice. Ensure analgesia, immobilization plan, and timely outpatient physiotherapy/orthopedic/rheumatology follow‑up.
- Follow‑up:
  - Orthopedics/Hand surgery urgent for septic joints, deep hand infections, prosthetic joint involvement.  
  - Rheumatology for PMR, recurrent crystal arthritis, or unclear inflammatory arthropathy.  
  - Primary care/physio for tendinopathy rehabilitation and osteoporosis assessment (DEXA referral where indicated).
- Practical ED notes:
  - Don’t delay arthrocentesis for imaging unless image guidance needed to avoid iatrogenic injury.  
  - If starting empiric antibiotics before aspiration is possible only when aspiration delayed by logistic/safety issues — still perform aspiration ASAP.

الأدوية، الإجراءات، والمراقبة (عربي مختصر)
- الأدوية: مسكّنات، NSAIDs عند الاقتضاء، كولشيسين بجرعات منخفضة حسب الإرشادات الحالية (تحقق)، أموكس‑كلاف للمضغات.  
- الإجراءات: بزل المفصل، تصريف/تنظيف جراحي لالتهابات اليد، جبائر مناسبة.  
- الخروج أو القبول: ادخال للمستشفى لحالات التهاب مفصلي إنتاني، عدوى يد عميقة أو ضعف نظامي؛ إحالة متابَعَة للروماتولوجي/جراحة يد/أورثوبيدكس حسب الحالة.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Never rely on serum uric acid alone to diagnose gout — synovial fluid crystal analysis is diagnostic. (Exam trick frequent in SBAs.)  
- Synovial fluid WBC >50,000 and neutrophil predominance strongly suggests septic arthritis, but overlap exists; presence of crystals does NOT exclude concurrent infection. Always correlate clinically.  
- Kanavel’s 4 signs = flexor tenosynovitis → assume need for urgent surgical review even if single sign present. Negative signs do not fully exclude early disease.  
- Finkelstein technique — ensure the patient makes a fist with thumb inside the fist; examiner ulnar‑deviates wrist. Confusion between Eichhoff and true Finkelstein is a common exam trap.  
- Painful arc interpretation: 60–120° → subacromial (supraspinatus/impingement); pain at extremes only → glenohumeral/AC pathology. Use exact arc numbers in answers.  
- Differentiate lateral epicondylitis vs radial tunnel by location of maximal tenderness (epicondyle vs 3–5 cm distal) and resisted middle‑finger extension test. PIN palsy = motor deficits without sensory loss.  
- Do not delay arthrocentesis for routine X‑ray when infection suspected — culture yield falls after antibiotics. Exam scenarios often test timing.  
- Bites: first‑line prophylaxis for cat/human/dog puncture wounds often amoxicillin‑clavulanate — exam scenarios expect this. Check allergy options (e.g., doxycycline for penicillin allergy in adults per local guidance).  
- Watch for outdated/unsafe statements in stems (older colchicine regimens, specific NSAID dosing like unusual naproxen schedules, absolute statements about aspirin and gout) — flag and verify with current guidelines.

نصائح للامتحان وأخطاء شائعة (عربي مختصر)
- اليوريك أسيد المرتفع وحده لا يثبت النقرس.  
- >50,000 خلية/مم³ في سائل المفصل يشير بشدة للعدوى لكن يوجد تداخل.  
- تذكر تفاصيل فحص فينكلشتاين وصيغة علامات كانافيل.  
- لا تؤجل البزل بسبب صورة روتينية إذا اشتبهت بعدوى.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary
- أي مفصل أحادي ساخن → بزل فوري وإرسال لعزل الجراثيم والبلورات، مزرعة دم إذا حمى، وبدء مضاد حيوي وريدي بعد البزل إذا الشك مرتفع.  
- علامات كانافيل → افترض التهاب غمد الأوتار الفلكسوري واطلب رأي جراحة يد عاجل؛ الخراج → تصريف.  
- دقرفان → فinkelstein إيجابي: جبيرة إبهام وراحة وNSAID؛ فشل التحسن → حقن ستيرويد أو جراحة.  
- الكتف: القوس المؤلم 60–120° → التهاب أوتار supraspinatus؛ فقدان الحركة النشط والسلبي → كتف متجمد → علاج طبيعي وحقن ستيرويد عند الحاجة.  
- PMR → بريدنيزون 15 ملغ يومياً مع متابعة روماتولوجية وتقييم لعلامات GCA.  
- لدغات/عضات: أموكس‑كلاف عادة لتغطية Pasteurella والميكروبات المختلطة — تحقق من التوصيات المحلية.

خلاصة سريعة بالعربية (موجزة)
- بزل المفصل عند وجود مفصل ساخن، علاج جراحي للمضاعفات اليدوية، جبائر وأدوية تحفظية للأوتار، وستيرويد منخفض الجرعة للـ PMR مع متابعة.

8. Question coverage map (concepts → typical FRCEM question foci)
- Q1–Q6: Acute monoarticular joint — septic arthritis vs gout/pseudogout; timing of arthrocentesis, synovial fluid interpretation.  
- Q7–Q9: Flexor tenosynovitis / Kanavel signs, hand infections, bite management and antibiotic choices.  
- Q10–Q12: De Quervain vs intersection syndrome; Finkelstein test technique and splinting/indications for steroid injection.  
- Q13–Q16: Rotator cuff impingement tests (painful arc, Neer/Hawkins, Empty‑can), adhesive capsulitis features and management.  
- Q17–Q19: Lateral elbow pain — tennis elbow vs radial tunnel vs PIN palsy; localization and special tests.  
- Q20–Q22: PMR recognition, initial steroid dosing and GCA screen; polymyositis vs PMR differentiation (CK, EMG, autoantibodies).  
- Q23–Q25: Synovial fluid thresholds, limitations of serum uric acid, co‑existence of crystals and infection.  
- Q26–Q28: Imaging choices (X‑ray, US, MRI) for joint effusion, tendon pathology, and when to use guided aspiration.  
- Q29–Q31: Bite wounds and empiric antibiotic selection (Pasteurella/human bite coverage), tetanus considerations.  
- Q32–Q34: Emergency disposition decisions — who to admit vs discharge, orthopedics/hand/rheumatology referral thresholds.

Sources to verify
- RCEM, NICE, ERC, local antimicrobial guidelines, national rheumatology/orthopaedics guidance, and current formularies (for colchicine dosing and specific antibiotic regimens).


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 21: MENTAL HEALTH
============================================================================================

Chapter 21: Mental Health

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Scope: ED mental‑health emergencies include acute behavioural disturbance (ABD)/agitation, acute psychosis/mania, self‑harm/suicidality, intoxication/withdrawal (alcohol, stimulants, GHB), and delirium from medical causes. Prioritise safety and exclude medical mimics.
- Pathophysiology (brief): agitation/ABD arises from toxicologic (sympathomimetic, anticholinergic, sedative withdrawal), neurologic (delirium, head injury, seizures), endocrine/metabolic (thyrotoxicosis, hypoglycaemia, electrolyte disturbance), and primary psychiatric processes (psychosis, mania). Severe agitation → hyperadrenergic state, hyperthermia, rhabdomyolysis, metabolic collapse.
- Legal/ethical: capacity is decision‑ and time‑specific; never assume incapacity from diagnosis. MHA pathways (Section 136, Section 2) determine detention and assessment logistics in the UK. [MHA 1983; RCEM Best Practice 2024]

Arabic summary:
- الطيف: ABD، ذهان، انسحاب/تسمم، اضطراب تأخّر عقلاني. استبعد الأسباب الطبية أولاً.  
- القدرة العقلية تتغير ويجب تقييمها لكل قرار؛ قوانين الاحتجاز (MHA) تحدد المسارات.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Presentation spectrum: mild agitation → violent ABD (biting/spitting, disorientation, inability to record observations). Look for clues: pupils, temperature, sweating, tremor, focal neurology, history of substance use, medication changes.
- Differential (key distinctions):
  - Intoxication/withdrawal: variable consciousness, autonomic signs, rapid onset.
  - Delirium/medical: acute onset, fluctuating cognition, abnormal vitals, often older patients.
  - Primary psychosis/mania: gradual change, chronic social decline, persistent hallucinations/delusions.
  - Neurologic lesion: focal signs, post‑traumatic onset.
- Red flags requiring immediate medical stabilisation:
  - Unable to obtain vital signs
  - Respiratory compromise or hypoxia
  - Convulsions
  - Profuse agitation with exhaustion or hyperthermia
  - Suspected acute poisoning/overdose
  - Active suicidal intent/plan or command hallucinations to self/other

Arabic summary:
- تميّز التسمم/الانسحاب، الهذيان، والذُهان بالسرعة، العلامات الحيوية، والعلامات العصبية.  
- أعلام حمراء: فقدان القدرة على قياس العلامات، صعوبات تنفسية، نوبات، فرط حرارة، نيّة انتحارية فورية.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate priorities: scene safety → brief focused assessment: ABCs, GCS, capillary glucose, pulse oximetry, temperature, ECG (if antipsychotic/droperidol anticipated), blood pressure.
- Initial tests: finger‑prick glucose; ECG (QTc baseline if possible); bloods as indicated — U&E, CK, LFTs, VBG/ABG if respiratory or metabolic compromise, paracetamol level if overdose suspected; pregnancy test for women of childbearing potential.
- Focused history: onset, substance use, meds, prior psychiatric history, previous suicide attempts, current intent/plan.
- Imaging/other: CT head if trauma, focal signs, acute confusion of unknown cause. Toxicology screens helpful but do not delay treatment.
- Psychiatric liaison: obtain psychiatric input concurrently with medical workup where indicated—do not delay psych assessment until “medically clear.” [RCEM Mental Health Toolkit 2021]
- Legal checks: if patient absconds and returns, repeat triage, vitals and risk assessment. Section 136 clock runs from entry to place of safety (24 h, with possible 12‑h extension). Section 2 requires two doctor recommendations + AMHP. [MHA 1983; RCEM Best Practice 2024]

Arabic summary:
- فحص سريع: ABC وقياس سكر، ECG قبل مضادات الذهان إن أمكن، وتحاليل موجهة (CK، وظائف كبد/كلية).  
- تقييم نفسي متزامن مع الفحص الطبي؛ أعِد تقييم المريض إذا عاد بعد هروب. القوانين تحدد أطر الاحتجاز.

4. Emergency management algorithms
Algorithm A — ABD (compact)
1) Safety and environment: secure low‑stimulus area, remove hazards, one‑to‑one observation, call security/police if necessary.
2) Verbal de‑escalation: calm tone, simple requests, time‑limited offers. Document attempts.
3) If imminent harm or restraint required despite de‑escalation:
   - Use minimum necessary, proportionate physical restraint; avoid prolonged prone restraint; reposition to supine/side‑lying.
   - If restraint >10 minutes → proceed to rapid tranquilisation. [NICE NG10]
4) Rapid tranquilisation (see Section 5 for doses). Monitor airway, breathing, circulation continuously.
5) Post‑sedation: continuous obs, ECG if QT‑prolonging drugs used, repeat assessment, escalate to critical care for airway compromise, unstable haemodynamics, refractory agitation, or failed sedation.
6) Concurrently: search for reversible causes and treat (hypoglycaemia, infection, intoxication, electrolyte disturbances).

Algorithm B — Suicidal/high‑risk self‑harm
1) Immediate safety: remove means, constant observation, one‑to‑one if high risk.
2) Rapid risk assessment (prior attempts, plan/intent, access to means, substance use).
3) Medical workup as indicated (tox screen, paracetamol level).
4) Urgent psychiatric liaison; admit if persistent high risk or inability to secure safety.

Arabic summary:
- مسار ABD: أمان الموقف → تهدئة لفظية → تقييد متناسب → إذا تجاوزت القيود 10 دقائق أعطِ تهدئة سريعة → مراقبة مستمرة.  
- مسار الانتحار: أزل الوسائل → رصد دائم → تقييم المخاطر → تواصل مع الطب النفسي.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
Drugs (high‑yield doses per RCEM/NICE):
- Ketamine IM 4 mg/kg — first‑line rapid tranquilisation for severe ABD where indicated. [RCEM Oct2023 / May2025]
- Droperidol IM 5–10 mg — alternative first‑line; monitor QTc. [RCEM Oct2023]
- Midazolam IM 5–10 mg — alternative (caution: prolonged sedation, elderly). [RCEM]
- Lorazepam IM 4 mg — alternative benzodiazepine option.
- Haloperidol 5 mg IM ± lorazepam 2 mg IM — effective combo; watch QTc and extrapyramidal effects.
- Diazepam IV maintenance 0.3 mg/kg (titrate) or lorazepam IV 0.03 mg/kg for escalation in monitored setting.
Practical notes:
- Adjust doses for frailty, comorbidity, and prior IM agents (reduce subsequent IV doses).
- Large IM volumes: plan multiple injection sites or higher concentration preparations.
- Avoid combining multiple sedatives without airway plan — increased respiratory depression risk.
Airway/RSI:
- In anticipated controlled airway need, avoid suxamethonium where risk of hyperkalaemia exists (severe exertional rhabdomyolysis, renal impairment) — prefer rocuronium for paralysis. Avoid routine opioid pre‑treatment in ABD. [RCEM Oct2023]
Specific scenarios:
- Serotonin toxicity: consider specialist toxicology input; some guidance references chlorpromazine IV/IM with hypotension risk — verify locally.
- Alcohol/benzodiazepine withdrawal: benzodiazepines are first line.
- GHB/GBL withdrawal: consider baclofen per toxicology guidance (TOXBASE).
Procedures and monitoring:
- Continuous cardiorespiratory monitoring after sedation, regular obs, temperature, CK and renal function if hyperthermia or prolonged agitation.
Disposition and follow‑up:
- Admit to medical/critical care for airway compromise, unstable haemodynamics, ongoing medical cause, severe overdose, or failed ED sedation.
- Psychiatric admission/liaison if high suicide risk, severe psychosis, inability to ensure safety, or need for MHA assessment.
- If discharged: clear safety plan, follow‑up arranged, documented capacity assessment, and restraints/medications recorded.

Arabic summary:
- أدوية التهدئة السريعة: كيتامين IM 4 mg/kg أو دروبروليدول 5–10 mg IM؛ بدائل بنزوديازيبينية وهالوبيريدول ± لورازيبام.  
- في التنبيب السريع تجنّب سوكساميثونيوم عند خطر فرط بوتاسيوم؛ استخدم روكورونيوم.  
- ادخل المريض إلى العناية المكثفة إذا وجود مشاكل في المسالك الهوائية، عدم استقرار، أو فشل التهدئة.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Exam tricks:
  - Strongest predictor of future suicide = prior suicide attempt.
  - Restraint >10 minutes → indicates need for rapid tranquilisation (NICE NG10). Cite drug/dose choices and monitoring.
  - Section 136 detention: clock starts on entry to place of safety (24 h, possible 12‑h extension); Section 2 needs two medical recommendations + AMHP. [MHA 1983]
- Common pitfalls:
  - Equating orientation with capacity — perform structured capacity test (understand, retain, weigh, communicate) and document. Diagnosis ≠ incapacity.
  - Delaying psychiatric assessment until “medically clear” — psychiatric input should be concurrent for self‑harm/overdose.
  - Forgetting medical causes — always exclude hypoxia, hypoglycaemia, sepsis, head injury, metabolic disturbance before attributing to primary psychiatric illness.
  - Prone restraint risk — avoid prone positioning to prevent positional asphyxia.
  - QTc and drug interactions — obtain ECG where possible prior to droperidol/haloperidol and correct electrolytes.
  - RSI drug caveats — don’t reflexively use suxamethonium in vigorous/exertional rhabdomyolysis or suspected hyperkalaemia risk.
  - Dosing and accumulation — account for prior IM doses when giving IV sedation.
  - Documentation: record de‑escalation attempts, capacity assessment, rationale for detention, exact drugs/doses/times, and plan.

Arabic summary:
- حيل للامتحان: المحاولة الانتحارية السابقة أهم مؤشر للخطر؛ إذا تجاوز التقييد 10 دقائق استخدم تهدئة سريعة.  
- أخطاء شائعة: عدم تقييم القدرات بشكل منهجي، تأخير تقييم الطب النفسي، تهميش الأسباب الطبية للاضطراب، وتجاهل مخاطر وضعية التقييد.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (Modern Standard Arabic)
- ABD حالة طارئة: ابدأ بالبيئة الآمنة والتهدئة اللفظية، وقيّم ABCs ووعي المريض وسكر الدم.  
- تهدئة سريعة: كيتامين IM 4 mg/kg أو دروبروليدول 5–10 mg IM؛ بدائل: ميدازولام 5–10 mg IM، لورازيبام 4 mg IM، هالوبيريدول 5 mg ± لورازيبام 2 mg IM. راقب التنفس وQTc.  
- RSI: تجنّب سوكساميثونيوم إذا خطر فرط بوتاسيوم؛ روكورونيوم مفضل. لا تخلط مهدئات متعددة بلا خطة مجرى هوائي.  
- قانونياً: Section 136 يبدأ عند دخول مكان الأمان (24 ساعة + 12 ساعة تمديد محتمل)؛ Section 2 يتطلب توصيتين طبيتين وAMHP.  
- راجع دائمًا الأسباب الطبية وقم بتوثيق تقييم القدرة والقرارات العلاجية.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- ABD recognition and priorities: agitation spectrum, red flags, safety sequence (de‑escalation → restraint → rapid tranquilisation). [RCEM Oct2023; NICE NG10]
- Rapid tranquilisation: ketamine 4 mg/kg IM, droperidol 5–10 mg IM, benzodiazepine/antipsychotic alternatives, monitoring and complications. [RCEM Oct2023/May2025]
- Airway and RSI in ABD: rocuronium vs suxamethonium, opioid pre‑treatment cautions. [RCEM Oct2023]
- Medical causes and diagnostics: ABCs, glucose, ECG, U&E/CK/LFTs, paracetamol level, CT head indications. [RCEM; NICE]
- Legal/ethical: capacity principles, Section 136 timing, Section 2 requirements, management after absconding. [MHA 1983; RCEM Best Practice 2024]
- Suicidality assessment and disposition: risk factors, removal of means, observation, psychiatric liaison. [RCEM Mental Health Toolkit 2021]
- Special scenarios: serotonin toxicity, alcohol/benzodiazepine withdrawal, GHB/GBL withdrawal, specific antidotes/agents (refer to toxicology). [TOXBASE; RCEM notes]
- Practical pitfalls/exam pearls: documentation, ECG before QT‑prolonging drugs, restraint positioning, dosing adjustments after IM drugs.

Sources to verify
- RCEM Acute Behavioural Disturbance in Emergency Departments (Oct 2023; May 2025 updates)  
- RCEM Mental Health Toolkit (2021)  
- RCEM Best Practice: The Patient Who Absconds (2024)  
- NICE NG10: Violence and aggression (short‑term management)  
- Mental Health Act 1983 (UK)  
- TOXBASE / local toxicology and anaesthesia/airway guidelines; ERC, local hospital protocols

(Study material — not individual patient advice. Verify drug availability, concentrations, and contentious/flagged items against local formularies, toxicology, and up‑to‑date guidelines.)


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 22: MAXILLOFACIAL/DENTAL
============================================================================================

Chapter 22 — Maxillofacial / Dental (compact FRCEM final chapter)

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core risks: airway compromise (oral blood, swelling, midface instability), major haemorrhage, dental/occlusal disruption, cranial nerve sensory deficits (V1/V2/V3 distribution). Always start with ABC, C-spine control as indicated.
- Typical injuries: anterior mandibular (TMJ) dislocation, mandibular fractures (inferior alveolar nerve risk → lower lip/chin numbness), orbital floor (blowout) fracture with infraorbital nerve injury/rectus entrapment, zygomatic/maxillary/Le Fort fractures (I/II/III), tooth avulsion (permanent vs primary management differs).
- Anatomy clinically useful: supratrochlear (medial forehead/medial eyelid), supraorbital (~2–3 cm above rim, mid‑pupil line), infraorbital (~1 cm below midpoint inferior orbital rim). Use these for sensory assessment and nerve blocks.

Arabic: مخاطر أساسية: انسداد مجرى الهواء، نزف، اضطراب الأسنان وفقدان الإحساس العصبي. الإصابات الشائعة: خلع الفك الأمامي، كسور الفك، كسر أرضية الحجاج، كسور Le Fort، اقتلاع الأسنان.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Anterior mandibular dislocation: sudden mouth opening/yawning then jaw locked open, protruded mandible, severe preauricular pain, inability to close. Differentiate from mandibular fracture: fractures more often give malocclusion, trismus, step-off, mobility.
- Mandibular fracture: pain, malocclusion, mobile tooth/segment, chin deviation, numbness lower lip/chin (inferior alveolar nerve).
- Orbital floor (blowout): peri-orbital swelling/ecchymosis, infraorbital numbness, vertical diplopia (esp. on upgaze), enophthalmos, restricted ocular movement — entrapment suggested by diplopia + positive forced duction.
- Le Fort patterns:
  - I: transverse maxillary mobility, malocclusion.
  - II: pyramidal midface mobility, infraorbital sensory loss.
  - III: craniofacial dissociation, flattened midface, CSF rhinorrhoea, high airway/bleeding risk.
- Avulsed tooth: identify as permanent vs primary (do NOT replant primary). Permanent teeth: best prognosis with immediate replantation (ideally <60 min).
- Red flags requiring urgent specialist input: airway compromise/stridor, expanding facial/neck haematoma, uncontrolled bleeding, CSF rhinorrhoea, diplopia with entrapment, visual loss, open fractures with contamination.

Arabic: مؤشرات الخطر: صعوبة في التنفس، نزف مستمر، تسرب سائل شفاف من الأنف (CSF)، ازدواجية الرؤية مع احتباس العضلة، فقدان البصر. لا تعيد زرع أسنان لبنية.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Initial priorities: ABCs, C-spine precautions if high mechanism. Control obvious haemorrhage; secure IV access.
- Focused facial/dental exam: inspect occlusion, open/close jaw, palpate mandible/zygoma for step/crepitus, assess CN V sensory branches, ocular exam (visual acuity, pupils, EOMs, look for enophthalmos, globe injury), look for CSF rhinorrhoea (β2‑transferrin if diagnostic doubt).
- Imaging:
  - CT maxillofacial (thin cuts) = gold standard for facial fractures (Le Fort, orbital floor, zygoma, mandible).
  - Panoramic OPG useful for mandible if CT not available.
  - Orbital CT with coronal slices for blowout and inferior rectus entrapment.
- Dental avulsion assessment: identify tooth type (permanent vs primary), check for associated alveolar/mandibular fracture, evaluate socket integrity; time since avulsion and storage medium critical.
- Ancillary: tetanus status, baseline labs in major trauma, consider pregnancy/vision documentation.

Arabic: فحص مركّز: فحص الإطباق، تحرّك الفك، حساسية الأعصاب الوجهيّة، فحص العينين. التصوير المثالي: CT وجهي بقطع رفيعة؛ OPG مفيد للفك السفلي إن لم يتوفر CT.

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. Initial (first minutes)
1. Airway: prepare for difficult airway. Maintain C-spine if indicated. Anticipate RSI or surgical airway. Avoid nasal intubation if suspected Le Fort/basal skull fracture.
2. Breathing/ventilation: oxygen, treat tension pneumothorax if present.
3. Circulation: direct pressure, packing for facial haemorrhage, large-bore IV/consider blood products per major haemorrhage protocol.
4. Disability/exposure: neuro, ocular, dental check. Rapid CT once resuscitated.

Arabic: إجراءات سريعة: حماية مجرى الهواء مع توقع صعوبات، تجنّب التنبيب الأنفي عند الشبه بكسر قاعدة الجمجمة، إيقاف النزف بالضغط والتعبئة.

B. Anterior mandibular dislocation — ED reduction algorithm
1. Confirm anterior dislocation (jaw locked open, protrusion) and exclude mandibular fracture.
2. Analgesia + sedation (procedural sedation or local + opioid ± benzodiazepine). Pad thumbs.
3. Technique: patient seated/supine. Place wrapped thumbs on occlusal surface/ramus; apply steady downward pressure to disengage condyle inferior to articular eminence, then posterior/upward (reduce into glenoid fossa). Expect muscle resistance—use steady force; warn patient.
4. Post‑reduction: assess occlusion, neurovascular status; soft diet, avoid wide opening; consider Barton bandage/support 48–72 h. If unreduced or recurrent: consider GA with muscle relaxation or operative management (OMFS).
Reference: DynaMed.

Arabic: خفض خلع الفك الأمامي: تسكين/تخدير، إبهامات محفوظة على الأضراس السفلية، ضغط لأسفل ثم للخلف، دعم بعد الخفض؛ إذا فشل، استخدام GA/استشارة جراحة الفكين.

C. Mandibular fracture management (ED)
- Do not attempt definitive fixation. Secure airway. Analgesia, cold packs, soft diet, avoid blind reductions. Open fractures → IV antibiotics and urgent OMFS. Immobilize (soft support/bandage); intermaxillary fixation only by specialists.

Arabic: كسور الفك: لا تحاول تثبيت نهائي في الطوارئ؛ استدع OMFS، أعطِ مسكنات ومضادّات حيوية إذا مفتوح.

D. Orbital floor (blowout) management
- Protect globe (shield), avoid nose-blowing, give antiemetic/analgesic. Urgent CT; urgent ophthalmology/OMFS/ENT for entrapment or large defect.
- Indications for urgent surgery: muscle entrapment with positive forced duction, persistent diplopia in primary position, large defect causing enophthalmos, oculocardiac reflex signs (bradycardia), orbital compartment syndrome (urgent lateral canthotomy).
- Consider prophylactic antibiotics if sinus communication (commonly amoxicillin‑clavulanate per local guidance) and check tetanus.

Arabic: كسر أرضية الحجاج: حماية العين، منع النفخ بالأنف، CT عاجل، جراحة عاجلة إذا احتبست العضلة أو فقدان رؤية أو قصور حركي واضح.

E. Tooth avulsion (permanent) algorithm
1. Confirm permanent tooth. If primary → do NOT replant; discard.
2. If patient cooperative: replant immediately into socket (hold in place). If not possible, store tooth in HBSS > milk > saline > patient’s saliva. Avoid dry storage; best if <60 min.
3. After replantation: flexible splint and urgent dental/OMFS follow-up within hours. Tetanus status and consider systemic antibiotics per dental trauma guidance.
4. Document tetanus, advise soft diet; confirm radiographs for root/alveolar fracture and follow-up for pulp necrosis/endo as advised.

Arabic: سنّ دائم مقتلع: أعد زراعته فوراً إن أمكن (<60 دقيقة أفضل)، أو خزّنه في محلول مناسب/حليب. لا تعيد زرع الأسنان اللبنية.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
Drugs / prophylaxis
- Analgesia and sedation: follow local procedural sedation protocols; calculate LA and sedative doses per weight and guidelines.
- Antibiotics: use for open fractures, sinus communication, or contaminated wounds. Common choices in many protocols: amoxicillin‑clavulanate for sinus-exposed orbital fractures; for tooth replantation antibiotics are often recommended (amoxicillin in children; doxycycline in adults in some guidance) — confirm current guidance (IADT/BAOMS/local).
- Tetanus prophylaxis as per immunisation status.
- Anti-emetics to prevent Valsalva in orbital fractures.

Procedures (ED)
- Jaw reduction (anterior dislocation) under sedation; pad thumbs; consider GA if unsuccessful.
- Local nerve blocks (infraorbital, supraorbital, supratrochlear) for analgesia—use 1–3 mL typical per site; aspirate and inject slowly; calculate max LA dose per agent/weight.
- Lateral canthotomy/cantholysis for orbital compartment syndrome (vision-threatening).
- Packing and surgical control for severe facial haemorrhage; early OMFS/ENT/vascular surgery input for uncontrollable bleeding.

Disposition & follow-up
- Immediate OMFS/ENT/ophthalmology referral for: airway compromise, Le Fort III, open/depressed fractures, diplopia with entrapment, globe injury, large facial wounds, complex dental avulsion needing operative management.
- Replanted permanent tooth: urgent dental/OMFS follow-up within hours, splint removal per dental advice (commonly ~7–14 days but variable).
- Stable, simple soft tissue facial injuries: ED wound care, antibiotics/tetanus as indicated, arrange outpatient OMFS/plastics follow-up.
- Admit if airway concern, uncontrolled bleeding, multi-system trauma, or need for operative management.

Arabic: الأدوية: مسكنات، مضادات حيوية عند وجود اتصال جيب أنفي/جرح مفتوح، وتطعيم ضد الكزاز حسب الوضع. إجراءات: خفض الفك تحت تسكين، بلوكات عصبية محلية، شق جوانب الحدقة للحدقة المدارية إذا لزم. إحالة فورية لجراحة الفم/العين عند مؤشرات الخطر.

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Never replant a primary (deciduous) tooth — classic trap.
- Permanent tooth: “milk is good, HBSS best; <60 minutes ideal for replantation” — mnemonic for storage/time.
- Anterior jaw dislocation = jaw locked open + protrusion; fracture = malocclusion/trismus/step-off.
- Infraorbital numbness → think orbital floor/zygoma fracture (V2); supraorbital vs supratrochlear localization is lateral vs medial forehead respectively.
- Avoid nasal intubation if suspected basal skull or Le Fort fractures — frequent exam/clinical pitfall.
- When doing nerve blocks, remember to aspirate before injection; do not inject deep to periosteum; calculate LA dose by agent/weight — exam questions may test landmarks (supraorbital 2–3 cm above rim; infraorbital ≈1 cm below rim).
- In blowout fractures: diplopia + positive forced duction → entrapment → urgent surgery. Enophthalmos may be delayed; absence of enophthalmos does not exclude significant fracture.
- Jaw reduction: pad thumbs; warn about biting—examiners like the “wrap thumbs in gauze” detail.

Arabic: نقاط للامتحان: لا تعيد زرع الأسنان اللبنية؛ الحفظ في الحليب أو المحلول مناسب؛ تجنّب التنبيب الأنفي في كسور قاعدة الجمجمة؛ تنفيس قوة العضلة في كسور أرضية الحجاج يعني احتباسًا ويستدعي جراحة عاجلة.

7. Arabic rapid-review summary (ملخص سريع بالعربية)
- ابدأ بالـ ABCs وحماية فقرات العنق عند الآلية العالية.  
- فكّ الفك الأمامي: فم مفتوح مقفل + بروز الفك → تقليل يدوياً (ضغط لأسفل ثم للخلف) بعد تسكين؛ احذر الإصبع.  
- كسر أرضية الحجاج: خدّ عين المريض، منعه من النفخ بالأنف، CT عاجل، جراحة عاجلة إذا احتبست العضلة أو فقدت الرؤية.  
- سنّ دائم مقتلع: أعد زرعه فوراً إن أمكن أو خزّنه في حليب/محلول مناسب؛ لا تُزرع الأسنان اللبنية.  
- تجنّب التنبيب الأنفي عند الشبهة لكسر Le Fort أو كسر قاعدة الجمجمة.  
- استدع OMFS/ENT/ophthalmology بسرعة في حالة النزف الشديد، CSF rhinorrhoea، مشاكل بصرية أو احتباس عضلي.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered; source Q-number ranges/concepts)
- Covered concepts (mapped to typical FRCEM question topics):
  1. Anterior mandibular (TMJ) dislocation — recognition, ED reduction technique, pitfalls.
  2. Mandibular fracture — features (malocclusion, inferior alveolar nerve), initial ED management.
  3. Orbital floor (blowout) fracture — signs (diplopia, infraorbital numbness), forced duction/entrapment, indications for surgery.
  4. Le Fort fractures (I/II/III) — clinical features, airway risk, CSF rhinorrhoea considerations.
  5. Tooth avulsion — permanent vs primary management, storage media, timing, splinting/follow-up.
  6. Facial nerve sensory landmarks and practical nerve blocks (supraorbital, supratrochlear, infraorbital).
  7. Airway and haemorrhage priorities in facial trauma and contraindications (nasal intubation).
  8. Imaging strategy — CT facial bones, OPG for mandible.
  9. ED procedures: lateral canthotomy for orbital compartment syndrome; indications for GA/muscle relaxant for failed reductions.
  10. Ancillary: tetanus, antibiotics (sinus communication), documentation and disposition criteria.
- Source Q-number ranges: not supplied in the chunk notes. Use the above concept map to cross-reference your question bank.

Arabic: خريطة التغطية: خلع الفك، كسور الفك، كسر أرضية الحجاج، كسور Le Fort، اقتلاع الأسنان، حواجز المسار الهوائي والنزف، بلوكات الأعصاب والـ CT/OPG. أرقام الأسئلة لم تُزوَّد.

Sources to verify
- RCEM guidelines, ATLS (latest), IADT (International Association of Dental Traumatology), BAOMS/OMFS guidance, DynaMed (mandibular dislocation), NICE/UK trauma guidance, ERC trauma airway guidance, local OMFS/ENT/ophthalmology protocols.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 23: INFECTIOUS DISEASES
============================================================================================

Chapter 23 — Infectious Diseases (final)

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core principle: rapid recognition and empiric treatment of life‑threatening infections saves lives; do not delay antibiotics/antivirals for severe disease while waiting for confirmatory tests. Include infection control/public‑health actions (notification, contact prophylaxis) as part of ED management where indicated.
- Common ED syndromes and key pathogens:
  - Acute bacterial meningitis: N. meningitidis, S. pneumoniae; Listeria risk in ≥50 y or immunosuppressed.
  - Severe malaria: Plasmodium falciparum most critical—high parasitaemia causes multi‑organ failure.
  - Dengue: plasma leakage in critical phase → haemodynamic compromise; bleeding risk from thrombocytopenia.
  - Varicella zoster (shingles): viral reactivation in dorsal root ganglia → dermatomal vesicles; risk of dissemination in immunosuppression.
  - Skin/soft tissue and zoonoses: impetigo, scabies, orf (parapox), animal bites; many are self‑limiting but need systemic therapy if spread or host immune compromise.
  - Healthcare exposures: needlestick risks—HIV/HBV/HCV; PEP and HBV immunoprophylaxis are time‑sensitive.
  - C. difficile: toxin‑mediated diarrhoea after antibiotics → risk of severe colitis, leukocytosis, AKI.

Arabic summary (موجز فسيولوجي/سريري):
القاعدة: العلاج المبكر لعدوى مهددة للحياة ضروري؛ تشمل الإدارة الطوارئ الإبلاغ والسيطرة الوبائية عند الحاجة.

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Meningitis: fever + headache + neck stiffness ± photophobia/altered mental status/seizure → meningitis pathway. Red flags: reduced GCS, focal neurology, papilloedema (consider CT before LP).
- Severe malaria: recent travel to endemic area + fever ± jaundice, confusion, shock, severe anaemia, AKI, high parasitaemia, thrombocytopenia.
- Dengue: high fever followed by defervescence; watch for warning signs — abdominal pain, persistent vomiting, mucosal bleeding, rising transaminases, falling platelets, narrowing pulse pressure.
- Varicella zoster: dermatomal unilateral vesicular rash with prodromal neuropathic pain; red flags = immunosuppression, dissemination, visceral involvement.
- Skin infections:
  - Impetigo non‑bullous: localized honey‑coloured crusts, no systemic upset → topical therapy.
  - Scabies: intense nocturnal pruritus, interdigital burrows; treat all contacts.
  - Orf: single nodular lesion after sheep/goat exposure — usually self‑limiting.
- Needlestick exposures: index case risk, type of exposure (percutaneous > mucous membrane), host vaccination status.
- C. difficile: profuse watery diarrhoea post‑antibiotics, leucocytosis, rising creatinine — suspect toxin‑mediated disease.

Arabic summary (التعرف والعلامات الحمراء):
التمييز يعتمد على العرض العام، السفر، والمظاهر الحادة (مثلاً تغير الوعى، صدمة، تدهور وظائف الأعضاء). تعامل مع أي حالة شديدة كعدوى مهددة للحياة حتى تثبت العكس.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Guiding principle: stabilise (ABCs) → take microbiology (blood cultures, swabs, baseline serology) → start empiric therapy in severe cases; confirm with targeted tests.
- Meningitis: blood cultures then immediate empiric IV antibiotics; CT brain before LP only if focal neurology, papilloedema, or low GCS. CSF: cell count, Gram stain, culture, PCR.
- Malaria: thick and thin blood films = gold standard (species ID, % parasitaemia); RDTs useful for screening but do not quantify parasitaemia and do not replace microscopy in severe disease.
- Dengue: NS1 antigen early; IgM/IgG later; management guided by clinical severity and serial labs (haematocrit, platelets, LFTs).
- C. difficile: stool toxin assay/PCR; interpret with clinical severity (WBC, creatinine, albumin).
- Needlestick: baseline recipient serology (HIV, HBsAg/anti‑HBs, HCV); test source if possible.
- Skin lesions and outbreaks: generally clinical diagnosis; send swabs/cultures only if systemic signs, cellulitis, atypical features, or outbreak investigation.

Arabic summary (النهج التشخيصي):
استقر أولاً، خذ مزروعات/فحوصات أساسية، وابدأ العلاج التجريبي في الحالات الشديدة؛ اختبارات محددة لتأكيد وتوجيه العلاج لاحقاً.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- Meningitis (ED algorithm):
  1. ABCs; resuscitate as needed.
  2. Blood cultures; give empiric IV ceftriaxone 2 g IV immediately. Add amoxicillin if ≥50 y or immunosuppressed to cover Listeria (do not delay antibiotics for LP/CT).
  3. Assess need for CT prior to LP (focal signs, papilloedema, low GCS). If CT required, still give antibiotics beforehand.
  4. Perform LP when safe; send CSF (cell count, Gram stain, culture, PCR).
  5. Notify public health if meningococcal suspected; arrange chemoprophylaxis for contacts per public‑health guidance.
- Suspected severe malaria:
  1. ABCs, correct hypoglycaemia, oxygen, IV fluids as needed.
  2. Take blood films + RDT, but give parenteral artesunate immediately for severe features (do not wait for microscopy).
  3. Admit to HDU/ICU; monitor parasitaemia, glucose, renal function, Hb/platelets; treat complications (transfusion, dialysis, ventilation).
- Dengue with warning signs/critical phase:
  1. Monitor haemodynamics and fluid balance closely; use judicious IV crystalloids for shock and guided by clinical markers (pulse pressure, urine output, haematocrit).
  2. Avoid NSAIDs/antiplatelets; transfuse platelets for bleeding per local policy.
- Needlestick exposure:
  1. Immediate wound care (wash), report, and assess exposure risk.
  2. Draw baseline recipient bloods; test source where possible.
  3. Start HIV PEP promptly for high‑risk exposures; consider HBIG + vaccine for HBV non‑immune recipients if source HBsAg positive.
  4. Arrange occupational health/infectious disease follow‑up and serial serology.
- C. difficile:
  1. Stop inciting antibiotics where possible.
  2. Start oral vancomycin 125 mg QID for 10 days as first‑line for initial episode.
  3. Escalate (fidaxomicin, surgery) for fulminant disease per local guidance.
- Skin infections:
  - Impetigo localized non‑bullous without systemic features → topical hydrogen peroxide 1% cream TDS for 5 days (NICE); oral antibiotics for extensive/systemic disease.
  - Scabies → permethrin 5% cream to whole household; consider oral ivermectin for outbreaks/widespread disease.
  - Varicella zoster → immunocompetent with early shingles (≤72 h) and ≥50 y: oral acyclovir 800 mg five times daily for 7 days (or equivalent valaciclovir/famciclovir). Immunocompromised/disseminated: IV acyclovir 10 mg/kg q8h (adjust renal).
  - Orf → supportive care; rarely requires antivirals.

Arabic summary (خوارزميات الطوارئ):
اعطِ العلاج التجريبي فور الاشتباه في حالات مهددة للحياة (السحايا، الملاريا الشديدة، الحزام الناري المنتشر) واتبع خطوات التشخيص والإحالة والمراقبة المذكورة أعلاه.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Key ED drugs/doses (well‑established):
  - Ceftriaxone 2 g IV immediately for suspected bacterial meningitis (add amoxicillin if ≥50 y or immunosuppressed) [NICE NG240].
  - Contact prophylaxis (meningococcal): ciprofloxacin 500 mg PO single dose commonly used — confirm local public‑health guidance [UKHSA].
  - Severe malaria: start parenteral artesunate immediately per WHO/local formulary (admit to HDU/ICU).
  - Varicella zoster: oral acyclovir 800 mg 5×/day for early cases in older adults; IV acyclovir 10 mg/kg q8h for immunocompromised/disseminated disease.
  - C. difficile: oral vancomycin 125 mg QID for 10 days first‑line [NICE NG199].
  - Scabies: permethrin 5% cream applied overnight to all contacts; ivermectin oral as alternative in outbreaks.
  - Needlestick PEP: initiate HIV PEP promptly for high‑risk exposures — typical strategy = two NRTIs + integrase inhibitor (follow local occupational health protocols). For HBV exposure in unvaccinated recipients: give HBIG and start vaccine series immediately.
- Procedures and disposition:
  - Admit all suspected bacterial meningitis, severe malaria, dengue with warning signs/critical phase, and disseminated zoster to appropriate monitored care (HDU/ICU as indicated).
  - Isolate patients with likely airborne or droplet transmissible infections as per local IPC; notify public health for notifiable diseases (e.g., meningococcal disease).
  - For needlestick exposures, ensure urgent occupational health/ID follow‑up and serial serology at recommended intervals.
  - Outpatient follow‑up: arrange primary/ID review for results, vaccination status (HBV), and antibiotic stewardship concerns (C. difficile recurrence).
- Practical notes:
  - Stop inciting antibiotics when C. difficile suspected.
  - For dengue, fluid management is guided by clinical markers—avoid overresuscitation.
  - For impetigo, use topical agents to limit systemic antibiotic use unless extensive.

Arabic summary (الأدوية والإجراءات والمتابعة):
تذكر الجرعات المؤسسة (سيفتر/أرتيسونات/acyclovir/vancomycin) ووجه المرضى ذوي المخاطر العالية إلى الملاحظة/الاستشفاء؛ راجع صحة التطعيم واتبع تعليمات الصحة العامة للمخالطين.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Do not delay empiric therapy in life‑threatening infections to obtain confirmatory tests — exams often test immediate IV ceftriaxone for suspected meningitis vs waiting for LP.
- Malaria: RDT positive is not a reason to avoid parasite quantification; for severe disease treat parenterally regardless of pending film.
- Meningitis contacts: remember to notify public health and offer chemoprophylaxis (ciprofloxacin 500 mg single dose commonly tested) — this is often examined as a systems/management step.
- Shingles: age ≥50 with early disease → oral antivirals; immunocompromised → IV acyclovir. These distinctions are frequent exam points.
- Impetigo: identify “localized non‑bullous without systemic features” → topical hydrogen peroxide 1% first‑line (NICE change) — avoid reflex oral antibiotics in exam answers unless systemic features.
- C. difficile: oral vancomycin is now first‑line for initial episode; metronidazole is downgraded. Exam makers test this change.
- Needlestick PEP: obtain baseline serology but do not delay starting PEP in high‑risk exposures — expected answer pattern.
- Bed bugs vs vectors: bed bugs cause bites and distress but are not proven disease vectors — commonly used as an exam reassurance/triage point.
- Watch for “traps”: asking for exact PEP drug doses — state local protocol and that PEP is time‑sensitive; avoid inventing non‑standard doses unless guideline cited.

Arabic summary (نصائح للامتحان والمحفوفات):
اجب بتفضيل العلاج المُباشر للحالات المهددة للحياة، تذكر تغييرات الإرشاد الحديثة (vancomycin للـC. difficile، hydrogen peroxide للـimpetigo) ولا تتأخر في بدء PEP عند الضرورة.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (مراجعة سريعة بالعربية)
- علاج عاجل للحالات الشديدة: لا تؤخر المضاد الحيوي/الدواء المضاد للفيروسات إذا كانت العدوى مهددة للحياة.
- التهاب السحايا: سيفترياكسون 2 غ IV فور الاشتباه؛ أضف أموكسيسيلين إذا ≥50 أو مضعف مناعة؛ أبلغ الصحة العامة وعالج المخالطين.
- الملاريا الشديدة: اعطِ أرتيسونات وريدي فوراً؛ لا تنتظر الشريحة.
- الحمى الضنك: راقب السوائل والصفائح والإنزيمات، لا تعطِ NSAIDs.
- الجدري/الحماق: مضعف المناعة → acyclovir وريدي؛ كبار السن المبكرون → acyclovir فموي.
- قرح الجلد/القشور: القليل يتطلب علاجًا جهازياً؛ impetigo محدودة → هيدروجين بيروكسيد 1% موضعي.
- لسعات الإبرة: اغسل الجرح، افحص المصدَر والمتلقي، ابدأ PEP/HBIG/لقاح للـHBV حسب الحالة، واتبع الفحوصات المتسلسلة.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Bacterial meningitis: recognition, immediate empiric antibiotics, CT vs LP, public‑health notification, contact prophylaxis.
- Severe malaria: recognition, role of RDT vs microscopy, parenteral artesunate, HDU/ICU care.
- Dengue: critical phase recognition, fluid management, avoidance of NSAIDs/antiplatelets.
- Varicella zoster (shingles): indications for oral vs IV antivirals, immunocompromised management.
- Impetigo: localized non‑bullous management (topical hydrogen peroxide vs systemic antibiotics).
- Scabies and household management; institutional control and ivermectin use.
- Needlestick exposures: wound care, baseline serology, HIV PEP strategy, HBV prophylaxis (HBIG + vaccine).
- C. difficile: suspicion after antibiotics, stool testing, oral vancomycin first‑line, stop inciting antibiotics.
- Zoonotic skin infections (orf) and bedside reassurance (bed bugs).
- Infection control and public‑health actions (notification, contact prophylaxis) across relevant conditions.

Sources to verify
- NICE guidelines (NG240 meningitis; NG199 C. difficile; NG153 and CKS where applicable), UKHSA meningococcal guidance (2024), WHO severe malaria and dengue guidance, UK Green Book (HBV), local occupational health/PEP protocols, RCEM/ERC for resuscitation/ED care.


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 24: HAEMATOLOGY
============================================================================================

Chapter 24: Haematology — Final concise FRCEM review

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Major categories
  - Bleeding from anticoagulation (VKA/warfarin; DOACs — dabigatran [direct thrombin], factor Xa inhibitors [rivaroxaban/apixaban/edoxaban]); heparin (UFH/LMWH). Reversal is agent‑specific. [NICE CKS]
  - Consumptive coagulopathy (DIC): systemic activation of coagulation → simultaneous bleeding + thrombosis; associated with sepsis, obstetric catastrophe, trauma.
  - Thrombotic microangiopathies (TTP/HUS): platelet microthrombi → MAHA, thrombocytopenia; TTP often neuropredominant, ADAMTS13 deficiency; HUS renal dominant (typical = diarrhoeal). 
  - Immune cytopenias: ITP (isolated severe thrombocytopenia), HIT (anti‑PF4/heparin antibodies → thrombosis).
  - Congenital/structural bleeding disorders: haemophilia A/B (deep bleeds, prolonged aPTT), VWD (mucocutaneous bleeding, variable lab pattern), HAE (bradykinin‑mediated angioedema).
  - Transfusion complications: acute haemolytic, anaphylaxis, febrile non‑haemolytic, TRALI (noncardiogenic pulmonary oedema), TACO (volume overload).
  - Practical guidance: prioritise ABCs, stop exposure (anticoagulant/transfusion), give specific antidote if available, use haemostatic resuscitation guided by physiology and POC tests (TEG/ROTEM) in massive haemorrhage [LiTFL].
- Pathophysiology highlights
  - DIC: ↑PT/aPTT, ↓platelets, ↓fibrinogen, ↑D‑dimer.
  - TTP: ADAMTS13 deficiency → uncleaved vWF multimers → platelet aggregation; coagulation screen usually normal but there is MAHA with schistocytes.
  - HIT: immune‑mediated platelet activation ~5–10 days after heparin exposure; thrombosis risk despite thrombocytopenia.

ملخص سريري/فيزيولوجى (مبسّط عربي)
- فئات رئيسية: نزف بسبب مضادات التخثر، DIC، TTP/HUS، ITP/HIT، اضطرابات خلقية (الهيموفيليا/VWD/HAE)، ومضاعفات نقل الدم.  
- أولويات: ABC → إيقاف المعرض المسبب → علاج معاكس محدد → إعادة التوازن التخثرى بدليل TEG/ROTEM عند النزف الشديد. [LiTFL]

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Anticoagulant‑related major bleed
  - Red flags: hypotension/tachycardia, expanding haematoma, intracranial signs, rapid Hb fall, recent/ongoing anticoagulant dose.
  - Identify agent and time of last dose.
- DIC
  - Bleeding + thrombosis in sepsis/OB/trauma; labs as above. Red flag: ongoing oozing, multiorgan dysfunction.
- TTP
  - Pentad (not always complete): MAHA (schistocytes), thrombocytopenia, renal impairment, fever, neurological signs. Clotting times usually normal — do not delay PEX.
- HUS
  - Predominant renal failure, often diarrhoeal prodrome (typical).
- HIT
  - Platelet fall ≈5–10 days after heparin, new thrombosis; use 4T pretest probability.
- ITP
  - Isolated severe thrombocytopenia with mucocutaneous bleeding; otherwise well.
- Haemophilia A/B
  - Spontaneous deep soft‑tissue or joint bleeding; markedly prolonged aPTT with normal PT/platelets.
- VWD
  - Mucosal bleeding, menorrhagia; platelet count usually normal.
- TRALI vs TACO
  - TRALI: acute hypoxaemia, hypotension, fever, noncardiogenic oedema within 6 h of transfusion.
  - TACO: hypertension, pulmonary oedema, volume overload features, responds to diuretics.
- Transfusion reactions red flags
  - Immediate fever/back pain/haemoglobinuria → acute haemolytic transfusion reaction (stop transfusion and resuscitate).
  - Urticaria/bronchospasm/hypotension → anaphylaxis (treat as anaphylaxis).

التعرّف والتمييز والتنبيهات (مبسّط عربي)
- تحقق من تاريخ الدواء، وقت الجرعة الأخيرة، وعلامات الصدمة أو نزف جوفى/مخى.  
- DIC = نزف + تخثر؛ TTP = شستوسايتس + صفيحات منخفضة + زمن تخثر طبيعى.  
- TRALI يظهر كوذمة رئوية غير قلبية خلال 6 ساعات؛ TACO يصاحب علامات حمولة حجمية.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate ED investigations (while resuscitating)
  - CBC (Hb, platelets), coagulation screen (PT/INR, aPTT), fibrinogen, D‑dimer, peripheral blood film (schistocytes), G&S/type & cross, renal function, lactate, bilirubin/LDH/haptoglobin (haemolysis), urine for haemoglobin.
  - POC: capillary/Hb, blood gas ± lactate, TEG/ROTEM in major haemorrhage.
- TEG/ROTEM interpretation (key exam mapping)
  - R time (clotting time) ↑ → clotting‑factor deficiency → give FFP (or PCC in VKA/DOAC context).
  - Alpha angle ↓ → low fibrinogen or slow fibrin build‑up → give cryoprecipitate or fibrinogen concentrate.
  - MA (maximum amplitude) ↓ → platelet dysfunction/low platelet count → give platelet transfusion.
  - Hyperfibrinolysis (LY30 high; threshold institutionally variable, commonly cited >7.5%) → give tranexamic acid. [LiTFL]
- Specific tests (do not delay treatment)
  - ADAMTS13 activity for TTP (send urgently but start PEX without waiting).
  - Anti‑PF4/heparin ELISA (and functional assay) for HIT; calculate 4T score first.
  - DOAC-specific assays: dabigatran‑sensitive thrombin time/ diluted thrombin time; anti‑Xa for Xa inhibitors (availability limited; time since last dose useful).
  - Factor assays for haemophilia; vWF antigen/activity for VWD.
- Imaging
  - CT head for suspected intracranial bleed; CXR/CTPA if pulmonary pathology suspected (ACS/PE/TRALI).

النهج التشخيصى والتفسير (مبسّط عربي)
- اختبارات فورية: Hb/صفائح/INR/aPTT/فيبرينوجين/مسحة دم للكشف عن شستوسايتس.  
- TEG: R↑ → FFP؛ α↓ → فيبرينوجين/كرايو؛ MA↓ → صفائح.  
- ارسل ADAMTS13 وanti‑PF4 لكن عالج دون انتظار النتائج الحرجة.

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. Life‑threatening bleeding in anticoagulated patient — rapid algorithm
  1. ABCs, secure airway, high‑flow O2, IV large‑bore access, blood bank notified, activate MHP if massive.
  2. Local haemostasis (direct pressure/surgical/IR) and haemodynamic resuscitation (blood products guided by physiology/TEG).
  3. Identify anticoagulant and timing.
  4. Specific reversal:
     - Dabigatran → idarucizumab 5 g IV (2 × 2.5 g) emergently. [well established]
     - Factor Xa inhibitors (rivaroxaban/apixaban) → andexanet alfa per local/manufacturer dosing (bolus + 2‑hr infusion) if available; if unavailable or for other Xa agents, give 4‑factor PCC (follow local dosing; commonly 25–50 IU/kg range). Flag licensing/availability. 
     - Warfarin with major bleeding → 4‑factor PCC (rapid) + IV vitamin K (5–10 mg) (give PCC first if life‑threatening).
     - UFH severe bleed → protamine sulphate (approx. 1 mg neutralises ~100 U heparin; adjust for time since last dose; follow local protocol).
  5. Supportive adjuncts: tranexamic acid (1 g IV) per local protocol, platelets/FFP/cryoprecipitate guided by labs/TEG.
  6. Monitor and admit to appropriate level (ICU/HD).

B. Elevated INR without major bleeding
  - INR 5–8, no bleeding: withhold warfarin ± oral vitamin K (typical IV/oral dose decisions per local guidance; common oral 1–5 mg). [NICE CKS]
  - INR >8 without bleeding: give oral or IV vitamin K (often 5 mg oral; IV 1–5 mg slow depending on situation) and withhold warfarin; repeat INR monitoring.
  - Major bleeding on warfarin: PCC + IV vitamin K.

C. Suspected TTP
  1. Recognise MAHA + thrombocytopenia ± neuro/fever/renal signs.
  2. Do not wait for ADAMTS13. Urgent plasma exchange (PEX) + high‑dose steroids (IV methylprednisolone/ oral prednisone ~1 mg/kg as per local practice); involve haematology/ICU. Avoid platelet transfusion unless life‑threatening bleeding.

D. Suspected HIT
  1. Calculate 4T score. If intermediate/high:
     - Stop all heparin products (including flushes), send anti‑PF4 ELISA and functional assay.
     - Start alternative anticoagulation (argatroban, bivalirudin, fondaparinux) per local protocol; do NOT give platelets unless bleeding.
     - Once HIT excluded, resume appropriate anticoagulation.

E. ITP (acute severe bleeding or platelet <30×10^9/L with bleeding)
  - Severe bleeding or platelet critical:
    - IVIG 1 g/kg ×1–2 and steroids (prednisone ≈1 mg/kg or equivalent). Platelet transfusion only for life‑threatening haemorrhage.
  - No major bleeding and moderate thrombocytopenia: oral prednisone first‑line; urgent haematology follow‑up.

F. Hereditary angioedema (HAE) acute attack
  - Airway risk → early C1 esterase inhibitor concentrate IV (first‑line) or icatibant 30 mg SC (bradykinin B2 antagonist) if licensed/available. If unavailable, consider FFP as rescue with haematology guidance.
  - Adrenaline/antihistamines largely ineffective (bradykinin mediator) — classic exam distractor.

G. Transfusion reactions — immediate management
  - Always: STOP transfusion, keep IV line patent with saline, call for help, send implicated unit and post‑reaction samples to transfusion lab.
  - Anaphylaxis: IM epinephrine 0.5 mg (500 µg) lateral thigh immediately; airway/oxygen/IV fluids; H1/H2 antihistamines and steroids as adjuncts.
  - Acute haemolytic: aggressive IV fluids, maintain urine output, monitor for DIC/renal injury; ICU.
  - TRALI: supportive oxygen/ventilation; do not rely on diuretics; consider critical care.
  - TACO: stop transfusion, O2, upright position, IV loop diuretics (furosemide 40 mg IV adult initial dose), haemodynamic support.

H. TEG‑guided massive haemorrhage protocol (quick)
  - R ↑ → FFP/PCC.
  - Alpha angle ↓ → cryoprecipitate or fibrinogen concentrate.
  - MA ↓ → platelets.
  - Hyperfibrinolysis (LY30 ↑) → tranexamic acid 1 g IV.

الخوارزميات الطارئة (مبسّطة عربي)
- نزف مهدد للحياة على مضاد تخثر: ABC → إعلام بنك الدم → idarucizumab 5 g (dabigatran) أو andexanet/PCC (Xa inhibitors) أو PCC+فيتامين K (warfarin) → دعم مختبري/TEG‑موجّه.  
- INR مرتفع بلا نزف: INR>8 → فيتامين K (فمياً/IV حسب السياق)؛ نزف كبير على warfarin → PCC + فيتامين K IV.  
- TTP → ابدأ تبادل البلازما فوراً + ستيرويدات (لا تنتظر ADAMTS13).  
- HIT → أوقف الهيبارين فوراً، ابدأ ضد‑هيبارين بديل وأرسل anti‑PF4.  
- تفاعلات نقل: أوقف النقل، حافظ على وريد بالمحلول الملحى، إعطاء أدرينالين للصدمة التأقية، سوائل للصدمة الهيموليتية، ومدير للتعامل مع TRALI/TACO.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Key acute drugs/doses (well established)
  - Idarucizumab 5 g IV (2 × 2.5 g) — dabigatran reversal. [established]
  - Andexanet alfa — reversal for apixaban/rivaroxaban per manufacturer/local protocol (bolus + infusion; dosing complex and variable) — verify local availability/licence.
  - 4‑factor PCC for life‑threatening warfarin/DOAC bleeding (typical range 25–50 IU/kg; local protocols vary) — follow local dosing.
  - Vitamin K IV 5–10 mg for major bleeding on warfarin; oral 1–5 mg commonly used for high INR without bleeding. [NICE CKS]
  - Protamine sulphate for UFH (≈1 mg protamine per 100 U heparin; adjust for timing; check local limits).
  - Tranexamic acid 1 g IV bolus (trauma/hyperfibrinolysis) — time sensitive.
  - Prednisone ≈1 mg/kg for ITP; IVIG 1 g/kg ×1–2 for urgent platelet rise.
  - IM epinephrine 0.5 mg for anaphylaxis.
  - Furosemide 40 mg IV initial dose for TACO (adult).
- Procedures
  - Emergency plasma exchange (PEX) for TTP (urgent haematology/ICU).
  - Surgical/IR haemostasis for expanding haematomas.
  - Exchange transfusion/simple transfusion in severe sickle chest (discuss with haematology/ICU).
  - Factor concentrate replacement for haemophilia/VWD per local haematology protocols.
  - Airway management and ENT involvement for HAE with laryngeal oedema.
- Disposition and follow‑up
  - Admit to HDU/ICU for life‑threatening bleeding, ongoing transfusion requirement, respiratory failure, or ongoing haemodynamic instability.
  - Haematology and transfusion services involvement early for PEX, factor concentrates, and complicated reversal.
  - Arrange specialist outpatient haematology follow‑up for confirmed ITP, TTP survivors, VWD/haemophilia management plans, anticoagulation restart planning.
  - Document and report transfusion reactions to transfusion service and pharmacovigilance.
- Safety/verification notes
  - PCC dosing and andexanet licencing vary by jurisdiction and product — always verify local protocol before administration.
  - IO samples have limitations: reliable for Hb and glucose but unreliable for WBC, platelets, and electrolytes [LitFL].

الأدوية والإجراءات والتنويم والمتابعة (مبسّط عربي)
- أدوية رئيسية: idarucizumab 5 g (dabigatran)، andexanet (rivaroxaban/apixaban حسب البروتوكول)، PCC (25–50 IU/kg شائع)، فيتامين K IV 5–10 mg للنزف على warfarin.  
- إجراءات: تبادل البلازما لـTTP، علاج جراحى/قسطرة للنزف المستمر، بدائل للدم/ترميم عامل فى الهيموفيليا.  
- التنويم: HDU/ICU حسب الاستقرار؛ إحالة هيماتولوجى مبكرة؛ توثيق/تبليغ تفاعلات النقل.

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Reversal mapping memorise:
  - Idarucizumab = dabigatran only. Do NOT use for Xa inhibitors.
  - Andexanet alfa = licensed for rivaroxaban/apixaban (complex dosing; check local policy). PCC is common alternative if andexanet unavailable. (Flag licensing). [Exam trap]
- Distinguish DIC vs TTP:
  - DIC → prolonged PT/aPTT, low fibrinogen, high D‑dimer.
  - TTP → normal clotting times, MAHA with schistocytes and severe thrombocytopenia.
- HIT: use 4T score before testing; if intermediate/high, stop all heparin and start non‑heparin anticoagulant.
- Platelet transfusion pitfalls:
  - Do not transfuse platelets routinely in ITP or TTP — generally ineffective/harmful unless life‑threatening bleeding.
- TRALI vs TACO:
  - TACO = volume overload, hypertension, responsive to diuretics.
  - TRALI = hypotension, fever, noncardiogenic pulmonary oedema, poor diuretic response.
- TEG exam mapping:
  - R ↑ → FFP; α ↓ → fibrinogen/cryoprecipitate; MA ↓ → platelets. Hyperfibrinolysis → TXA.
- IO laboratory pitfalls: Hb and glucose reasonable; do not rely on IO for platelets/WBC/electrolytes in exams.
- HAE distractors: antihistamines/adrenaline often ineffective; C1 esterase inhibitor/icatibant are targeted therapies.
- Sickle chest syndrome:
  - Exam often expects antibiotics + oxygen + consider transfusion; avoid reflex steroid use as default—check scenario and local guidance.
- Licensing and local guidance caveats:
  - Andexanet alfa, PCC dosing, exact O2 targets in ACS vary—state “follow local protocol” in answers if asked.

نصائح للامتحان ومطبات شائعة (مبسّطة عربي)
- تذكر: idarucizumab فقط لدا بيغاتران؛ andexanet لــapixaban/rivaroxaban (تحقق محلياً).  
- DIC = اختبارات تخثر مطوّلة؛ TTP = زمن تخثر طبيعى + شستوسيتس.  
- لا تعطى صفائح فى ITP/TTP إلا عند نزف يهدد الحياة.  
- خريطة TEG مهمة: R→FFP؛ α→فيبرينوجين؛ MA→صفائح.

7. Arabic rapid-review summary (مختصر سريع بالعربية)
- استعمل نهج ABC أولاً، ثم حدد السبب: مضاد تخثر؟ تفاعل نقل؟ TTP؟ DIC؟ ITP؟  
- نزف مهدد: idarucizumab 5 g لـdabigatran؛ andexanet أو PCC لـXa inhibitors (تحقق محلياً)؛ PCC + فيتامين K لل warfarin؛ بروتامين للهيبارين.  
- TTP = تبديل بلازما فوري (لا تنتظر ADAMTS13).  
- HIT = أوقف الهيبارين وابدأ مضاد‑هيبارين بديل واطلب فحص anti‑PF4.  
- ITP شديدة = IVIG 1 g/kg ± ستيرويد (1 mg/kg).  
- توقف كل نقل دم مشبوه فوراً، حافظ على وريد بالمحلول الملحي، أرسل العينات وبنك الدم.  
- استخدم TEG/ROTEM لتوجيه منتجات الدم: R↑→FFP، α↓→فيبرينوجين/كرايو، MA↓→صفائح.  
- تحقّق محلياً من تراخيص andexanet/PCC وجرعاتها قبل الاستخدام.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Anticoagulant bleeding and reversal: dabigatran (idarucizumab), factor Xa inhibitors (andexanet alfa vs PCC), warfarin (PCC + vitamin K), protamine for heparin. (DOAC reversal)
- Massive haemorrhage and haemostatic resuscitation: TEG/ROTEM interpretation and product mapping; TXA use. (MHP/TEG)
- DIC vs TTP vs HUS differential and immediate management (PEX for TTP). (Microangiopathies)
- HIT recognition (4T score), testing (anti‑PF4) and management (non‑heparin anticoagulants). (Immune thrombocytopenias)
- ITP acute management: steroids, IVIG, platelet transfusion threshold. (ITP)
- Transfusion reactions: acute haemolytic, anaphylaxis, TRALI, TACO — recognition and immediate steps. (Transfusion)
- Haemophilia/VWD acute bleed management: factor replacement, DDAVP, VWF concentrate. (Inherited bleeding disorders)
- Hereditary angioedema acute therapy: C1 inhibitor, icatibant, role/limits of FFP. (HAE)
- Sickle cell acute chest syndrome: recognition and ED management including transfusion considerations. (Sickle cell)
- Practical lab approach: immediate tests (CBC, PT/aPTT, fibrinogen, D‑dimer, blood film), ADAMTS13/anti‑PF4/DOAC assays, IO sample limitations. (Diagnostics)
- Emergency drugs and doses frequently tested: idarucizumab, IV‑vitamin K, PCC ranges, protamine rule of thumb, TXA, IVIG/steroids, IM adrenaline, furosemide for TACO. (Drugs/doses)
- Exam‑style pitfalls: idarucizumab wrongly chosen for Xa inhibitors, platelet transfusion in ITP/TTP, TRALI vs TACO differentiation, TEG mapping, IO lab limits. (Exam traps)

Final note
- This is study material only, not patient-specific advice.

Sources to verify
- RCEM guidelines/teaching resources, ERC/ERC resuscitation guidance, NICE (CKS and NG guidance) — e.g., NICE CKS (Warfarin), NICE NG158; local transfusion service and haemostasis protocols; LiTFL/LitFL TEG/IO primers; institutional DOAC/andexanet/PCC policies; haematology society guidelines (local/national).


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 25: ENVIRONMENTAL EMERGENCIES
============================================================================================

Chapter 25: Environmental Emergencies

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Core idea: environmental emergencies = physiologic injury from heat/cold, pressure changes (diving/altitude), electricity/lightning, toxins (CO, cyanide, chemicals), radiation, blast. Treat physiology first (ABCs, oxygen, circulation) and reverse offending exposure (remove from heat/contaminant, descent or recompression for diving).
- Mechanisms:
  - Heat: direct thermal cellular injury → systemic inflammatory response, coagulopathy, rhabdomyolysis, multi‑organ failure.
  - Cold: (not expanded in chunk notes) hypothermia causes coagulopathy, arrhythmias, altered drug kinetics — manage per hypothermia algorithms.
  - Diving: pressure changes → inert gas supersaturation → bubble formation (DCS) and barotrauma; arterial gas embolism from lung over‑expansion.
  - Altitude: hypobaric hypoxia → AMS, HACE, HAPE via hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction and cerebral/ pulmonary oedema.
  - Electricity/lightning: direct tissue/nerve/heart injury, thermal burns, muscle necrosis → rhabdomyolysis, arrhythmias (AC more VF risk; DC more asystole/tetany — simplification).
  - CO: binds haemoglobin (COHb) and impairs O2 delivery; pulse oximetry unreliable.
  - Cyanide (smoke inhalation): inhibits cellular respiration → very high lactate, rapid collapse.
  - HAZMAT/radiation: local and systemic toxicity; radiation causes prodrome (nausea/vomiting) and lymphocyte kinetics predict dose.
- Initial ED priorities: scene safety (PPE, hot/warm/cold zone), remove contaminated clothing, ABCs, high‑flow O2 for hypoxia/CO/DCS/AGE, IV access, monitor, early specialist alerts (hyperbaric, toxicology, HAZMAT, radiation).

Arabic summary (مختصر):
- الطوارئ البيئية تنتج عن حرارة/ضغط/تيار/سموم/إشعاع؛ البدء بالحماية ثم ABCs وإزالة التعرض؛ الأكسجين 100% مهم مبكراً لبعض الحالات (CO، غوص، انصمام فقاعي).

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Diving
  - Nitrogen narcosis: euphoria, impaired judgment, incoordination at depth (classically >≈30 m); improves on ascent.
  - Decompression sickness (DCS): after ascent — musculoskeletal “bends”, skin changes, fatigue, delayed neurologic signs (minutes–hours).
  - Arterial gas embolism/pulmonary barotrauma: immediate chest pain, dyspnoea, sudden neurologic deficits (stroke-like) after ascent.
  - Inner-ear barotrauma: vertigo, tinnitus, vomiting during descent.
- Heat illness
  - Heat exhaustion: temp <40°C, preserved mental status, profuse sweating, cramps.
  - Heat stroke: core temp ≥40°C with CNS dysfunction (confusion, seizures, coma) — emergency.
  - Red flags: rapid deterioration, coagulopathy, very high CK, oliguria/AKI, DIC, severe acidosis.
- Electrical/lightning
  - Lightning: Lichtenberg figures (fern-like), keraunoparalysis (transient limb paralysis/pallor/coolness), possible arrhythmia.
  - Electrical injury: burns at entry/exit, myoglobinuria, fractures, delayed arrhythmias.
- CO and smoke inhalation
  - Nonspecific symptoms (headache, nausea, confusion); SpO2 often misleading (normal). Cyanide: rapid collapse, severe lactic acidosis.
  - Red flags: LOC, neuro deficits, cardiac ischemia, very high lactate.
- HAZMAT/chemical
  - Clues: multiple victims, unusual odors, visible contamination, ocular irritation, rapid skin damage. Caustic exposures require wet decontamination.
- Radiation and blast
  - Radiation: early nausea/vomiting may indicate significant exposure.
  - Blast: primary blast injures air‑filled organs (lungs, ears); initial normal exam possible — consider 24‑hour observation.

Arabic summary (مختصر):
- ملاحظة الوقت (أثناء الغطس مقابل بعد الصعود) مهمة للتفريق بين narcosis وDCS؛ قياس COHb مهم عند الاشتباه في تسمم أول أكسيد الكربون؛ ضربة الشمس = حرارة ≥40° مع خلل عصبي.

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Immediate: ABCs, continuous cardiac monitoring, SpO2 (note limitations), core temperature (rectal/oesophageal preferred), blood glucose, neuro exam.
- Targeted tests:
  - CO suspicion: arterial/venous blood gas with CO‑oximetry for COHb (pulse oximetry unreliable).
  - Smoke inhalation: ABG, CO‑oximetry, lactate (for cyanide), ECG (ischemia/arrhythmia), CXR.
  - Heat stroke: electrolytes, CK, creatinine, LFTs, coagulation, urinalysis for myoglobin.
  - Diving/neuro deficits: CT brain ± CT angiography if stroke suspected; CXR for barotrauma/pneumothorax; urgent hyperbaric evaluation.
  - Radiation: baseline FBC and serial lymphocyte counts q6–12 h to estimate dose.
  - HAZMAT: decontaminate first; consult poison control; collect exposure history and substances.
- Imaging: CXR (pneumothorax, pulmonary contusion, blast lung), CT brain/spine for suspected AGE or focal deficits, FAST/CT for trauma as indicated.
- Interpretation pitfalls:
  - Normal pulse oximeter does NOT exclude CO poisoning.
  - Early normal CXR/CT does not exclude blast lung or delayed DCS manifestations.

Arabic summary (مختصر):
- فحوصات رئيسية: CO‑oximetry لقياس COHb، CK ومؤشرات الكلوى لضربات الشمس/الصدمة العضلية، تكرار عد الدم للحالات الإشعاعية، تصوير للصدر/المخ حسب الأعراض.

4. Emergency management algorithms
- General immediate steps (all environmental exposures)
  1. Scene safety → PPE, establish hot/warm/cold zones.
  2. Remove patient from exposure → remove clothing (removes ~80–90% contamination).
  3. Airway, 100% O2 if hypoxia/inhalation/CO/DCS/AGE, cervical spine as indicated.
  4. IV access, cardiac monitor, temperature, neuro monitoring; early specialist alerts (hyperbaric/toxicology/HAZMAT).
- Heat illness
  - Heat exhaustion: cool, hydrate (oral/IV), monitor.
  - Heat stroke (core ≥40°C + CNS dysfunction):
    1. ABCs; intubate if altered airway.
    2. Rapid cooling: ice‑water immersion is gold standard for exertional heat stroke. If immersion unavailable use evaporative cooling (spray + fans) and cold packs to groin/axillae.
    3. IV fluids, monitor for rhabdomyolysis, DIC, electrolytes; avoid antipyretics as primary therapy.
- Diving (narcosis/DCS/AGE)
  - Nitrogen narcosis: ascent to shallower depth, O2, supportive.
  - DCS: 100% O2, immobilize, IV fluids, analgesia, urgent hyperbaric recompression (definitive).
  - AGE/pulmonary barotrauma: 100% O2, consider supine or left lateral decubitus for suspected venous air embolism (traditional); urgent hyperbaric consult; treat tension physiology as per trauma.
- Electrical/lightning
  - ABCs, monitor ECG (risk of arrhythmia); check CK, renal function, urine output target >1 mL/kg/hr initially for rhabdomyolysis; manage burns; consider fasciotomy for compartment syndrome.
  - Lightning-specific: expect possible transient paralysis (keraunoparalysis) — monitor and supportive care.
- CO and smoke inhalation
  - 100% high‑flow O2 immediately.
  - CO: measure COHb by co‑oximetry. Consider hyperbaric O2 for severe cases (LOC, neuro deficits, cardiac ischemia, severe acidosis, pregnancy) per local protocol [thresholds vary — verify locally].
  - Cyanide: if suspected (smoke inhalation + high lactate, rapid deterioration), give hydroxocobalamin 5 g IV (Cyanokit) empirically while arranging further care [Cyanokit].
- HAZMAT/chemical/radiation
  - Establish zones; remove clothing; dry decontamination for non‑caustic powders; wet decontamination for caustics/unknown/CBRN in warm zone; staff PPE.
  - Radiation: decontaminate, baseline FBC, serial lymphocytes; KI only for confirmed/likely radioactive iodine exposure per public-health guidance.
- Altitude illnesses
  - AMS: descent, rest, acetazolamide (commonly 125 mg PO twice daily for prophylaxis/tx) and oxygen as needed [CDC Yellow Book 2024].
  - HACE: immediate descent, dexamethasone 8 mg then 4 mg q6h, oxygen, urgent evacuation.
  - HAPE: descent, oxygen, nifedipine SR (e.g., 30 mg q12h or 20 mg q8h) and consider PDE5 inhibitors/salmeterol in selected cases per guidance [CDC Yellow Book 2024]. Verify specific dosing locally.

Arabic summary (مختصر للخوارزميات):
- خطوات عامة: سلامة المكان → إزالة الملابس → ABCs → أكسجين 100% → إعلام اختصاصي.  
- ضربة الحرارة المجهدة: غمر بارد (غمر بالماء المثلّج) أسرع علاج؛ سيانيد مشتبه = هايدروكسيوكوبالامين 5 جم وريدياً؛ غوص مع DCS/AGE = أكسجين 100% ثم إعادة ضغط عاجلة.

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
- Key drugs/doses (established/commonly cited):
  - Oxygen 100% for CO, DCS/AGE, smoke inhalation, heat stroke as needed.
  - Hydroxocobalamin 5 g IV for suspected cyanide poisoning (Cyanokit).
  - Acetazolamide for AMS prophylaxis/treatment: commonly 125–250 mg PO twice daily (prophylaxis usually started 24–48 h prior) [CDC Yellow Book 2024]. (Pediatric dosing noted in some sources — verify locally.)
  - Dexamethasone for HACE: 8 mg IV/IM/PO once, then 4 mg q6h [CDC Yellow Book 2024].
  - Nifedipine SR for HAPE prevention/treatment: 30 mg q12h or 20 mg q8h (examples in CDC Yellow Book 2024) — verify local practice.
  - Sildenafil/tadalafil reported as adjuncts for HAPE prevention (sildenafil 50 mg q8h / tadalafil 10 mg bid reported in literature) — evidence variable; verify locally.
- Procedures:
  - Rapid cold‑water immersion for exertional heat stroke.
  - Decontamination: remove clothing; dry decon first for powders; wet decon for caustics/unknown in warm zone.
  - Hyperbaric recompression for DCS/AGE and selected CO cases — urgent transfer to specialist centre.
  - Airway control and intubation for altered consciousness/compromised airway.
  - Chest decompression/chest tube for tension pneumothorax/persistent barotrauma as per trauma protocols.
  - Fasciotomy for compartment syndrome from electrical injury if indicated.
- Disposition:
  - Heat stroke: admit to critical care; monitor for organ failure.
  - DCS/AGE: urgent transfer to hyperbaric centre; consider ICU post‑recompression.
  - Electrical injuries: observe for 24 hours for arrhythmia if significant exposure/syncope; admit if CK rise/renal risk/burns.
  - CO: consider admission for symptomatic or high COHb, pregnancy, cardiac ischemia, neuro signs; local hyperbaric criteria apply.
  - HAZMAT: admit as per toxicity and exposure; decontaminated patients may still need monitoring.
  - Blast: consider 24‑hour observation even if initially asymptomatic.
- Follow-up:
  - Neuro/cognitive follow-up after CO or AGE/DCS due to delayed neurologic sequelae.
  - Renal follow-up if rhabdomyolysis or AKI.
  - Occupational/public‑health notification for significant HAZMAT/radiation incidents.

Arabic summary (مختصر الأدوية/الإجراءات/التنويل):
- أدوية رئيسية: هايدروكسيوكوبالامين 5 جم للسيانيد؛ أسيتازولاميد 125–250 مج مرتين يومياً للوقاية/علاج AMS؛ ديكساميثازون للـ HACE.  
- إجراءات رئيسية: غمر بارد لضربة الحرارة المجهدة، ديكونتامينيشن منظم، وإحالة عاجلة للغرف الضاغطة عند DCS/AGE.

6. High-yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Timing clues: narcosis/inner‑ear barotrauma occur at depth/descent; DCS/AGE present after ascent (AGE immediate; DCS may be delayed) — use timing to choose diagnosis.
- Pulse oximetry is unreliable in CO poisoning — exam answers that rely on normal SpO2 to exclude CO are incorrect.
- “Hot/dry” vs “sweaty” skin: classic teaching for classic vs exertional heat stroke is not reliable — use core temp + CNS signs.
- Decontamination: dry decon first for powders/non‑caustic solids; wet decon for caustics/unknown/CBRN — do NOT wet powder before removing it.
- Lightning specific: Lichtenberg figures pathognomonic; do not mistake for thermal burns.
- Rhabdomyolysis: urine output target >1 mL/kg/hr initially, monitor CK, treat with aggressive IV fluids to prevent AKI.
- Hyperbaric oxygen: many exam stems ask about indications — remember LOC, neuro deficits, cardiac ischemia, pregnancy are commonly cited triggers for urgent hyperbaric consultation but local thresholds vary.
- Altitude exam tips: acetazolamide is the “prophylaxis” answer; dexamethasone is the immediate drug for HACE; nifedipine is for HAPE.
- Radiation: early vomiting/nausea within hours is the best early indicator of significant whole‑body exposure — exam may test lymphocyte kinetics (fall in lymphocytes).
- Electrical exposures: AC (50–60 Hz) more likely to cause VF; DC more likely to produce asystole/tetany — exam may test rhythm associations.
- Don’t assume normal initial imaging excludes blast or DCS — observation and re‑evaluation are often required.

Arabic summary (نصائح للامتحان):
- استخدم توقيت الأعراض للتفريق بين narcosis وDCS؛ لا تعتمد على oximeter لرفض تسمم CO؛ أسيتازولاميد للوقاية من AMS، ديكساميثازون للـ HACE.

7. Arabic rapid-review summary (مراجعة سريعة بالعربية)
- مفاهيم أساسية: الطوارئ البيئية تشمل الحرارة، الغوص، الكهرباء/البرق، السموم (CO/سيانيد)، الإشعاع، والانفجارات. العلاج الأولي = سلامة الموقع → ABCs → أكسجين 100% عند الشك → إزالة الملابس/الديكونتامينيشن → تنبيه الأخصائيين.
- حرارة: ضربة الشمس = حرارة ≥40°C + خلل عصبي → غمر بالماء البارد (مجهدة) أو تبريد تبخيري (كلاسيكية)، مراقبة للـ rhabdo وDIC.
- غوص: narcosis يحدث في الأعماق ويزول بالصعود؛ DCS بعد الصعود يعالج بالأكسجين 100% وإعادة ضغط عاجلة.
- كهرباء/برق: فحص لـ ECG، CK، رصد البول لصالح تجنّب الفشل الكلوى؛ كيرونوباراليسيس قد يكون عابراً.
- CO/دخان: أعطِ أكسجين 100% فوراً؛ استخدم co‑oximetry لقياس COHb؛ هايدروكسيوكوبالامين 5 جم وريدياً عند اشتباه بسيانيد.
- HAZMAT: جفف أولاً للمواد الغير كاوية؛ اغسل بالماء للمواد الكاوية/غير المعروفة؛ افصل المناطق (hot/warm/cold).
