- Wide complex tachycardia:
  - Unstable → immediate synchronised DC cardioversion.
  - Stable monomorphic VT → IV amiodarone (commonly 300 mg over 10–60 min) or procainamide per local practice; torsades → IV magnesium sulfate 2 g.
- VF/pulseless VT: defibrillate per ALS, high‑quality CPR, adrenaline and antiarrhythmic per ACLS.
- STEMI reperfusion triage:
  - If anticipated primary PCI delay >120 min → give fibrinolysis (if no contraindication) then transfer (pharmacoinvasive strategy). If PCI available within timeframe → primary PCI. Give aspirin 300 mg and follow local antiplatelet/anticoagulant protocols.
  - Avoid nitrates if RV infarct suspected (V4R STE) — give cautious IV fluid bolus for preload support.
- Cardiac tamponade: urgent echo; if unstable → pericardiocentesis; use IV fluids as temporary preload support only.
- Cardiogenic pulmonary oedema: oxygen, early CPAP, diuretics, nitrates if not hypotensive.

Arabic concise:
خوارزميات طارئة مختصرة: بطء عرضى → أتروبين 0.5 mg متكرر حتى 3 mg → تحفيز خارجي إذا فشل؛ SVT مستقر → مناورة فالزالفا المعدلة ثم أدينوزين 6→12 mg؛ WPW+AF لا تعطى محصرات العقدة الأذينية البطينية — بروكاييناميد أو صدمة إذا غير مستقر؛ STEMI: قرر PCI مقابل إذابة جلطة بناءً على زمن الوصول (120 دقيقة).

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow‑up
- Key drug doses (well established):
  - Atropine: 0.5 mg IV bolus, repeat q3–5 min to max 3 mg. [Resus Council UK 2021]
  - Adenosine: 6 mg rapid IV bolus → 12 mg if needed (some protocols use up to 18 mg).
  - Amiodarone (acute arrhythmia): 300 mg IV bolus over 10–60 min (ED regimens vary).
  - Magnesium sulfate: 2 g IV for torsades.
  - Procainamide: used for stable pre‑excited AF or some stable VT (local availability determines use).
- Procedures and practical points:
  - Transcutaneous pacing: pads AP or as per device, increase current until capture, confirm central pulse; if fail reposition pads then escalate to transvenous pacing.
  - Synchronized DC cardioversion: use biphasic device settings per manufacturer; typical exam/ED energies: 100–150 J for SVT/VT with pulse, 200 J common for AF (verify local guidance).
  - Pericardiocentesis: emergent for tamponade with instability; perform under echo guidance if possible.
  - Device management: urgent ICD interrogation for recurrent shocks; magnet may suspend tachytherapy but device behaviour varies — consult local policy/device team.
  - LVAD: measure MAP by Doppler; contact VAD team early.
- Disposition and follow‑up:
  - Unstable arrhythmias, new high‑grade AV block, persistent symptomatic bradycardia requiring pacing → admit/CCU and urgent cardiology ± pacing/EP referral.
  - STEMI → PPCI centre or fibrinolysis pathway and cardiology admission.
  - WPW with documented pre‑excited AF or syncope → cardiology/EP follow‑up and consider ablation.
  - Pericarditis with high‑risk features (fever, large effusion, tamponade, failure of NSAIDs, raised troponin, immunosuppression) → admit and early cardiology.
  - Infective endocarditis suspicion → take 3 blood cultures before starting antibiotics when feasible; admit and involve ID/cardiology/CT surgery as needed.

Arabic concise:
الأدوية والإجراءات: أتروبين 0.5 mg حتى 3 mg، أدينوزين 6→12 mg؛ أميودارون 300 mg IV loading شائع؛ ماغنسيوم 2 g لتورسادس. التحفيز الخارجي ثم الوريدى عند فشل، صدمة مزامنة عند عدم الاستقرار. القبول: CCU/PCI/EP حسب الحالة.

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Always check mechanical capture after pacing — electrical capture on ECG is not sufficient (frequently tested).
- Modified Valsalva (semi‑recumbent + leg raise) > standard Valsalva — choose modified in exam stems (REVERT 2015).
- Atropine typical exam dose: 0.5 mg bolus, max 3 mg — many pick 2 mg incorrectly.
- Never give AV‑nodal blockers (beta‑blocker, CCB, digoxin, adenosine) for irregular wide complex tachycardia when WPW suspected — exam trap.
- Treat wide complex regular tachycardia as VT if in doubt — safest answer on exams.
- Posterior MI: tall R / ST depression in V1–V3 → add V7–V9; don’t miss (common fault).
- De Winter pattern = LAD occlusion equivalent — treat as STEMI (exam/MCQ trap labelled “not STEMI”).
- Nitrates can precipitate severe hypotension in RV infarct — check V4R in inferior STEMI before nitrates.
- ICD magnet use: device behaviour varies; don’t assume permanent suspension of therapy — mention device/centre policy in answers.
- Theophylline/amino­phylline and other historical therapies for bradycardia are not standard: flag as outdated — verify local guidance.
- Adenosine in asthma: caution in severe/reactive airways disease; not absolute contraindication in mild disease (exam nuance).
- Wellens syndrome = pre‑angiography high risk—do not perform exercise stress test.
- BP/pulse in LVAD: absent palpable pulse ≠ no perfusion; use Doppler MAP measurement.

Arabic concise:
خدع الامتحان: تحقق من الالتقاط الميكانيكى بعد التحفيز، اختَر مناورة فالزالفا المعدلة، أتروبين 0.5 mg حتى 3 mg ليس 2 mg، لا تستخدم محصرات العقدة في WPW+AF، تعامل مع QRS واسع كـVT إذا شككت.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary (مختصر سريع بالعربية)
- عدم الاستقرار الإيقاعي → صدمة كهربائية محدثة (مزامنة/عدم مزامنة حسب الحالة).  
- بطء عرضي حاد: أتروبين 0.5 mg متكرر حتى 3 mg → إذا فشل: تحفيز خارجي عابر ثم تحضير لتحفيز وريدى أو دائم.  
- SVT مستقرة: مناورة فالزالفا المعدلة ثم أديينوزين 6→12 mg؛ SVT غير مستقر → صدمة مزامنة.  
- WPW + الرجفان الأذيني: لا تعطِ محصرات عقدة AV؛ مستقِر → بروكاييناميد؛ غير مستقر → صدمة.  
- QRS واسع منتظم → افترض VT؛ غير مستقر → صدمة مُزامنة؛ torsades → ماغنسيوم 2 g IV.  
- STEMI: إذا انتظار PCI >120 دقيقة وفى مريض ملائم → إذابة جلطة ثم تحويل؛ RV infarct (V4R) → تجنب النتروجليسرين.  
- التامبوناد: ثلاثية بيك (ضغط منخفض، JVP مرتفعة، أصوات قلب مكتومة) → صدى عاجل وبزل إن كان غير مستقر.  
- أجهزة مثل ICD/LVAD: استدعِ فريق الجهاز، لا تفترض غياب ضغط نابض عند LVAD; المغناطيس قد يوقف الصدمات لكن السلوك يختلف حسب الجهاز.  
- تحقّق دائمًا من الالتقاط الكهربى والميكانيكى بعد التحفيز، وحافظ على معالجة الأسباب القابلة للانعكاس (شوارد، أدوية، نقص أكسجة).

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- Arrhythmia urgency hierarchy; defibrillation/cardioversion vs pharmacologic therapy. [Resus Council UK 2021]
- Symptomatic bradycardia management and atropine dosing; transcutaneous/transvenous pacing, capture confirmation.
- Pacing troubleshooting and pad positioning; causes preventing capture (hyperkalaemia).
- SVT management: modified Valsalva (REVERT 2015), adenosine dosing, cardioversion.
- Wide complex tachycardia management: VT vs SVT with aberrancy; amiodarone/procainamide; torsades and magnesium.
- Pre‑excited AF (WPW): contraindicated AV‑nodal blockers, use procainamide or cardioversion.
- STEMI triage: PCI timing (120 min), fibrinolysis indication, posterior/V4R leads, De Winter/Wellens patterns.
- ECG interpretation pearls: QTc calculation, posterior leads V7–V9, De Winter T‑waves, Wellens, device ECG signs.
- Pericarditis vs MI vs tamponade: ECG findings, indications for pericardiocentesis.
- Device/LVAD management: ICD shocks, magnet use caveats, MAP by Doppler in LVADs.
- Cardiogenic pulmonary oedema: CPAP early, nitrates caution in RV infarct.
- Infective endocarditis sampling (3 blood cultures) and initial management.
- High‑risk conduction disease (Mobitz II, bifascicular/trifascicular) and pacing indications.
- Exam traps and pitfalls: mechanical vs electrical capture, modified Valsalva superiority, atropine max dose, avoid AV‑nodal blockers in WPW+AF, treat wide complex as VT if unsure.

Note: no specific question numbers were provided in the supplied material — map lists the concepts/topics represented.

Sources to verify
- Resuscitation Council UK (Adult Tachycardia and Bradycardia Algorithms) 2021; REVERT trial (2015) for modified Valsalva; ESC and AHA/ALS guidelines for cardioversion/arrhythmia therapy; NICE guidance where applicable (e.g., syncope / HOCM referral); RCEM/UK national guidance for ED practice; local hospital/device manufacturer policies for ICD/magnet behaviour; LitFL ECG resources for ECG pattern recognition.

(Study notes — educational only, not patient‑specific advice. Verify local protocols and device behaviours before clinical application.)


============================================================================================
CHAPTER 32: ALLERGY
============================================================================================

Chapter 32: Allergy

1. Clinical overview and pathophysiology
- Definition: Anaphylaxis = acute, potentially life‑threatening systemic allergic reaction, usually IgE‑mediated. Allergen cross‑links IgE on mast cells → massive mediator release (histamine, tryptase, leukotrienes, prostaglandins) → vasodilation, increased vascular permeability, bronchospasm, mucosal oedema.
- Non‑IgE syndromes that mimic allergy:
  - Bradykinin‑mediated angioedema (ACE‑inhibitor) — no urticaria, may be delayed and recur.
  - Scombroid poisoning — histamine‑like reaction from spoiled fish.
  - DRESS (drug reaction with eosinophilia and systemic symptoms) — delayed (2–8 weeks), systemic organ involvement.
  - Immune‑checkpoint inhibitor toxicity — delayed, organ‑specific immune reactions (eg colitis).
- Key principle: diagnosis is clinical; time‑critical therapy (adrenaline IM) must not be delayed for tests. [Resus Council UK 2021]

موجز بالعربية:
- التأق حالة مناعية حادة تهدِّد الحياة؛ الآلية غالبًا IgE وإفراز وسائط حادة.  
- توجد حالات شبيهة (وذمة ACE‑I، تسمم السمك، DRESS، سمية مثبِّطات المناعة) لكن آلياتها مختلفة.  
- القرار سريري والعلاج الفوري لا ينتظر الفحوصات. [Resus Council UK 2021]

2. Recognition, differential diagnosis, and red flags
- Typical presentation: sudden onset after exposure + ≥1:
  - Airway: stridor, throat/tongue/facial swelling, hoarseness, progressive dysphagia.
  - Breathing: wheeze, severe dyspnoea, hypoxia, poor air entry.
  - Circulation: hypotension, syncope, collapse, tachycardia, poor peripheral perfusion.
  - Skin: widespread urticaria, angioedema, flushing (may be absent in up to 10–20%).
- Rapid differentials (distinguish clinically):
  - Vasovagal syncope (bradycardia, pallor, precipitant, self‑limited).
  - Acute severe asthma (history of asthma, no urticaria/angioedema).
  - Panic attack (hyperventilation, preserved oxygenation).
  - Myocardial ischaemia (age/risk factors, ischaemic ECG changes).
  - ACEI angioedema (no urticaria, ongoing ACEI exposure).
  - Scombroid (recent fish ingestion).
  - DRESS / delayed drug reactions (rash + fever + organ dysfunction days–weeks later).
- Red flags (need immediate escalation):
  - Progressive airway compromise (stridor, increasing dysphagia, tongue/facial swelling).
  - Refractory hypotension despite initial therapy.
  - Rapid deterioration or collapse.
  - Hypoxaemia or severe bronchospasm not responding to bronchodilators.
  - Pregnant patient with haemodynamic compromise — early senior / obstetric input.

موجز بالعربية:
- الأعراض المفاجئة بعد مهيِّج مع علامات جوهرية: مجرى هواء، تنفُّس، دوران أو جلد.  
- التفريق مهم: وذمة ACE‑I (بدون شرى)، تسمم السمك، ذبحة صدرية، ذعر.  
- علامات الخطر: ازدياد انسداد المجاري الهوائية، هبوط ضغط مستمر أو نقص أكسجة. 

3. Diagnostic approach and interpretation
- Main rule: treat clinically; do not delay adrenaline for investigations.
- Tests that support diagnosis:
  - Serum tryptase: peaks ~1–2 hours after onset; take sample as soon as possible and another baseline ≥24 hours later. A normal tryptase does NOT exclude anaphylaxis. [Resus Council UK 2021]
  - Bloods/ECG/ABG/peak flow as clinically indicated (to assess organ dysfunction, hypoxia, acidosis).
  - Pregnancy test if relevant.
  - For delayed reactions: CBC (eosinophilia in DRESS), LFTs/renal panel, and organ‑specific tests.
- Documentation: record suspected trigger, timing of exposure, symptom onset, treatments given and response.
- When to refer for further testing: arrange allergy clinic for skin tests or specific IgE testing after recovery (timing depends on allergen and severity).

موجز بالعربية:
- التشخيص سريري؛ فحوصات داعمة: التربتيز (ذروة 1–2 ساعة، أخذ أساسى ≥24 ساعة لاحقًا)؛ دم، ECG حسب الحاجة.  
- العَملِيَّة: وثِّق المهيّج، توقيت الأعراض واستجابة العلاج، وأحِل إلى حساسية لاحقًا. [Resus Council UK 2021]

4. Emergency management algorithms
A. Immediate resuscitation (algorithmic steps)
1) Immediate actions: ensure scene safety, remove trigger (stop infusion/stop feed), call for senior/anaesthetic/critical care help early.
2) Positioning: lie flat with legs elevated unless respiratory distress or vomiting (prevents sudden collapse). [Resus Council UK 2021]
3) Oxygen: high‑flow oxygen (if hypoxic or respiratory distress).
4) Adrenaline IM: give immediately into mid‑anterior lateral thigh (1:1000).
   - Adults and children >12 years: 500 µg (0.5 mL)
   - Children 6–12 years: 300 µg (0.3 mL)
   - Children 6 months–6 years: 150 µg (0.15 mL)
   - Infants <6 months: ~100–150 µg (0.1–0.15 mL)
   - Repeat every 5 minutes if persistent features.
   [Resus Council UK 2021]
5) IV access and fluids: large bore IV; adults 500–1000 mL crystalloid bolus and repeat as required; children 20 mL/kg boluses.
6) If severe bronchospasm: nebulised salbutamol ± nebulised adrenaline as adjuncts (they do not replace IM adrenaline).
7) If persistent hypotension or refractory anaphylaxis despite repeated IM doses → consider IV adrenaline infusion in monitored setting with expert help; IV boluses have high arrhythmia risk. Example infusion regimen (expert use only): 1 mg in 100 mL 0.9% saline (10 µg/mL) and titrate to effect on monitored patient. Use local critical care protocols. [Resus Council UK 2021]
8) Airway management: prepare for early intubation if airway swelling progresses; have equipment for difficult airway and anaesthetic support. Surgical airway if complete obstruction and cannot intubate/ventilate.
9) Adjunct drugs:
   - H1 antihistamine (IV/PO) for cutaneous symptoms (not life‑saving).
   - H2 antagonist optional for additional cutaneous control.
   - Steroids (eg IV hydrocortisone) commonly given to potentially reduce protracted/biphasic reactions but do NOT treat immediate airway or circulatory compromise. Evidence for preventing biphasic reactions is limited — practice varies.
10) Observation and reassessment: monitor vitals, urine output, respiratory status; repeat adrenaline and fluids as necessary.

B. Refractory anaphylaxis escalation
- If ongoing shock despite repeated IM adrenaline and fluids:
  - Call critical care/anaesthetics.
  - Start IV adrenaline infusion (expert) and titrate; use continuous cardiac monitoring.
  - Consider vasopressors/inotropes per critical care guidance.
  - Consider bronchoscopy for airway oedema assessment if available.
  - Consider adjuncts for specific causes (see section 5).

موجز بالعربية:
- خوارزمية سريعة: أوقف المهيّج → استدعِ المساعدة → مريض مستلقٍ مع رفع الأرجل → أكسجين → أدرينالين عضلي فورًا (كرر كل 5 دقائق) → سوائل ووسائل داعمة → تحويل لوحدة عناية إذا مقاوم.  
- الأدرينالين الوريدي والجرعات السريعة للحالات المقاومة فقط وبحضور خبرة ومراقبة. [Resus Council UK 2021]

5. Drugs, procedures, disposition, and follow-up
Drugs and doses (exam‑important)
- Adrenaline IM (1:1000): adult = 500 µg IM; paeds per age bands above. Repeat every 5 minutes as needed. [Resus Council UK 2021]
- IV adrenaline infusion: expert setting only — example: 1 mg in 100 mL 0.9% saline (10 µg/mL), titrate carefully under monitoring. Avoid unsupervised IV boluses except by experienced clinicians.
- IV fluids: adults 500–1000 mL crystalloids bolus; children 20 mL/kg boluses.
- Nebulised salbutamol for bronchospasm; nebulised adrenaline for upper airway obstruction adjunctively.
- Antihistamines: H1 (eg cetirizine PO/IV) for skin symptoms; H2 optional.
- Steroids: IV hydrocortisone commonly used; not immediate rescue therapy.
- ACEI angioedema specific treatments: stop ACEI. Icatibant or C1 esterase concentrate have been used in severe cases—availability and evidence vary; follow local protocol.

Procedures
- Early airway anticipation and preparation for difficult intubation if swelling or progressive obstruction.
- Monitor on continuous ECG, SpO2, BP; consider arterial line/advanced monitoring in ICU.
- Take blood for tryptase as soon as possible and repeat baseline ≥24 hours later.

Disposition and observation
- Disposition depends on severity and response:
  - Discharge criteria (common RCUK approach): complete resolution of symptoms, observed response within 5–10 minutes after single adrenaline dose, reliable follow‑up and access to emergency care, patient given adrenaline auto‑injector and training — may consider discharge after ~2 hours (local practice varies).  
  - Observe ≥6 hours if ≥2 adrenaline doses were required.  
  - Observe ≥12 hours (or admit) if >2 doses required, severe respiratory compromise, protracted absorption risk (eg large intradermal exposure), poor access to emergency care or uncertain cause. [Resus Council UK 2021]
- On discharge: provide adrenaline auto‑injector(s) (at least one; two often prescribed), educate on indications and use, give written action plan, counsel on avoidance of trigger, and arrange urgent specialist allergy follow‑up (weeks).
- For ACEI angioedema: stop ACE inhibitor, document, advise permanent cessation, arrange follow‑up; consider specialist therapies only per local guideline.
- For DRESS or immune‑checkpoint toxicities: stop offending drug and arrange urgent specialist (dermatology/haematology/oncology) input; systemic steroids often required.

Practical pitfalls and verification flags
- Antihistamines/steroids do not replace adrenaline (lifesaving drug).
- IV bolus adrenaline outside monitored/expert setting is risky — wrong answer in many exam vignettes.
- Icatibant/C1 esterase concentrate for ACEI angioedema: variable evidence and availability—check local policy before use.
- Observation timeframes and discharge criteria vary by jurisdiction—confirm local guidance.

موجز بالعربية:
- أدوية رئيسية: أدرينالين عضلي فورًا (بالجرعات المذكورة)، سوائل، موسعات قصبية نيبولية، مضادات الهيستامين وستيرويد داعم.  
- الأدرينالين الوريدي ومداخل الشرايين والإنعاش المتقدِّم للحالات المقاومة فقط.  
- المراقبة والمدة تعتمد على شدة الاستجابة: ملاحظة قصيرة إذا استجابة فورية بعد جرعة واحدة؛ ≥6 أو ≥12 ساعة في حالات أكثر خطورة.  
- عند الخروج: تعطى حقيبة أدرينالين تلقائي وتعليم ومتابعة حساسية. 

6. High‑yield FRCEM exam tricks and common pitfalls
- Adrenaline is first‑line and time‑critical; never choose antihistamine/steroid alone for life‑threatening airway/circulatory compromise.
- Always lie the patient flat (unless respiratory distress/vomiting) — tested commonly.
- Know the IM adrenaline adult dose = 500 µg (0.5 mL of 1:1000) and paediatric age bands: 6–12 yrs = 300 µg; 6 months–6 yrs = 150 µg. Many questions test an 11‑year‑old = 300 µg. [Resus Council UK 2021]
- IV adrenaline is for refractory anaphylaxis under monitoring/expert help — selection of IV bolus in non‑monitored scenario is usually wrong.
- Tryptase timing: peak 1–2 hours; baseline ≥24 hours — normal tryptase does not exclude anaphylaxis.
- Absence of urticaria does NOT exclude anaphylaxis (20% may lack skin signs) — don’t be misled by lack of rash.
- ACEI angioedema clues: no urticaria, poor response to antihistamines/steroids, and ongoing ACEI use — consider bradykinin mechanism.
- Beware of distractors: scombroid poisoning (fish history) presents like histamine reaction but management focuses on antihistamines/supportive care; DRESS and immunotherapy toxicities are delayed presentations — timing is key.
- Biphasic reaction risk increases when adrenaline administration is delayed or when initial reaction severe; watch for recurrence during observation period.

نصائح للامتحان بالعربية:
- الأدرينالين أولاً دائمًا؛ لا تستبدله بمضادات الهيستامين/الستيرويد للحالات المهدِّدة.  
- احفظ جرعات الأدرينالين العضلي حسب الأعمار؛ تجنّب الأدرينالين الوريدي إلا بحضور خبرة.  
- التربتيز داعم لا حاسم؛ غياب الشرَى لا ينفي التأق.

7. Arabic rapid‑review summary
- التأق (anaphylaxis) = طارئ مناعي غالبًا IgE → إفراز وسيطات → هبوط ضغط، صفير، وذمة؛ العلاج الفوري أدرينالين عضلي في الفخذ.  
- تمييز مهم: وذمة ACE‑I (براديكينين) و DRESS وسمك سكامبرويد وسمية مثبِّطات المناعة — التاريخ والوقت بعد المهيِّج يوجهان التشخيص.  
- خطوات إنعاش سريعة: أوقف المهيِّج، مريض مستلقي، أكسجين، أدرينالين IM فورًا وكرر كل 5 دقائق، سوائل، استعد للمجرى الهوائي، تحويل للعناية المركزة إذا مقاوم.  
- اختبارات داعمة: تربتيز (ذروة 1–2 ساعة؛ مكرر أساسى ≥24 ساعة).  
- عند الخروج: حقيبة أدرينالين وتعليم ومتابعة حساسية.  
- المراجع المحلية (Resuscitation Council UK 2021 وغيرها) للتفاصيل والبروتوكولات المحلية.

ملخّص بالعربية مختصر ومباشر:
- أدرينالين عضلي الآن → سوائل ودعم → مراقبة → تعليم ومتابعة.

8. Question coverage map (concepts covered)
- No source Q‑numbers were supplied. Concepts represented in supplied material (use this map to match likely FRCEM questions):
  - Pathophysiology of anaphylaxis (IgE mast cell mediator release).
  - Recognition criteria and clinical features (airway, breathing, circulation, skin).
  - IM adrenaline dosing (adult and paediatric age bands) and timing (repeat every 5 minutes).
  - Positioning of patient (lie flat with legs raised) and scene safety.
  - Differential diagnoses: vasovagal, asthma, panic, MI, scombroid, ACEI angioedema, DRESS, checkpoint inhibitor toxicity.
  - Diagnostics: clinical diagnosis, tryptase timing/interpretation, supportive blood tests.
  - Emergency algorithm and refractory anaphylaxis management (IV adrenaline infusion as expert step).
  - Adjunctive therapies: antihistamines, steroids (role and limitations), nebulised bronchodilators.
  - Specific syndromes: ACEI angioedema management considerations, scombroid, DRESS, immune‑checkpoint toxicities.
  - Observation, disposition and follow‑up: auto‑injector, allergy referral, observation durations per response.
  - Exam‑style pitfalls: substituting antihistamines for adrenaline, misinterpretation of tryptase, misuse of IV adrenaline.

Sources to verify
- Resuscitation Council (UK) — Anaphylaxis and Refractory Anaphylaxis algorithms, 2021.  
- Local and national guidance: RCEM, ERC, NICE, local paediatric resuscitation and allergy services protocols.
